(3) **`Secret` transport across the WASM host boundary is a gate item for the owner's security review** (gate 10's checklist in `docs/reviews/m3-security-review.md`, under its existing `Secret<T>` row): the designer records the reading (tag 14; the inner value only to a kind-`secret` import, `js_handler` today, as ABI §8.1 lists; redacted to `14, Unit` otherwise, so cassette lookups see that a header is secret and never its value; `runtime/rt/tests/vt.rs` pins both directions) and its risk (a kind-`secret` import is the one place a secret leaves the guest in clear, so each such import is a reviewed addition), and does **not** sign it off. Until the owner's signature: no new kind-`secret` import, no widening of what `js_handler` receives, and the host zeroes the slice after the call as ABI §8.1 says. (4) **§17.44 is read per backend**, as §17.80 reads `D003`/`D001`: on WASM `String.parse_int` and `Float.to_int_checked` answer `Some` for every i64, the differential keeps skipping such programs on WASM, and a fixture that depends on the range carries `@js`/`@wasm` expectations. The remaining W1/R4 readings (plan §7 "W1 and R4" items 1–16: the linker lowering libcore's sign extension, saturating truncation and `call_indirect` encoding to the §1 profile; the L3/L12 calling convention for compiled code; catch entries carrying their exits; the static block; the `start`/`outcome`/`tests`/`stats` payloads; the extra `sakura_host` imports; tagged `sakura-vt1` with host-side codecs; fibers and virtual time; the JS-built handler protocol with `Llm.stream` as `D090`) are **endorsed as built**; the R3 hand-built tests' older convention is retired when W2 next touches them. Performance: one task allocation fewer per decoded refined value on JS. Changed: §17.80 (pointer), §12.3 (`V8004`). Impact: `runtime/js/sakura_runtime.js` (`satisfies`; Opus, with W2, small), `sakura_cli/tests/parity.rs`, one `tests/semantics` fixture, `sakura_diagnostics` (one sentence), plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.1; the owner (gate item).

156. **WS-P2 close-out: the cancellation acceptance reworded, the resumable loop as WS-P2b, the `handled_suspend` peak judged as young-generation sizing under a stated protocol, §17.138 narrowed to call consumers (WS-P2 `0d30acf`/`67155a0`/`ce9ebe1`, deviations 5, 8, 9, 10, 13; round 11).** Decision: (1) **Plan §5 item 3 is reworded**: "a cancelled task never runs a bound perform's clause, and every bound perform remains a cancellation point: the dispatch tests the current task's cancellation flag before the clause (pinned by the runtime unit test), and `handler_cancel_bound.sak` shows cancellation, finalizer and trace records identical to the unbound form." The old wording assumed a cancellation can arrive while a task runs; in the JS runtime a task is cancelled only while suspended (`cancelTask` reaches a suspended task, which unwinds at its resume), so the first operation that *observes* a cancellation is the resumption, by construction, in every program. The guarantee the design needs (§8.6: every effect operation is a cancellation point; §17.113: bound dispatch does not lose it) is the test before the clause, which the fixture and the unit test pin. Why: an acceptance no correct implementation can meet measures nothing. (2) **WS-P2b, the resumable loop.** A loop (join point) in a CPS body whose only CPS steps are unbound performs or `cps` builtins is emitted as a `for (;;)` with a resumable entry: on the fast path (handler found, clause direct) the iteration continues inside the loop; on the slow path the loop's live state is captured once in a continuation that re-enters the loop at its head, so an iteration pays no join call and no closure unless it actually suspends or forwards to a CPS clause. Rules: per join point, deterministic, only when every other bind of the loop body is direct-emittable (§17.111's `Facts`), with blame spans, `Check` binds, the trace and the gauge accounting unchanged (an iteration that re-enters through the continuation counts one frame, otherwise none). Acceptance: `handled_fwd` and `handled_suspend` emit the loop form (an emitted-JS snapshot); `handler_cancel_in_loop`, `handler_cancel_bound`, `million_operations`, the nested-`with` case and every `tests/semantics` fixture unchanged under `--no-direct`, trace parity and on WASM; a new fixture suspends twice in one loop and resumes with the right state; advisory deltas on `handled_fwd` (9.02×/14.33× today) and `handled_suspend` (5.18×/4.07×), judged at the next quiet window against the `handled` band (2.0 at depth 1, 3.0 at depth 16; the forwarding and suspend cases are reported beside it, they have no ceiling of their own in §17.109); the `handled_suspend`/Node RSS cell remeasured and the per-iteration closures confirmed or ruled out as the allocation-rate cause. Owner Opus, `emit.rs` (reading `direct.rs`'s `Facts` only), **after WS-P5 lands** (WS-P5 edits `emit.rs` for blame); plan `performance-r1.md` §9. Rejected: polling `T.cancelled` per iteration without a perform (the perform is the cancellation point); doing it in Core (the loop form is an emission choice; Core's join points are the LIR's input and the LIR does its own under §17.118). (3) **`handled_suspend`/Node peak RSS (+37%) is judged as young-generation sizing, not a retained-memory regression, under a stated protocol.** §17.136 keeps the maximum gate and the confirmation group, and a confirmed Node crossing stays a block *unless* the implementer shows all three of: (a) the head's post-GC heap equals the base's (here 5.0 MB both, 60 runs); (b) `--trace-gc` attributes the whole rise to new-space growth (a semi-space doubling) with no additional mark-compact and no old-generation growth; (c) under a capped semi space (`--max-semi-space-size`, diagnostic only, never in the harness or a baseline) base and head peak equal (69–70 MB both). WS-P3's `binary_trees` would have failed (b) and (c): its dead temporaries were pretenured into the old generation, which is exactly what the protocol separates from nursery sizing. When the three hold, the cell is a **warning carrying the diagnostic**, recorded in `bench/deltas.md` with the three readings and the commits, and the baseline takes the new number at the next owner bless (the quiet window) like every other change; the harness is unchanged (no exemption list, no flag): the ruling is the designer's per cell, as a definition boundary is (§17.150). WS-P2's RSS block is therefore closed as a warning; WS-P3's `binary_trees` block closes when Astra confirms WS-P2's 157–168 MB at the quiet window (§17.138). Why: an allocation-rate change that makes V8 grow its nursery is the engine's heuristic answering a faster program, which §17.136 already recognised on Bun; the protocol keeps the gate's power on the case it caught. Rejected: a median gate; making Node advisory like Bun (the stable engine stays the gate); re-blessing before the quiet window; ignoring the block. (4) **§17.138 is amended**: the consumer must be a **call** (`Call`, or `Prim` of a function), never an allocation: with `new Tree(1, [r, bottom_up$d(…)])` V8 allocates the literal before evaluating the nested call, so the producer's result survives the whole subtree, and `binary_trees`/Node n=14 measured 257–290 MB that way against 157–168 MB with calls only (deviation 9). A producer that sets the blame site keeps its `$S.s` line in front of the consumer, and a consumer that sets the site is not inlined into. (5) The named maintainer review of WS-P2 (plan §5 item 6) is still owed and is listed for the owner with the gate item of §17.155 (3). Performance: as measured by WS-P2 (d1/d4/d16 at 0.84–0.86× Node, 0.95–1.46× Bun); `handled_fwd` and `handled_suspend` to WS-P2b. Changed: §17.113 (pointer), §17.138 (amended), plan `performance-r1.md` §5 item 3, §8 (pointers), §9 (WS-P2b). Impact: Opus (WS-P2b), Astra (the `deltas.md` row with the protocol's three readings; the quiet-window confirmation of `binary_trees`), owner (review).

157. **DX6 recorded: hand-written release tooling (ADR 0003); the owner's GNU matrix A→B→C supersedes §17.147's musl proposal; crates.io is deferred for the alpha (research/13 `d47ff83`, DX6 `fb4c4e9`..`474a8b1`, REQUESTS 2026-10-18 DX6 research and DX6; round 11).** Decision: (1) **ADR 0003** records the tooling decision: a hand-written `release.yml`, `tools/release/release.py` and `tools/install/install.sh` driven by one inventory, `packaging/targets.json`, over cargo-dist 0.33.0, for the reasons research/13 §1 established by source inspection (its shell installer succeeds without verification when `sha256sum` is absent and accepts a missing checksum; its PowerShell installer compares no checksum; its npm wrapper downloads at install time instead of carrying the binary; its stock publishing has no per-channel owner gate). Reconsidered when a cargo-dist release verifies unconditionally in both installers and its npm packaging carries binaries; status Proposed until the owner accepts it in review. (2) **§17.147 (1) is amended to the owner's matrix**: A `aarch64-apple-darwin` and `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`; B adds `aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu`; C adds `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` and `x86_64-apple-darwin`; each stage after the previous one has shipped and been accepted on a fresh host. GNU, not musl: the archives are not static, the installer and the npm launcher refuse musl with a message naming the reason (as built), and Alpine is unsupported at the alpha; a musl stage, if ever, is a later owner decision with its own qualification. (3) **crates.io is deferred for `v0.1.0-alpha.1`**: the channels are the GitHub release archives with the installer, the Homebrew tap and npm. Why: `cargo publish` of `sakura_cli` is impossible as the workspace is structured (unpublished path dependencies; `include_str!` and build scripts read std, the runtimes, `docs/llms.txt` and `skills/` from outside the crate root), the two ways to make it possible (a generated self-contained source package `sakuralang` whose generator copies the embedded inputs into the package root, or publishing every internal crate with registry versions) are each an architecture decision with STABILITY and licensing consequences, and no consumer needs it today: agents and people get the binary from the installer, npm or the tap, and a `cargo install --path` user builds from the repository with the pinned toolchain. A placeholder or name-reservation crate and a crate whose build downloads the compiler are forbidden (research/13 §2 item 3). When: revisited for the first beta, or earlier if a `cargo install sakuralang` request exists, through a new ADR whose default to research is the generated source package (reviewed `cargo package --list`, provenance inside the package, `cargo publish --dry-run --locked` as the acceptance). Meanwhile `release.yml` keeps no crates job (as built), `docs/release.md` marks the crates.io channel "deferred (§17.157)" and drops the `release-crates` environment from the one-time setup, and the "reduced first-release channels" acceptance research/13 asks of the owner is the owner's acknowledgement of this entry in REQUESTS. (4) Recorded for the owner, not decided here: Blacksmith is an owner installation and cost check before any Linux CI switch, with release builds and every publishing job staying on GitHub-hosted runners (npm trusted publishing requires them); signing stays SHA-256 over every asset plus GitHub artifact attestations on the approved publication, with unsigned/not-notarized status stated plainly, until the owner chooses a key. Rejected: publishing internal crates (STABILITY keeps them unpublished; it multiplies names and release ordering); restructuring the workspace now for a channel without a consumer; a musl build labelled static. Performance: none. Changed: §17.147 (amended), ADR 0003, plan `agent-dx.md` §3 (answer), `docs/release.md` (Sol, two sentences). Impact: Sol (`docs/release.md`, CHANGELOG line if the channel list is user-visible there), owner (ADR acceptance, environments, the dispatch dry run on GitHub, name ownership).

158. **Order after round 11 (round 11).** In flight: WS-P7 (ownership, §17.149), WS-P5 (contracts, §17.115), M4-W2 (WASM parity), DX7 (VS Code). Next, in this order and with these gates: WS-P8 (fusion, §17.151) now, it is small and lives in `opt.rs`, and its peephole runs before WS-P2's emission; WS-P2b (§17.156) after WS-P5 lands, because both edit `emit.rs`; L4 (§17.118) after WS-P5, `direct.rs` is settled since WS-P2 and WS-P2b changes emission, not facts; M4-TEST (b) after W2's first green local run; the quiet-window close-out when WS-P7, WS-P8 and WS-P5 have landed (WS-P4b, WS-S10 and WS-B10 are on master), **without waiting for WS-P2b**: the forwarding and suspend cases carry no ceiling of their own and are reported with their cause, and the `handled_suspend` RSS cell is a warning under §17.156 (3); M4-OPT after W2 and L4; the M4 envelope after M4-OPT; DX8 as `agent-dx.md` states. Nothing else moves. Changed: plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.7 item 12 and its dependency line, plan `performance-r1.md` §9. Impact: none beyond the routing.
159. **Gate 10 passed under the owner's delegation; `Secret` transport on WASM approved; `Secret.map` becomes audited; the test runner always passes its permissions (security review `docs/reviews/m3-security-review.md`, REQUESTS 2026-10-18 "gate 10"; round 12).** Question: gate 10 is the owner's signature on the M3 security packet (§17.128); on 2026-10-18 the owner delegated it in writing ("u do the security review i dont mind"), and §17.155 (3) had routed the WASM `Secret` transport to the same gate. Decision: (1) **Gate 10 is passed.** Parts A (escape rules), B (cassette format) and C (`Untrusted` in templates, with §17.130's `V2033`) are approved as the rules in force, reviewed against `6ccce80` and recorded as "reviewed and signed by Claude Fable 5.1 under the owner's written delegation of 2026-10-18", never as the owner's personal review; the owner can overturn any line. M3 is complete, the §17.128 freeze is lifted, and the review's findings, none in a frozen file, are plan WS12. The review was adversarial: four Medium findings outside the packet's parts and four on the WASM boundary are required work before a published release or before WASM parity is called complete. (2) **`Secret.map` takes a `Trust` reason.** A pure closure cannot perform, but it can defect with the clear value in its message (`Secret.map(token, fn(t) { Option.expect(None, t) })` prints the token in the `D030` record), so `map` was an unaudited egress; with a reason it is listed by `sakura audit` beside `expose`. §6.4's sentence "prevents accidental disclosure only" stays true and now has no exception for a deliberate program short of `@trust`. (3) **`sakura test` always sets `SAKURA_PERMISSIONS`**: without `--record`, `fs` grants empty, `net` and `env` empty, so a test that installs `Env.host()`, `Fs.host()` or `Http.host()` itself fails closed by the runtime's checks, not only by Node's permission model, which `node:sqlite` bypasses (a test wrote a database file into the package directory). `Test.fixtures()` keeps its own grants. (4) **WASM carries `Secret` headers**: `http_send` and `http_stream_open` become imports of kind `secret`. Under the stated trust model the host is the trusted base and owns linear memory, so the `14, Unit` redaction is hygiene against honest host paths (cassettes, logs, traces, `show`), the same guarantee the JS `Secret` class gives; making the two HTTP imports kind-`secret` is the egress the JS backend's `fetchArgs` already is, the cassette imports stay redacted, and `js_handler` already crosses the `Llm.anthropic` key the same way. The kind-`secret` list is `js_handler`, `http_send`, `http_stream_open`, and no import joins it without a line in the review record. ABI §8.1 is corrected (1.0.3 erratum): the host drops its references rather than "zeroes its copy" (a JS string cannot be zeroed), there is no LIR check (redaction is dynamic), and the guest zeroes its transport payload. (5) **`Sql.sqlite`'s lazy `Fs.root()` runs in the clause's task** on both backends (the W2 open question): JS's `sql_sqlite` performs it under the current task's identity as §17.155 (1) already requires of runtime-internal drives, and `tests/wasm.rs` compares that trace with task numbers. (6) The `serve` host gets an explicit request-body cap (`413`) and survives an invalid handler reply (`500`), a cassette exchange line is validated at `open`, the proof key's directory mode and owner are checked as the file's are, and the workflows outside `release.yml` pin action SHAs; the installer verifies the GitHub attestation when `gh` is present. Why: the delegation is a reason to review carefully, not a reason to wave anything through; the three frozen rules were found to be the right ones and enforced, and holding M3 open for findings in unfrozen files would freeze code the findings do not touch. Rejected: "changes requested" on the packet (no finding is in a reviewed rule, and WS9b would re-freeze code the fixes never touch); keeping the WASM refusal (it bought no security the trust model can state and broke `Http.bearer`/`Llm.anthropic` over `Http.host()` on WASM); redacting defect messages at run time instead of auditing `map` (a message is the program's text, and the audit site is the invariant §6.4 names); dropping `Secret.map` (std sinks and derived credentials need it). Performance: none at run time beyond one body-length check per served request. Changed: §6.4 (`Secret.map` signature), §10.2 (`Env`/`Fs`/`Http` host handlers under `sakura test`), §17.128 (closed), §17.155 (3) (decided), ABI §8.1/§8.4 erratum (M4-RT), plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §2 WS12, §3.7, §4; gate report gate 10. Impact: Opus (WS12 items 1–5, 7, 8), Sol (item 6), the owner (review-record T5, T8); `website/content/docs/roadmap.mdx` and `tour/memory.mdx` (M3 complete; WASM `Secret` wording after item 7 lands).
160. **L4 endorsed: twins are the mechanism, M4-OPT keeps the bar; the restart fallback stands with a counter; the byte gauge stands; ABI 1.1.0 is the ABI; `countdown` is WS-P9 (REQUESTS 2026-10-18 "L4"; plan §7 L4 items 2–5, 7; round 12).** Decision: (1) **Split.** "Frame reuse in direct regions" (§17.118) is read as L4 built it: no frames in a direct region, each region compiled a second time as a native twin, twins calling twins. L4 delivered the mechanism; M4-OPT owns the §17.118 bar (≥ 2× over `--no-direct` on `fib`, `countdown`, `nbody`) and decides, by measurement, whether lambdas, clauses, bound performs and closure calls of function-typed parameters need twins to reach it. `fib` already meets the bar (0.10 s against 0.40 s); `nbody` (0.18 s against 0.29 s) and `countdown` (0.12 s against 0.13 s) do not, and each has a named cause: `countdown` is (5) below; `nbody`'s frames fell from 52,038 to 7,005 while `boxes` did not move, so M4-OPT profiles it first, and if the remaining cost is the boxed generic words of `List<Float>` elements that ABI §4 keeps until M5 (§17.93), the `nbody` bar is **carried to M5 with §17.152's representation decision, stated in `bench/deltas.md`**, never quietly missed. (2) **The restart fallback is endorsed.** A twin that runs out of gauge returns `ABORTED` through every twin above it and the frame-protocol caller makes the same call through frames; direct code performs nothing, so the second run is unobservable, and a defect in the first attempt is the one the frame path would hit first (the same code, the same site). Bound: the work is at most doubled once per entry from frame code and only for regions `func::may_abort` cannot clear, and the arguments are held only then. To keep that bound measurable, M4-OPT adds a `restarts` counter to `stats()` (ABI 1.2.0, MINOR, appended after `drops`) and the benchmark record carries it; a program whose `restarts` is not 0 or 1 per entry is a bug, not a cost. ABI §5's "the caller rewrites the call" is corrected to what is built in the same bump. (3) **The gauge counts estimated native stack bytes** (`64 + 8 × variables` per twin, budget 128 KiB) rather than 400 frames: the two limits are each backend-local and neither is observable in a program (the fallback is frames, not a defect), so there is no parity question; the 512 KiB Wasmtime default stack is the stated assumption and `deep_recursion_gauge` is the pin. A host with a smaller stack is a host configuration, not a language case. (4) **ABI 1.1.0 is endorsed**: the four counters (`frames`, `boxes`, `dups`, `drops`), the direct-style paragraph of §5, `sakura_abi_version` `0x010100`. §10's `sakura run --stats` does not exist and is deleted in the 1.2.0 wording; `SAKURA_WASM_STATS=<path>` is the interface, and the JS lane reports no frames, boxes or counts. (5) **`countdown`'s mutual recursion is WS-P9**, no longer conditional: L4 measured the mutual half directly (the region aborts after the gauge and runs through frames; frames 1,006 against 1,007), which is the measurement §17.154 item 3 waited for. WS-P9 turns tail calls between members of a direct component into jumps into one merged loop with a dispatch parameter, in `sakura_core::opt::tail_calls` so both backends gain, mirrored in the LIR under §17.118's parity; it runs after WS-P2b lands because both edit `opt.rs`/`emit.rs`, and before M4-OPT measures the bar. Why: the twin design is the smallest thing that removes frames from the hot path without making the runtime re-entrant; a counter is cheaper than an argument about whether the double run matters. Rejected: a nested trampoline inside a running task (re-entrant `run`); rewriting the call at the gauge (a twin has no frame to rewrite into); folding WS-P9 into M4-OPT (it edits shared Core files other agents are in). Performance: none beyond the counter. Changed: plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.8, plan `performance-r1.md` §9 (WS-P9 decided). Impact: Opus (M4-OPT, WS-P9), ABI 1.2.0 (Opus, M4-OPT).
161. **The pull-based JS boundary and handle kinds 5–6 are endorsed; the `Sql.sqlite` task question was decided in §17.159 (5) (REQUESTS 2026-10-18 "W2"; plan §7 W2 items 8–9; round 12).** Decision: (1) **Nothing callable crosses the boundary.** A clause's closure arguments stay in the guest frame and cross as `Unit`; JS code that needs one answers `4` (session, argument index, unit flag, arguments) and the guest performs the call and resumes the JS continuation, as for an operation. A JS function passed to an operation crosses as a handle of kind `callback` (5); the guest wraps it in a native closure bound to the clause's handlers and calling it runs the function on the host through `js_callback`; the result is an opaque handle of kind `jsvalue` (6) that the guest only passes back, never decodes. Invariants that go with the endorsement: a kind-5 handle is retired when its clause's handler is destroyed (WS12 item 8), a kind-6 value is never inspected by the guest, and a failure a callback sends to its clause's context skips the clause's continuation (W2 pinned it in `llm_stream_anthropic`). ABI §8.2's import and handle-kind lists are updated to what is built in the 1.2.0 bump (§17.160 (4)); until then L4's and W2's additions beyond the list are the as-built state and `parity.rs` fails on an import the host lacks. (2) **`Sql.sqlite`'s lazy `Fs.root()`** runs in the clause's task on both backends; that is §17.159 (5), decided in this round's security entry and implemented under WS12 item 8, after which `tests/wasm.rs` compares the trace with task numbers. The W1 question (whether internal tasks count in §17.80's numbering) is closed by it: a runtime-internal drive spends no task number, whether or not it performs. Why: a boundary that moves closures would need the host to hold guest continuations, and the guest's ownership rules (§7) would then have to reach JS values; the pull form keeps every continuation in the guest, which is also what makes `Host.close()` sound. Rejected: marshalling closures to JS functions (ownership leaks); a second numbering scheme for internal tasks. Performance: one host round trip per callback call, already measured in W2's parity runs. Changed: §17.80 (pointer, internal tasks), ABI §8.2 (1.2.0). Impact: Opus (WS12 item 8, ABI 1.2.0).
162. **Defect message and site parity on WASM is required for M4 exit (WS-P5 deviation 7, L4 deviation 9, W2 deviation 13; round 12).** Question: a WASM contract defect carries no span and says ``contract `nonzero` violated`` where JS says ``precondition `nonzero` of positive violated``; `parity.rs` compares codes only; W1 item 7 left `sakura.debug` spans to `sakura_lir`. Decision: the `defect` record (§12.2) is a versioned contract an agent reads, and its `message` and site are the fields that tell an agent what to fix; code-only parity is not parity. **L5** (Opus, M4-LIR, after WS12 item 8): the LIR carries WS-P5's site table into the module (`sakura.debug`, ABI §12, which already reserves the section), WASM defect records render the same message as JS from the same kind, contract name and def name, and the record's site is the same `file:line:col`; `parity.rs` compares code, message and site, masking only a backend-named expectation. Static messages such as `Option.expect on None` versus the program's own `"boom"` are in scope: the message is the program's text (§17.159 (3) reasoning). Acceptance: the parity matrix (§3.7 row 4) stays green with the three fields compared on every fixture and example; `defect_*` fixtures' `.stdout` files need no backend variants afterwards. Why: parity that compares codes only would let the two backends drift in exactly the field agents act on; the site table exists since WS-P5, so the cost is a section and a renderer. Rejected: declaring message parity an M5 item (M4's acceptance says "identical stdout and effect traces", and a defect's stderr record is the one output an agent must read); comparing messages but not sites (half a contract). Performance: a custom section, no run-time cost; the message is rendered only when a defect is raised. Changed: §14.3 M4 acceptance (defect records identical), plan §3.7 row 4 wording, §3.8. Impact: Opus (L5), Sol (M4-TEST (b) parity matrix compares the three fields).
163. **§12.1 is trimmed to the CLI that exists, and the three WASM-only gaps are each placed (M4-DOCS; round 12).** Decision: (1) **`sakura build --release` is removed from §12.1.** §6.1's sentence that checks run in every build stands; there is no build mode that drops checks, and the only build-time switches are `--target`, `--emit`, `--layout`, `--manifest` and `--out-dir`. If an optimisation level ever exists it will be an RFC, because "fast by default" (§17.103) means the default is the fast one. (2) **`sakura mcp serve --tools a,b`** stays in §11.2 as planned, labelled so, scheduled after the first release and outside M4; `docs/mcp.md` and the MCP page already say "planned", and `compiler_info.features` must not advertise it (§17.141). (3) **`--manifest` on `--target wasm` is built** with V4b (§17.164): the manifest's statuses come from the same proof records and the `target` field says which profile the obligations were discharged for; a WASM build without it has no assurance record, which contradicts §17.76's purpose. (4) **`sakura test --contracts` on WASM is built** in M4-OPT, whose proved-check deletion needs a test that runs contract tests on both backends against the same proofs; its parity is a `parity.rs` test-mode fixture. (5) **`--record` stays JS-only through M4**, documented in one sentence on the testing page: recording is a development-time activity, the JS backend is always present, and the cassette a JS run writes is read by both backends. It moves to M5's list only if a WASM-only host appears. Why: a documented flag that errors is a lie to an agent, and each gap is either cheap in an item already scheduled or has a stated reason to wait. Rejected: implementing `--release` as a no-op (a flag that does nothing teaches agents to pass it); dropping `--manifest` on WASM (the manifest is the trust record); building `--record` on WASM now (no consumer). Performance: none. Changed: §6.1, §11.2, §12.1 (build row). Impact: Opus (V4b: WASM manifest; M4-OPT: `--contracts` on WASM), Astra (testing page sentence, MCP page unchanged).
164. **Verify: `unknown(resource_limit)` is a reusable result for `sakura verify`; the summary counts reused records under schema 1.1; `Entry.format` is the version field; V4b runs now (REQUESTS 2026-10-18 "V4"; plan §7 V4 items 1, 3, 6; round 12).** Decision: (1) **An `unknown` whose reason is `resource_limit` is cached and reused by `sakura verify`**, and only there: under §17.106 the `rlimit` binds deterministically for a given key (solver identity, options and limit are in the key, the clock is not), so re-solving reproduces the same `unknown` at the full limit, the most expensive query in the tree, on every run. `sakura build` never reads an `unknown` (it drops a check only on `proved`, §17.98), so nothing moves from "history" to "a reason to drop a check". A timeout `unknown`, if a solver ever reports one, stays uncached. §17.86 is amended with a pointer. (2) **The `summary` record of `sakura verify --json` gains `reused`** (the number of obligations whose record came from an authenticated entry) beside its counts, and the human summary prints `(n reused)`; this is the acceptance of plan row 9 made observable without a solver wrapper. Adding a field is a MINOR change to the JSON Lines contract: records written by `sakura verify` carry `"schema_version":"1.1"`, consumers ignore fields they do not know (§12.2's rule from this entry on), and `sakura check`'s records stay `1.0` until they change. (3) **`Entry.format` (WS12 item 5) closes the version-field question**: a proof cache entry whose `format` differs from the compiler's is "missing" and is solved again, as pre-V4 entries are today; no migration code is written for a cache. (4) **V4b** (the i64 replay through the LIR evaluator, deviation 6) is unblocked by L4 and runs now, in `sakura_cli/src/verify.rs` and `sakura_eval`, in parallel with WS12 item 8 (different crates), together with the WASM manifest (§17.163 (3)). Acceptance: a `disproved` i64 obligation is `confirmed` by replay on the LIR evaluator and the JS-profile replay is unchanged; `sakura build --target wasm --manifest` writes the manifest with `target: "wasm"`. Why: determinism is what makes a cache honest, and §17.106 bought it for `unknown` too; a count in the summary is a smaller contract change than a per-record flag and says what an agent wants to know. Rejected: a per-obligation `reused` flag (noise in every record); reusing `unknown` in `build` (nothing to reuse: build reads only `proved`); a cache migration. Performance: a tree with `unknown` obligations verifies in the time of its changed obligations. Changed: §17.86 (pointer), §12.1 (`sakura verify` row: `summary.reused`), §12.2 (schema 1.1 for verify records; unknown-field rule). Impact: Opus (V4b; `reused`; `unknown` reuse, each with its test), Astra (`agents/verify.mdx`: reuse sentence, the `reused` count).
165. **Eval pilot disposition: no diagnostic contract or language change; three bounded message improvements with independent reproducers; the next eval's harness changes; no paid run without a fresh key and the owner's approval (REQUESTS 2026-10-18 "DX5 pilot"; `evals/agent-dx/reports/2026-10-18-pilot.md`; round 12).** Reproducers (this round, a scratch `sakura new lib` project, never a task file): a pure `apply(f: fn() -> Int ! {Env})` calling `f()`; a pure caller of a `! {Fail<ParseErr>}` function; `Env.gett("HOME")`; `List.lenght([1])`; `List.map([1, 2])`; `Env.get("A", "B")`. Findings: **`V3001`** already carries a `cause` span naming the call that introduced the effect (`` `f` performs `Env` here ``), distinguishes an ordinary effect from a residual `Fail` in its fixes ("Handle the effect locally with `with`" against "Recover from the failure locally with `try`/`catch`"), and `explain V3001` names both repairs; the request's first half is built. **`V1002`** on an effect operation has a suggestion and a `machine_applicable` fix (`Env.gett` → `Env.get`), but the module-function form (`List.lenght`: "`List` has no function or variant `lenght` in its home module `std/list`") has no suggestion though it names the module. **`V2002`** names the callee's type and the two counts but not the callee or its module ("this call passes 2 arguments to a function of type `fn(String) -> Option<String> ! {Env}`, which takes 1") and leaves `expected`/`actual` null. Decision: (1) **No new code, no schema change, no semantics change**: codes are stable, and the pilot shows retrieval exhaustion, not a missing feature. (2) **Three message items** (Opus, small, one PR, a `tests/ui` fixture each; message text is not part of the versioned contract, the code, schema and span roles are): `V2002` names the callee and its home module and fills `expected`/`actual` with the two counts (`` `Env.get` (std/env) takes 1 argument, this call passes 2 ``; a lambda or local keeps today's type form); `V1002`'s module-function form gets the same did-you-mean and `machine_applicable` fix as the effect form when one candidate is within edit distance 2 (the flat two-edit fallback is withdrawn by §17.187; the proportional rule and the prefix rule remain); `V3001`'s `with` fix names the effect's deterministic std handler constructor when its home module exports exactly one (`with Env.of(…)` instead of `with ?handler`), applicability still `incomplete`, and `explain V3001` adds the `Fail` paragraph (`try`/`catch`) it lacks. (3) **Harness changes before any next run** (Opus, DX5 runner; offline replay coverage first): record every lookup identifier including `doc`, with returned size and success; split structured compiler diagnostics (parsed `--json` records) from codes quoted in retrieved text; export remaining aggregate input and the refused next-request count per episode; a separately budgeted higher-input arm (60,000 aggregate input) paired against the 40,000 arm in the same design, failures retained; at least two order seeds. (4) **Paid runs**: the exhausted key is gone and the $5.80 remainder authorises nothing; a next run needs a fresh owner key, the owner's written approval of the worst-case dollars and the episode and token caps, under the $10 total of plan `agent-dx.md` §3 unless the owner raises it. Task authoring (Astra) continues offline; no task solution is pasted into a skill or an `explain`. Why: the pilot's hard failures are censored by input, and the one submitted HTTP candidate's codes are ordinary resolve/arity/effect errors whose messages can carry one more fact each at no contract cost. Rejected: a new "unhandled effect in callback" code (the `cause` span is that fact); widening the fix to insert an argument or guess an API; relaxing a frozen signature or permission to lift a score. Performance: none. Changed: plan `agent-dx.md` §1 DX5 row (next-run conditions), §4. Impact: Opus (messages, harness), Astra (report follow-up, tasks), owner (key, approval).
166. **Order for the rest of M4 and the M4 exit gate (round 12).** The release `v0.1.0-alpha.1` is gated on WS12 items 1–3 and 5–7 and the owner's dispatch; nothing below waits on it except DX8's plugin. In flight: WS-P2b (`emit.rs`), WS12 items 1/2/5/3/4/7 (Opus), WS12 item 6 landed (Sol), M4-TEST (b) (Sol). Then, in this order, each with its owner: (1) **WS-P9** (Opus) after WS-P2b lands (§17.160 (5)); (2) **WS12 item 8** (Opus) after item 7 lands, carrying §17.159 (5) and §17.161's invariants; (3) **V4b + WASM `--manifest` + `reused` + `unknown` reuse** (Opus) now, in parallel with (2); (4) **DX5 harness telemetry and the three message items** (Opus) now, small, in parallel; (5) **L5** defect message and site parity (Opus) after (2), with M4-TEST (b) comparing the three fields (Sol); (6) **M4-OPT** (Opus) after (1), (2) and (5): `--layout=niche` with the research/08 §6.2 nested-option corpus on both layouts, proved-check deletion with the predicate's computation gone unless WS-P5's bracket elision already removed it, `--contracts` on WASM, the `restarts` counter and ABI 1.2.0 (§17.160, §17.161), and the §17.118 bar or its stated carry (§17.160 (1)); (7) **the M4 envelope** (Opus WS-B: the `wasm` lane with Node and Wasmtime engines; Sol: toolchains and the nightly; Astra: numbers) after (6); (8) **the quiet-window bench and bless**: the JS close-out (plan `performance-r1.md` §5) is ready now, since WS-P5, WS-P7 and WS-P8 have landed, and runs the first time the owner idles the machine; the WASM envelope joins at the next quiet window after (7); Astra measures, the owner blesses, agents never bless (§17.153); (9) **DX8**: the plugin (Sol) after `v0.1.0-alpha.1` has shipped and DX1 has been stable for that release; the playground (Opus) after (6); Linguist when the usage threshold is real. **M4 exit gate** (the owner, or a delegated reviewer as §17.159 (1) did): the parity matrix green on `{js, wasm} × {ubuntu, macos}` comparing stdout, traces, test records and defect code, message and site; `sakura verify` reusing records with `reused` in its summary and a `disproved` i64 obligation confirmed by replay; WS12 items 4 and 8 landed; `--layout=niche` passing the nested-option corpus; the §17.109 M4 ceilings measured on the reference machine at a quiet window and recorded in `bench/deltas.md`, each miss carried to a named decision; the §17.118 bars met or carried under §17.160 (1); M4-DOCS rechecked against the CLI built from the exit commit. Research/12 (Astra) starts now with the parts that need no quiet-window numbers (§17.167). Why: (1), (2) and (5) touch files M4-OPT needs settled; (3) and (4) touch none of them; the quiet window is the owner's calendar, not an agent's. Rejected: starting M4-OPT before WS-P9 (both edit `opt.rs`); blessing on a loaded host; holding the envelope for DX8. Changed: plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.8 (the table), §3.7 dependency line (pointer). Impact: Opus, Sol, Astra as named; the owner (release dispatch, quiet window, bless, exit gate).
167. **M5 planning starts now: research/12 first, then five workstreams with acceptance bars; no M5 code before a design round reads research/12 (plan §3.7 row 11; round 12).** Decision: (1) **Research first.** Astra writes `research/12-m5-native.md` now; the parts that need quiet-window numbers (the `List` representation question of §17.152, the record representation of §17.138) are marked "pending the §17.166 (8) window" and filled when it has run. The document answers: a Cranelift survey against ABI 1.x (calling convention, the frame protocol and twins natively, `i64`/`f64` scalars, the generic word, host imports as native function pointers, WASI-free), the native build of `sakura_rt` (it is Rust; what in `wasm.rs` is WASM-specific), Perceus reuse over the LIR's `Dup`/`Drop` with a hit-rate instrumentation design, borrow inference over §17.149's owned facts, `W8001`'s rule (when a persistent update copies because the value is shared, and where to point), and platform B's targets (§17.157 (2)). (2) **Workstreams, in order, each with its bar** (`docs/plans/m5-native.md`, a skeleton now, filled by round 13): **N0** reuse and borrow instrumentation on WASM (`reuses`, `borrows` counters; hit rate on the paired `rbtree` workloads, §17.118), so M5's optimisations are measured against today's runtime before a native backend exists; **N1** the native runtime: `sakura_rt` built as a native static library with the ABI §8 host imports for the root handlers (Console, Clock, Random, Env, Fs) in Rust, `host_root_handlers` and the run-mode corpus passing natively from the LIR evaluator's harness; **N2** the Cranelift backend: LIR → Cranelift IR, frame protocol and twins, `sakura build --target native` behind a feature flag, the full parity corpus `{js, wasm, native}` with identical stdout, traces and defect records; **N3** Perceus reuse: constructor sites reuse a uniquely owned object of the same size class, `List.set` and record update in place when unique, hit rate reported in every benchmark record, `sieve`/`nbody`/`rbtree` deltas against §17.152's carried ceilings; **N4** borrow inference: parameters the LIR proves only read are passed borrowed, `dups`/`drops` on the benchmark record fall by the amount research/12 predicts, parity unchanged; **N5** `W8001` copy diagnostics: a warning at the update whose value is shared, with a `cause` span at the sharing site, positive and negative `tests/ui` fixtures, never firing where reuse succeeded. M5's exit is §14.3's line plus the §17.109 M5 envelope on platform A, native and WASM both green on the parity matrix. (3) Owners: Astra (research/12, measurement), Opus (N0–N5), Sol (toolchains for the native lane, CI for platform B when it starts). Why: M4 taught that the ABI document written first let two implementers start in parallel (ABI §0); research/12 is that document for M5, and N0 makes every later claim measurable. Rejected: starting Cranelift before the native runtime exists (nothing to link); Perceus before its hit-rate counter (no bar); a second IR for native (the LIR is the backend-independent IR, §17.118). Performance: the §17.109 M5 envelope. Changed: plan `m5-native.md` (new skeleton), plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.7 row 11 (research now). Impact: Astra, Opus, Sol.
168. **Road to 1.0: a provisional outline (round 12).** Recorded as `docs/plans/road-to-1.0.md`, labelled provisional in every heading; nothing in it is a date or a promise. Stages: (1) `v0.1.0-alpha.1` (channels §17.157; M3 closed); (2) M4 exit (§17.166) and `v0.1.0`, the deliberately scoped first non-alpha preview STABILITY.md names; (3) **the normative spec** in `docs/spec/`: grammar (§3), static semantics (types, rows, refinements, contracts), dynamic semantics (effects, handlers, defects, determinism), the diagnostic contract (§12.2 schemas, code stability), the ABI as a normative annex, each section with its conformance fixtures, written from the implementation and the `tests/semantics` corpus, and only text that is pinned by a fixture is normative; it starts after M4 exit, with the grammar and diagnostics first because agents consume them; (4) **stability guarantees** before any `1.0`: source compatibility within a major, diagnostic codes never reused, `--json` schemas versioned and additive within a major, the ABI MAJOR rule (§0), a deprecation path of at least one minor, an MSRV policy; (5) **packages** (plan §6 question 1, milestone P): MVS resolution, lockfile hashing, capability ceilings, a registry decision, after M5 unless a consumer appears; (6) **platforms** B and C (§17.157 (2)) each after the previous stage has shipped and been accepted on a fresh host; (7) **the evidence threshold** from real usage before `1.0`: at least three programs outside this repository maintained for one release cycle by someone other than the owner and the agents, each with a `sakura test` suite and a reported upgrade across a minor; the agent-DX suite at a stated pass rate under a stated budget on two independent model families; zero open soundness bugs in the checker or verifier for one release cycle; the benchmark envelope held at a quiet window for two consecutive releases. Why: 1.0 is a claim about other people's programs, and the only evidence for it is other people's programs. Rejected: dating the stages; a spec written before the implementation it describes is stable; a 1.0 without packages. Changed: plan `road-to-1.0.md` (new). Impact: the owner (every stage boundary is theirs).
169. **Native ABI: the offset-heap compatibility profile `offset32-v1`, an explicit instance context, C ABI exports, ABI 1.3.0 and 1.4.0 (research/12 §4, §4.1; REQUESTS research/12 item 1; round 13).** Question: research/12 found that `sakura_rt`'s `Mem` already implements the `u32` offset space over a native `Vec<u8>`, that a direct-pointer heap would change offsets, descriptors, closures, free lists and transport at once, and that the native wrapper needs an instance context the WASM module gets for free from its single linear memory. Decision: (1) **N1 and N2 build on ABI 1.2.0's bytes unchanged**, named the layout profile `offset32-v1`: 8-byte headers and slots, the 32-bit `Word`, the §3.4 layout table, arena ids, frames, handler activations and the §8 copy transport are byte-identical on native; a `Word` is an offset from a heap base, never a host pointer, and a native `Ptr` LIR value is an offset too. The heap is one explicitly 8-byte-aligned backing allocation reserved to `SAKURA_MAX_HEAP` and committed in 64 KiB steps (`mmap`/`VirtualAlloc`-class reservation, so the base never moves; a `Vec<u8>` is not acceptable because its base moves on growth and its alignment is 1); generated code loads the base from the context once per function entry and again after any call that may allocate, which the LIR validator checks. (2) **Every exported and imported native function takes `ctx: *mut SakuraInstance` first** and uses the target C ABI (System V on Linux, Apple AArch64 on macOS); the signatures are written in ABI.md §1b "Native target profile" as C prototypes, never inferred from Rust layout. Internal twins may use Cranelift's private convention (tail-capable where the target supports it; the WS-P9 loop and the frame fallback remain the correctness path, as research/12 §2.1 says). `code` stays a `u32` index into a per-module table of native function pointers; no address is stored in a `Word`. Overflow and zero divisors are branches to the defect path, never CPU traps; a trap is `D090`. The Rust runtime builds with `panic = "abort"` for the native library; nothing unwinds across generated code, and host adapters convert their own failures to status `3 host_failure` (`D091`). (3) **Versions.** ABI 1.2.0 is M4-OPT's (§17.160, §17.161). **1.3.0** (N0b, MINOR) appends four `u64` counters to `stats()` after `restarts`: `reuse_attempts`, `reuses`, `borrow_candidates`, `borrows` (§17.176 defines them; the export then returns 120 bytes). **1.4.0** (N1a, MINOR) adds §1b: the profile name `offset32-v1`, the context and C prototypes, the dispatch table, the native handle table's generation and retirement policy (slots retired before wrap, as §7.2), and the `sakura.native` metadata record every native artifact carries (target triple, baseline ISA, Cranelift version, profile, ABI version, `module_hash`) that the driver checks before linking or running, as `V8003` does for WASM. Neither bump changes a shipped byte; if M4-OPT has not landed 1.2.0 when N0b is ready, N0b waits, never reorders. (4) **A pointer-width profile is a separate, later decision.** `ptr64-v1` (64-bit `Word`, direct pointers) may be prototyped only after N2's `offset32-v1` baseline is measured at a quiet window, under its own §17 entry and ABI MINOR with a distinct profile name; a program compiled against one profile never links against the other, and nothing in N0–N5 depends on it. Why: the offset heap is the only profile whose correctness the M4 corpus, the cycle and lifetime tests and `sakura.debug` already pin; porting it is an engineering task, redesigning it is a measured decision; the context argument is what lets one process hold two instances (`Host.close()`, tests) without a global. Rejected: `Vec<u8>` as the heap (moving base, unaligned); addresses in `Word` (breaks the 32-bit tables and transport); widening pointers while porting (research/12 §4.1); an LLVM or QBE backend now (research/12 §2: no failing workload justifies a second backend). Performance: base-plus-offset addressing costs one register and reloads after allocating calls, which N2 reports in its compile and run records so the `ptr64-v1` question is argued from numbers. Changed: §13.5 (profile named), ABI.md (1.3.0 and 1.4.0 when they land). Impact: Opus (N0b, N1a, N2), the ABI document.
170. **Native host coverage in M5: the standard services natively, no Node bridge in the acceptance, `@external(js)` is `V8004` on `--target native`, no WASI (research/12 §6.2; REQUESTS research/12 item 2; round 13).** Decision: (1) **N1a** supplies the root handlers in Rust: Console, Clock (host and virtual), Random (`sakura-gen-v1` unchanged, entropy only when authorised), Env, Fs with `file_*` through capability-relative directories (`cap-std` 4.0.3 over `std::fs`; the permission checks stay the runtime's, the crate is a mechanism). (2) **N1b** supplies, in this order, `Http` (`reqwest` 0.13.5 with `rustls` 0.23.46, no default features, redirects and proxies off unless the existing host policy enables them, grants rechecked on every redirect, bodies and chunks bounded, streaming incremental), `Sql.sqlite` (`rusqlite` 0.40.2 `bundled`, one connection-owning worker per connection, the lazy `Fs.root()` in the clause's task as §17.159 (5)), `serve` (`hyper` 1.12.0 server primitives; the 1 MiB body cap, `413`, the `D091`-and-`500` invalid-reply path and the listen permissions as the JS host built them under WS12 items 3 and 4), `Llm` (Rust code inside the host over the native `Http` adapter plus the existing SSE parser's semantics; no provider SDK; cassettes and `Http.scripted` are the parity oracle), and every `sync` helper of `builtins.sigs` the JS host serves today (`cli_*`, `secret_*`, `log_write`, the JSON and schema helpers, string helpers): each gets a Rust adapter or, if it cannot be ported for a stated reason, an entry in a native exclusion table that `sakura build --target native` reports as `V8004`. (3) **`@external(js, …)` in a user package is `V8004` on `--target native`**, the code `target_unsupported_external` that WASM already emits; its explain text gains the native sentence; no new code, no schema change. Handle kinds 5 (`callback`) and 6 (`jsvalue`) exist only in the JS bridge profile; the native host has no kind 5 or 6 and never reinterprets a handle as a pointer. (4) **An explicit Node bridge may stage N1b development but counts for nothing**: the N1b acceptance and the `{js, wasm, native}` parity matrix run with no Node process, and a bridge, if written, lives under `tools/` and is deleted or clearly labelled before M5 exit. (5) **Excluded from M5 and named**: arbitrary JS FFI on native, WASI as a native host, cross-thread value sends (§17.171), any provider beyond what `Llm` already supports. Why: §17.167 N1's bar is "the run-mode corpus passes natively", and the corpus uses every standard service; a bridge would make that sentence true without a native host existing. Rejected: `sqlx` (async and multi-database machinery the SQLite-only API does not use); `axum` (routing the `serve` adapter does not need); a new diagnostic code for native unavailability (`V8004` already says it). Performance: the native host is in-process Rust; N1b records per-service latency on the `json_api` and `agent_loop` examples against the JS host as directional evidence, not a ceiling. Changed: `V8004` explain text (Opus, N1b), plan `m5-native.md`. Impact: Opus (N1a, N1b), Sol (`cap-std`, `reqwest`, `rustls`, `rusqlite`, `hyper`, `tokio` pins in `Cargo.toml`, licence notices), Astra (parity and latency records).
171. **Concurrency on native: one thread owns an instance, a current-thread executor, a bounded blocking pool, cancellation detaches and never undoes, promotion to atomic counts is deferred (research/12 §6.1; REQUESTS research/12 item 3; round 13).** Decision: (1) **One OS thread owns a Sakura instance**: its heap, scheduler, reference counts and handle tables are touched by that thread only; the `unsafe impl Sync` justification of the WASM single-thread global is not carried to native. The host executor is Tokio 1.53.3's `current_thread` runtime on that thread; `complete` and `invoke` are called only between trampoline returns, and a completion arriving while the guest runs is queued, as ABI §8.5 already requires. (2) **Blocking I/O** (`Fs`, `file_*`, `Sql.sqlite`) runs on a bounded pool (`SAKURA_BLOCKING_THREADS`, default 4; admission also bounded by `SAKURA_MAX_REQUESTS`) that receives copied inputs and returns copied bytes and ids over a channel; no worker holds a guest offset, a borrowed value or the instance. Network I/O runs on the current-thread executor. Native host parallelism is not parallel Sakura execution. (3) **Cancellation**: `abort(request_id)` drops the HTTP future or timer; a blocking job cannot be interrupted (Tokio's `spawn_blocking` contract), so the request is detached (state `cancelled`, epoch bumped, continuation dropped), the job runs to completion, its result is discarded by the §8.5 stale check and any handle it produced is retired; SQLite additionally receives `sqlite3_interrupt` through `rusqlite`'s interrupt handle so a long statement stops early. A cancelled `Fs` or `Sql` operation is reported as cancelled, never as undone; a committed write stays committed, which the `Sql.sqlite` cancellation fixture asserts by reading the database afterwards. (4) **Shutdown** (`Host.close()`, process exit, a trap): cancel every pending request, wait for in-flight blocking jobs (each is one bounded operation), run the resource ledger of §8.6 (close handles, drop connections), then return; finalizers that need to suspend run through the finalizer trampoline, never by dropping a future. (5) **Cross-thread sends** (§13.5's promotion to atomic counts) are deferred past M5: no `Task` or value crosses threads; the promotion protocol over the reachable graph, its race tests and a named maintainer review are a later entry, listed in §16. Pure CPU loops gain no new cancellation points. Why: the ABI's request generations, task epochs and queued completions were designed so that a host thread never needs guest state; the pool and the executor are the smallest native host that honours them. Rejected: a multi-thread Tokio runtime driving the guest (the heap is non-atomic); aborting blocking threads (impossible safely); `Arc<Rt>` or a root-only atomic counter as "thread safety" (research/12 §6.1). Performance: one channel hop per blocking completion; the `json_api` example on the native host reports it. Changed: §8.4 and §13.5 (pointer), §16 (promotion item), ABI.md §8.5 native paragraph in 1.4.0. Impact: Opus (N1a, N1b), Sol (`tokio` pin).
172. **`W8001` is a proven-copy static warning with a witness span; unknown uniqueness stays in `--dump owned`; observed copies are not a diagnostic in M5 (research/12 §8; REQUESTS research/12 item 6; round 13).** Question: §17.167 N5 says "never firing where reuse succeeded", and research/12 shows that a site may copy on one execution and reuse on another, and that WS-P7's `shared(<reason>)` is a loss of proof, not a proof of copying. Decision: (1) **`W8001` fires only when the analysis proves the copy**: the consumed argument of a persistent update is `shared` for a reason with a located witness in the same def that is live at the update on every path to it: `borrowed-after` (a later use of the binding), `captured` (a closure that captured it and is live after the update), `stored` (an allocation holding it that is live after the update). It never fires for `origin` (a parameter or a non-fresh builtin may be unique at run time), `escapes-call` (the callee may consume) or `join` (a mixed join). Primary span: the update; `cause` label: the witness (the later use, the capture, the store), using the existing `cause` field and no new structured field; message: "this update copies shared `List` storage; `<name>` still holds the earlier version" with the witness named; fixes are `suggested`, never `machine_applicable`, and suggest shortening the earlier version's lifetime only where semantics permit, never deleting a retained version or reordering effects. (2) **Cause-span provenance** is added to `opt::owned`'s `Shared` (an optional witness span beside the reason) and printed by `--dump owned`; `sakura check --json` output for `W8001` carries only the documented `cause` label, so the diagnostic schema is unchanged. (3) **The N5 bar is reworded**: "never fires at a site that reuses in any execution of the corpus" is pinned by the N0b counters: every positive `tests/ui` fixture is also run with counters and has zero reuse hits and non-zero copied nodes at the warned site's program; every negative fixture with a dynamic reuse site shows hits and no warning. (4) **No observed-copy report in M5.** A profile-guided copy report, if ever added, is a runtime report with hit and miss counts under `sakura bench` or a `--stats` output, never a `W` code and never run by `sakura check`; it needs its own entry. Why: a warning agents cannot refute teaches them to delete persistence (research/12 §8); a proof with a span they can read teaches them where the alias is. Rejected: warning on `origin` (noise on every parameter); a blanket warning at mixed sites (violates N5's own bar); a new `witness` JSON field (the `cause` label already carries a span). Performance: none; the analysis is the existing pass with one more field. Changed: §8.2 (`W8001` sentence), `W8001` explain text (Opus, N5), plan `m5-native.md` N5. Impact: Opus (N5), CODEOWNERS review as a diagnostic-contract change (wording only).
173. **"Sanitizer-clean" in §14.3 means four named lanes, each with a detection control, and the generated-code lane is a checked lowering plus Memcheck, not ASan (research/12 §7; REQUESTS research/12 item 7; round 13).** Decision: the M5 exit claim reads "runtime: Miri and ASan/LSan; generated code: Memcheck with allocator annotations and the checked lowering", and each lane is a CI job that fails when its seeded fault is not detected. (1) **Miri** over `sakura_rt`'s host tests (allocator, objects, arenas, lists, maps, scheduler, codecs) with fake I/O and the provenance cases research/12 §7 item 1 names. (2) **ASan + LSan** with the pinned nightly on `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` over the runtime, the native host crate and bundled C dependencies (SQLite compiled under the sanitizer where `rusqlite`'s build allows; every uninstrumented library listed in the job's output). (3) **Memcheck** on `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` over the native parity corpus with the allocator registering every block, free and reuse through Valgrind client requests (`crabgrind` 0.4.0 behind a `valgrind` feature of `sakura_rt`, compiled out otherwise) so one arena does not hide use-after-free between objects; a baseline ISA the installed Valgrind supports. (4) **The checked lowering**: `sakura build --target native --native-checks` (behind the `native` feature) routes every generated load, store and bulk copy through bounds and poison checks against the allocator's own shadow (poison on free and on reuse-token consumption, unpoison on allocation), covering frame slots and globals; it is the generated-access coverage ASan cannot give CLIF, and it runs the corpus on both platform-A targets. (5) **Detection controls** (`tests/native_detect.rs`): one generated out-of-bounds load, one freed-object read, one double drop, one invalid slot kind, one stale callback or request, each a fixture that must be caught by the lane that claims it; a lane that misses its fault is red, and no acceptance sentence cites a lane without its control. (6) The semantic and resource stress list of research/12 §7 item 5 becomes `tests/semantics` fixtures and runtime tests tagged `m5-stress`, run under the direct, reuse and borrow toggles with the live-object baseline asserted. Lanes (2) and (3) are Linux-only; macOS runs (1), (4) and the stress set. Why: linking an ASan runtime instruments nothing Cranelift emitted; a claim must name the tool that would have caught the bug. Rejected: "full ASan coverage" as the exit wording (false for generated code); treating a green Rust suite as memory safety of emitted code; skipping Memcheck because the checked lowering exists (they catch different faults: the lowering knows Sakura's shadow, Memcheck knows the machine). Performance: the checked lowering is a debug build; the Memcheck lane is a nightly schedule plus on demand, required on pull requests only for the fixtures `native_detect.rs` names. Changed: §14.3 (M5 acceptance sentence), plan `m5-native.md` §4. Impact: Opus (lowering, annotations, fixtures), Sol (`native-sanitize` jobs: nightly toolchain, Valgrind install, scheduling), named maintainer review before the lane is cited for a security claim.
174. **Regions: general region inference is deferred out of M5; §14.3's phrase is satisfied by the existing arenas plus a measured trigger (research/12 §10 "Regions gap"; REQUESTS research/12 item 8; round 13).** Question: §14.3's M5 line says "regions as an internal optimization" and N0–N5 has no row for it; research/12 observes that the activation and frame arenas are not evidence of a value-region optimisation and warns against holding every value until task end to improve counters. Decision: (1) No region inference lands in M5; §14.3's M5 line is amended to "regions deferred (§17.174)". (2) **The trigger**: after N3 and N4 have landed and the quiet window has measured them, Astra reports, per allocation-kernel program (`binary_trees`, `shapes`, `pipeline`, `rbtree`), the share of `requested` bytes whose objects die inside the direct twin that allocated them (a counter N0b can add behind the `valgrind`-style feature, not in `stats()`); if that share on any program whose §17.109 ceiling is missed exceeds the share Perceus reuse already recovers, a region round opens with its own research and entry, scoped to non-escaping allocations in direct twins with per-value drops and finalizers kept. If the measurement is unavailable, the default is no region work. Why: reuse and borrowing attack the same allocations with mechanisms the LIR already has; regions need an escape analysis the compiler does not, and an unmeasured one would be built to move a counter. Rejected: a frame-local bump region now (speculative; §1 item 9 forbids a hidden cost at the use site, and a region that outlives its values is one); redefining the arenas as the feature. Performance: none in M5. Changed: §14.3 (M5 line), §8.4 (pointer). Impact: Astra (the measurement), a later round.
175. **Toolchain and release gates for native: Cranelift 0.136.3 pinned, debug minimums, OS floors, the compile budget set from N2's numbers, `--target native` ships behind a cargo feature then as an experimental flag, the GNU matrix A→B→C (research/12 §1, §2.1, §9; REQUESTS research/12 item 9; §17.157; ADR 0003; round 13).** Decision: (1) **Pins** (`compiler/Cargo.toml`, exact versions, the latest stable on 2026-10-18 from the crates.io registry): `cranelift-codegen`, `cranelift-frontend`, `cranelift-module`, `cranelift-object`, `cranelift-native` 0.136.3 (declared Rust minimum 1.96.0; the toolchain is 1.99.1), `target-lexicon` 0.13.5, `gimli` 0.34.0, `object` 0.40.0; host crates as §17.170 (`tokio` 1.53.3, `reqwest` 0.13.5, `rustls` 0.23.46, `rusqlite` 0.40.2, `hyper` 1.12.0, `cap-std` 4.0.3); `crabgrind` 0.4.0 behind the `valgrind` feature. Upgrades are deliberate and qualified (Wasmtime's monthly cadence is not a reason to move); the family moves together. (2) **Crates**: `sakura_codegen_native` (LIR → CLIF → object, the `sakura.native` record, DWARF line tables and unwind tables via `gimli`/`object`) and `sakura_host_native` (links `sakura_rt` as a `staticlib`, the adapters of §17.170, the `main` driver); `sakura_cli` gains the cargo feature `native` that pulls both; `sakura_rt` gains `crate-type = ["rlib", "staticlib"]` under its own `native` feature and keeps the `wasm32` build unchanged. Dependency direction stays §14.1's: `sakura_cli → sakura_codegen_native → sakura_lir`, and `sakura_host_native` depends on nothing above `sakura_rt`. (3) **Debug minimums from N2**: symbols, a DWARF line table mapping to Sakura sites, unwind tables (`.eh_frame` on Linux, compact unwind or `__eh_frame` on macOS) that a native profiler and `lldb`/`gdb` backtrace accept on both platform-A targets; the logical task stack keeps rendering from `sakura.debug`'s site table; variable and type DWARF is a later bar. (4) **Native compile budget: pending N2's measurement.** N2 reports, per benchmark program on the reference machine, lowering, codegen, object emission and link time separately at p95; the round after N2 sets the end-to-end `sakura build --target native` budget from them. Until then the research/10 §6 build budget applies to compile-to-object (link excluded and reported beside it), and no native compile-time claim is made. (5) **OS floors**, stated in STABILITY.md only once the native smoke tests of (7) exist: Linux glibc ≥ 2.35 (the `ubuntu-22.04` release runner of `packaging/targets.json`); macOS ≥ 12.0 on `aarch64-apple-darwin` (`MACOSX_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=12.0` in the release build). Distributed artifacts use the baseline ISA of the triple, never `target-cpu=native`. (6) **0.x flag policy**: until N2's parity matrix is green on both platform-A targets, `--target native` exists only in builds with the `native` cargo feature and no release artifact carries it; from the first 0.x minor after that, release binaries carry it, `sakura build --help` labels it experimental, STABILITY.md lists it outside compatibility, and a native build prints one line naming the profile and ABI; the label is removed by a later entry after platform B has shipped. (7) **Matrix**, consistent with §17.157 (2) and ADR 0003 (one entry per stage in `packaging/targets.json`): **A** `aarch64-apple-darwin`, `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` is M5's exit platform: native corpus and examples, the parity matrix `{js, wasm, native} × {ubuntu, macos}`, debug and unwind smoke, the §17.173 lanes, the §17.109 envelope at a quiet window on the reference host. **B** `aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu` after A has shipped and been accepted on a fresh host: AArch64 ELF and alignment, a GitHub-hosted `ubuntu-24.04-arm` CI lane running the parity matrix natively (QEMU is accepted only for functional smoke, never for numbers), the bounded-heap and cancellation fixtures. **C** `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` and `x86_64-apple-darwin` after B: PE/COFF with the Windows calling convention, stack probes and unwind tables, Windows paths and handle semantics in the adapters, Intel macOS deployment target; native debugger symbols on Windows are a stated limitation (Sakura's own site table renders traces) until a CodeView decision. No musl stage (§17.157 (2)); the installer and npm launcher keep refusing it. Why: pins are the only API stability Cranelift offers before 1.0 (research/12 §2); a feature gate lets N2 land without a release promise; the matrix is the owner's, and a native lane joins it per stage rather than redefining it. Rejected: `rustup target add` as a cross toolchain (research/12 §9); a native compile budget invented before N2 emits an object; shipping the flag before parity. Performance: measured by N2 and the quiet window, not promised here. Changed: §14.1 (two crates named, Cranelift 0.136.3), §13.5 (pointer), STABILITY.md later (floors, experimental flag, by Sol when (6) applies), plan `m5-native.md` §5. Impact: Sol (pins, features, CI lanes, STABILITY and `docs/release.md` sentences when the flag ships), Opus (crates), the owner (platform acceptance on a fresh host, as ADR 0003).
176. **Reuse and borrowing: the N0b counters, WS-P7 facts recomputed in the LIR from the snapshot with a parity test, reuse-token and borrow boundaries, the `List.map` gate in N3 (research/12 §3, §4, §5; REQUESTS research/12 item 4; round 13).** Decision: (1) **Counters** (ABI 1.3.0, §17.169 (3)), each cumulative per instance, deterministic for a fixed execution: `reuse_attempts` counts executed storage-reuse opportunities (a constructor site holding a reuse token, an owned collection-node update testing a node, an owned wrapper update), `reuses` counts the ones that consumed existing storage; `borrow_candidates` counts, at run time, calls through sites the LIR marked as a candidate pair `E` (a live-after-call owned argument whose `Dup`/`Drop` pair borrowing would remove, research/12 §3.2); `borrows` counts calls that actually passed an argument under the borrowed convention (zero before N4). The benchmark record (schema `sakura-bench-v1` unchanged, additive fields) carries the four plus `reuse_rate = reuses / reuse_attempts` (`null` when attempts are zero), the counter set keyed by the record's `abi_version`, and miss reasons in a debug build only (`shared`, `static_singleton`, `incompatible_layout`, `no_candidate`); a wrapper hit never masks copied descendants, because collection-node attempts and hits are counted per node. (2) **WS-P7 facts reach the LIR by recomputation, not serialisation**: `opt::owned` runs over the committed Core snapshot inside `sakura_lir::lower` exactly as the direct facts do (§17.118), the snapshot stays fact-free (§17.149 (2)), and `--dump owned` from the JS path and from the LIR must agree on every corpus program (a parity test beside `--dump direct`'s). Before N0b lands, the LIR's conservative ownership stands, which is why no reuse claim predates N0b. (3) **Reuse tokens** (N3): a token is created only when the consumed object's count is 1 and its class, layout row, slot kinds and child count match the constructor that will consume it (same size class alone is insufficient, research/12 §3.1); it lives inside one LIR region with no suspension point, `Perform`, host call, unknown call or escaping closure between its creation and its consumption; every exit edge of that region, including per-instruction defect cleanup edges and restart aborts (§17.160 (2)), frees an unconsumed token; the LIR validator checks linearity (created once, consumed or freed exactly once on every path, never duplicated, never exposed as an object); finalizer and resource objects are never candidates; a restartable entry's held inputs are not unique and legitimately lower the hit rate. (4) **Borrow boundaries** (N4): a parameter is passed borrowed only to read-only builtins and direct twins (N4a), then within a direct component by bounded call-graph inference (N4b); never across a suspension, a handler clause, a captured closure, a host call or an unknown callee; the owner outlives the borrow because the caller holds it in a frame slot or a twin local for the whole call; a borrow that would add a post-call `Drop` in tail position is declined and the owned convention kept; N4's numerical bar is `dups` and `drops` falling by exactly the `borrow_candidates` the baseline recorded on an isolated fixture set, and by `E` minus promotions on the benchmark programs, with no invented percentage. (5) **`List.map` joins N3** as §14.3 requires: `let original = [1,2,3]; let updated = List.map(original, fn(x) { x + 1 })` leaves `original` intact when live; with `original` dead, `map` reuses the leaf and tail storage when the result layout is compatible (same element representation and leaf width), proven by `reuse_attempts` and `reuses` in the fixture's record and by `requested` bytes; a defect inside the mapped function frees the token and the partially built result without leaking (a fixture with a failing closure at element two under `audit_owned`). Why: a counter that counts candidates as hits would make N3 unfalsifiable; recomputing a fact from one IR is a parity test for free (§17.118); token linearity in the validator is cheaper than a leak hunt. Rejected: serialising facts into the snapshot (§17.149 (2)); same-size-class reuse without a layout check; borrowing across suspension (the frame owns the value, not the callee); a percentage bar before `E` exists. Performance: four counter increments on paths that already test a count; nothing in a release build beyond that. Changed: §14.3 (pointer), §13.2 (facts in the LIR), ABI §10 (1.3.0), plan `m5-native.md` N0b, N3, N4. Impact: Opus (N0b, N3, N4), Astra (the paired `rbtree` protocol and `bench/deltas.md` rows), `tools/sakura_eval` (`audit_owned` covers tokens).
177. **Representation decisions stay pending the quiet windows, each with the measurement that decides it and a stated default (research/12 §5, §10; REQUESTS research/12 item 5; §17.152; §17.160 (1); round 13).** Decision: nothing changes representation in N0–N5; M5 ships `offset32-v1` with the 32-way trie, boxed generic words and constructor-site reuse only, and each question below is decided by the named measurement in a later entry. (1) **`List`: trie, flat owned sequence, or hybrid.** Decided by the second quiet window after N3 (the first establishes the native baseline and §17.152's carries; the second compares the research/12 §5 prototypes), on the reference host, native lane: `sieve`, `nbody`, `spectral_norm` time and copied bytes against `rbtree` retained versions, `pipeline` and the §17.116 builders' time, copied bytes and RSS; a flat representation wins only if the persistent workloads stay within their §17.109 ceilings. Default if the window does not happen: the trie stays and the three ceilings are carried again under §17.152 (2), named in `bench/deltas.md`. (2) **Scalar leaves** (unboxed `Int`/`Float` elements): decided at the same window by `nbody` and `spectral_norm` `boxes` and time against the generic adapters' cost on `shapes` and `map_set`; default: boxed words stay (ABI §4). (3) **Record reuse and representation** (§17.138's 104-byte `Tree`): decided by N3's `reuses` on `nbody`, `shapes`, `binary_trees` at the first window after N3; a layout change beyond constructor-site reuse needs an ABI MINOR under a new profile; default: constructor-site reuse only. (4) **`nbody`'s §17.118 carry** (§17.160 (1)) is read at the first window after N2 on the native lane and closed or carried with (2). Why: research/12 was asked for numbers and declined to invent them; the defaults make M5 shippable without the windows while keeping every ceiling a named carry rather than a quiet miss. Rejected: deciding `List` from the historical JS ablations (§17.152 says they motivate, not decide); a hybrid "when owned" switch without the shared-copy cost measured. Performance: none until a window decides. Changed: §17.152 (pointer). Impact: Astra (both windows, the prototypes' protocol), the owner (announces and blesses). Round 18 adds question (5), the `Option` niche (§17.199 (1)), and question (6), `deltablue`'s ownership through parameters and a dense `Int`-keyed map (§17.201 (2)); plan `m5-native.md` §3 lists both.
178. **The M5 queue: N0a may start now beside the M4 close-out; everything else has a named start condition (round 13).** Decision: `docs/plans/m5-native.md` is the executable plan with these rows. **N0a** (Sol; now, in parallel with §17.166's queue): the two crates of §17.175 (2) as compiling skeletons with one smoke test each (an object with a function returning a constant linked and run; the staticlib linked into a Rust test that calls `sakura_abi_version`), the pins in `Cargo.toml` and `Cargo.lock`, the `native` features, a `native` CI job on both platform-A runners, and the Valgrind and nightly installs for §17.173. It touches no file of rows 1–8 of plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.8 except the workspace `Cargo.toml` (additive lines) and `Cargo.lock`, so it may start now; §17.167's "no M5 code before the design round" is satisfied by this round. **N0b** (Opus; after M4-OPT lands ABI 1.2.0): §17.176 (1) and (2). **N1a** (Opus; after N0a, and after M4-OPT's ABI text has landed so the native profile text is written against 1.2.0): §17.169, §17.170 (1), §17.171, ABI 1.4.0. **N1b** (Opus; after N1a; in parallel with N2, different crates): §17.170 (2)–(4). **N2** (Opus; after N1a and L5, which supplies the site table it renders defects from): the Cranelift backend behind the feature, the parity matrix with native. **N3** (Opus; after N2 and N0b; accepted against ceilings at the first quiet window after it lands, §17.153 (4)): §17.176 (3), (5). **N4** (Opus; after N3, same validator files): §17.176 (4). **N5** (Opus; after N0b; in parallel with N2): §17.172. **Measurement** (Astra): the native lane in `bench/harness`, the paired `rbtree` protocol, the windows of §17.177, `bench/deltas.md` rows, the `docs/` native page when §17.175 (6) ships the flag. **Platform B** (Sol): after A is accepted, §17.175 (7). **M5 exit** (the owner or a delegated reviewer as §17.159 (1)): §14.3's M5 line as amended (§17.172–§17.174), the parity matrix `{js, wasm, native} × {ubuntu, macos}` green comparing stdout, traces, test records and defect code, message and site, the §17.173 lanes with their controls green, the paired `rbtree` hit rate in every record, the §17.109 M5 envelope measured at a quiet window on platform A with every miss a named carry, and WASM still green. Why: the only M5 work that touches no M4 file is the toolchain skeleton, and it is the longest lead item (pins, licences, CI minutes); everything else waits for the ABI text M4-OPT owns. Rejected: starting N1a's runtime port before 1.2.0's text (two agents editing one ABI bump); starting N3 before N0b (no bar). Performance: none. Changed: plan `m5-native.md` (rewritten from the skeleton), plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` is untouched (its §3.8 queue stands). Impact: Sol (N0a now), Opus, Astra, the owner.
179. **Source extension `.sak` replaces `.sakura` (owner, 2026-10-18).** Decision (the owner's): Sakura Lang source files end in `.sak`; this lands before the first release, `v0.1.0-alpha.1`, as a clean switch, no fallback: no `.sakura` fallback and no deprecation path, because nobody uses the language yet. The compiler reads only `.sak`; a `.sakura` file named on the command line, or a tree whose only sources are `.sakura`, is a usage error (exit 2) naming the rename, and elsewhere `.sakura` files are skipped. The `sakura` binary, `sakura.toml`, `sakura.lock`, the `sakura:///std/…` URI scheme, the language id, the TextMate scope `source.sakura` and the `sakura` code-fence label do not change. Why (owner): `.sk` is taken by Skript, the Minecraft scripting language (SkriptLang/Skript, about 1.35k stars, about 130k installs across its VS Code extensions); `.sak` is used only by an old game data archive format (Postal) and is free in GitHub Linguist. Supersedes the extension clause of §17.47. Changed: §2 (source files), §9, §12.1, every path in this document. Impact: every `.sakura` file renamed with history; compiler discovery, the LSP, MCP, formatter, test runner, harnesses, the VS Code extension, the skill bundle, docs and the website follow; agent-DX task manifests move to revision 2 because their starter inventory changed. <!-- rename:keep -->

180. **Platforms B and C: the concrete rows, Linux ARM64 first, then Windows x64 and Intel macOS, with the portability decisions a code audit forces (owner's matrix §17.157 (2); §17.175 (7); ADR 0003; audit of 2026-10-18; round 14).** Question: the owner wants 1.0 to include a Raspberry Pi (Linux ARM64) and Windows x64 plus Intel macOS; the plan had one line per stage and no rows. A read of the compiler, runtime, packaging and workflows found that Linux ARM64 and Intel macOS have no code-level risk (no architecture or endianness assumptions, every binary encoding explicitly little-endian, `install.sh` already maps both) and that Windows has four blockers: the `file://` URI model (`disk::file_uri` writes `file://C:%5Cdir%5Csrc%5Cmain.sak`, and the module name, the `sakura.toml` lookup, the cwd strip, the LSP and MCP paths and git-base mode all parse URIs by `/`), solver lookup on `PATH` without `.exe`, CRLF (a `\r` stays inside comment tokens and multi-line strings, `fmt --check` and snapshot compares are byte-exact), and packaging with no `.exe`, no `.zip`, no Windows entry anywhere.
