Decision: (1) **Targets and inventory.** B adds `{"target": "aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu", "runner": "ubuntu-22.04-arm", "npm": "sakuralang-linux-arm64-gnu", "os": "linux", "cpu": "arm64", "libc": "glibc"}` to `packaging/targets.json`; C adds `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` (runner `windows-2025`, npm `sakuralang-win32-x64-msvc`, `os` `win32`, `cpu` `x64`, archive `.zip`, binary `sakura.exe`) and `x86_64-apple-darwin` (runner `macos-15-intel`, npm `sakuralang-darwin-x64`, `os` `darwin`, `cpu` `x64`). **32-bit `armv7` is out**: Cranelift has no 32-bit ARM backend, so the M5 native target could never follow and a JS-only tier would be permanently second class; GitHub hosts no armv7 runners; the Raspberry Pi target is 64-bit Raspberry Pi OS (bookworm, glibc 2.36). **musl stays out** (§17.157 (2)); the installer and launcher keep refusing it by name. (2) **Floors** (STABILITY.md, by Sol, once each stage's smoke test exists, §17.175 (5)): Linux glibc ≥ 2.35 on both CPUs (release builds on `ubuntu-22.04` and `ubuntu-22.04-arm`; CI may use `ubuntu-24.04-arm`); macOS ≥ 12.0 on both CPUs (`MACOSX_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=12.0`); Windows 10 x64 or later, the MSVC release built with `-C target-feature=+crt-static` so no Visual C++ redistributable is needed. Distributed binaries use the triple's baseline ISA, never `target-cpu=native`. (3) **Channels.** `install.sh` serves the three Unix targets as built (its Rosetta check already routes an Apple-silicon shell under Rosetta to arm64); a new `tools/install/install.ps1` serves Windows: pinned version, `Get-FileHash -Algorithm SHA256` against `SHA256SUMS` and failure without a sum, `Expand-Archive` into `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\sakuralang\bin`, no PATH or profile edits (the command is printed), unsupported CPUs refused by name; npm platform packages for all five targets, the launcher resolving `bin/sakura.exe` on `win32` and skipping the signal re-raise there; the Homebrew formula gains Intel macOS and stays macOS plus Linux x64 (Homebrew does not support Linux ARM64; the formula's refusal names the installer and npm); no bottles, the formula installs the release archive as today. (4) **Code decisions.** (a) **URIs**: `disk::file_uri` and `uri_path` produce and accept RFC 8089 on every OS (`file:///C:/dir/src/main.sak`, forward slashes, the drive letter kept, percent-encoding as today), the module name is derived from the path relative to `src/` and never from URI text, and every string test on a URI (`rfind("/src/")`, `ends_with("/sakura.toml")`, the cwd strip, the MCP prefix filter, git-base URIs) goes through that one helper. Unix output is unchanged and the existing snapshots pin it; the `--json` `file` field and the LSP and MCP URIs are therefore identical for the same project on every OS. (b) **Line endings** (§2): a line terminator inside a string literal denotes `\n` whatever the file's ending (Rust's rule), a comment never includes the `\r`, `sakura fmt` writes `\n` and reports a CRLF file as unformatted naming line endings as the cause, `sakura new` writes `.gitattributes` with `*.sak text eol=lf` and `*.snap text eol=lf` (withdrawn for `*.snap` by §17.196 (1): `*.snap -text`), and the test runner's snapshot mismatch names line endings when stripping `\r` would make the two equal. Fixtures: a CRLF semantics fixture (checked in with `-text`) whose stdout and trace equal its LF twin, a CRLF `fmt` case, both ABIs. (c) **Processes**: the Node lookup honours `PATHEXT` (`node.exe`, then `node.cmd` through Rust's batch spawning; `SAKURA_NODE` still wins); a `sakura run` or `test` child status whose code is outside `0..=255` (Windows NTSTATUS, for example Ctrl-C) exits `EXIT_DEFECT` like a Unix signal, with one stderr line carrying the raw code; permission grants name the path Node's loader will see: the real path on Unix, `std::path::absolute` on Windows (§17.186, which replaced this item's first wording); the solver lookup appends `EXE_SUFFIX`, and a solver the deadline killed is reported as killed on Windows too (a flag, not a signal). (d) **Stated limitations at C**: no proof cache on Windows (the key store is Unix-only; proofs rerun), native debugger symbols per §17.175 (7), `bench/` runs on the reference host only (`os.wait4`); each in STABILITY.md's target sentence and the install page. (5) **CI and release.** `ci.yml`'s rust and parity jobs add `ubuntu-24.04-arm` at B and `windows-2025` and `macos-15-intel` at C; the solver step gains the three `RUNNER_OS-RUNNER_ARCH` cases from the pinned z3 and cvc5 assets, and a lane whose pinned version has no asset builds it in a cached step rather than skipping verify tests; the tests the audit lists as `#[cfg(unix)]` stay skipped on Windows with that attribute, the symlink escape test in `m3.rs` is gated whole, and `agent_docs`' backslash rejection becomes separator normalisation. `release.yml`'s build steps run under `shell: bash`, the host-floor step gains a Windows case, and the release notes and `tools/install/test.sh` read `targets.json` instead of naming targets. The native CI guard and the `native` job stay platform A until M5 row B. (6) **Acceptance**, each stage on a fresh host by the owner (ADR 0003): **B** on a Raspberry Pi 4 or 5 with 64-bit Raspberry Pi OS: `install.sh`, `npm i -g sakuralang`, `sakura new cli` then `check`, `test`, `build`, `run`, `run --target wasm`, the MCP smoke of `release.py`; **C-Windows** on a fresh Windows 11 x64 machine: `install.ps1`, npm, the same list, `sakura fmt --check` clean on a fresh project under `core.autocrlf=true`, the VS Code extension's LSP, a Claude or Codex MCP configuration; **C-Intel** on a fresh Intel Mac: `install.sh`, `brew install`, npm, the same list. (7) **Order and owners.** The code items of (4) are portability fixes that need no release and may start now (Opus; the solver item after V4b lands, since V4b edits `sakura_verify`); B's packaging and CI (Sol) start when the owner has accepted `v0.1.0-alpha.1` on a fresh host with npm published (stage 1's evidence); C's (Sol: `install.ps1`, `.zip` and `.exe` in `release.py`, formula, launcher, workflows) after B is accepted; the install page per OS and the STABILITY sentences (Astra drafts, Sol lands) with each stage. Why: the matrix is the owner's; each stage's cost is now a list a fresh agent can execute, and the Windows code work is the long lead item, so it starts first. Rejected: cross-building B from x64 and smoking under QEMU (numbers forbidden by §17.175 (7); native ARM runners are free for public repositories); a universal macOS binary (two archives keep one inventory entry per target and the formula per CPU); an MSI or winget manifest (after C ships and a request exists); PATH edits by the installers (ADR 0003); a musl build labelled static. Performance: none. Changed: §2 (line terminators), plan `road-to-1.0.md` (stage 6 rows), plan `m5-native.md` §5 and rows B and C. Impact: Opus, Sol, Astra as named; the owner (acceptance on fresh hosts).

181. **WS-P2b answers: a step bound on resumable loops, identity-forwarding clauses dispatch outward directly (WS-P10), and one review packet for WS-P2 and WS-P2b (plan `performance-r1.md` §8 WS-P2b items 2, 5 and 9; round 14).** Decision: (1) **Bound: at most 8 CPS steps per loop body.** A join-point loop with more is emitted in the shared-continuation form, `--dump direct` names the reason `too-many-steps(n)`, and a `tests/semantics` fixture with nine steps pins the fallback with trace parity; `loop_resume_twice`'s six-step `spin` stays resumable (§17.190 (1); first written as five). The duplicated remainder is linear in the step count, so the bound caps the worst case at nine copies of one body; no corpus or benchmark loop has more than five, and plan §5 item 4's 2× module budget on `micro.sak` remains the size gate. A step count rather than a byte budget because eligibility must not change with unrelated edits to the body and must be readable in the source. Opus, one function in `emit.rs`, now, before M4-OPT starts. (2) **WS-P10, direct forwarding.** A clause is an *identity forwarder* when its body is exactly one perform of the same operation of the same effect with the clause's parameters in order and nothing else (no state read or write, no other expression, no `fail`), and the handler's type is `Handler<E, {E}>`. Core marks the clause `forwards` on its `MakeHandler`; the runtime's `find` skips an instance whose clause for the operation forwards **when tracing is off**, so the perform reaches the next instance outward in one walk and, when that clause has a twin, takes the tail-direct path of §17.113 (b). With tracing on, dispatch runs the clause as today, so the two §17.80 records (inner and outer) are produced unchanged and trace parity holds by construction. Cancellation: the outer dispatch keeps its test before the clause, and the forwarder's own test read the same flag at the same moment (a task is cancelled only while suspended, §17.156 (1)), so nothing observable is lost. Not eligible: `Http.scoped` (it checks first), a forwarder that renames, reorders or adds arguments, one that performs another effect or sits inside `try`, and any clause with state. Acceptance: `handled_fwd` advisory toward the `handled` band (9.0× / 7.4× on the noisy run, §17.183); fixtures: a forwarder nested in a forwarder, a forwarder under `--trace` byte-identical to today, cancellation through a forwarder on `handler_cancel_bound`'s pattern, each ineligible shape above shown unmarked in `--dump core`, `million_operations` and the nested-`with` case unchanged, `--no-direct` and WASM parity; the WASM runtime's `find` gets the same flag, and the ABI text lands with N0b's 1.3.0 because M4-OPT owns 1.2.0. Owner Opus, after M4-OPT (both touch `runtime/js` dispatch and the ABI): `sakura_core` (the flag, `--dump core`), `runtime/js` and `runtime/rt` (`find`), fixtures. Rejected: calling the outer clause directly from the perform site (the outer instance is dynamic); erasing `Handler<E, {E}>` forwarders in Core (the instance must exist for `with` nesting and for tracing). (3) **Review packet.** Opus writes `docs/reviews/ws-p2-handlers.md`: what WS-P2 and WS-P2b changed in where cancellation-point and trace code runs, the invariants (§17.113's list, §17.156 (1)) and the fixture that pins each; the owner's sign-off there closes plan §5 item 6 for both; the packet changes no code. Why: one mechanism, one review. Performance: WS-P10 is the remaining lever on `handled_fwd`; it removes the clause's `find`, `cctx` and CPS hop per iteration. Changed: plan `performance-r1.md` §9 (WS-P10 row; items 2, 5 and 9 of §8 WS-P2b point here), §5 item 6 (the packet). Impact: Opus (bound now; packet now; WS-P10 after M4-OPT), the owner (review).

182. **Agent-DX answers: a 7,500-byte bar on `SKILL.md`, a prefix rule for `V1002`, `V2002` names the callee as written, and the next eval is a paired three-task run inside the remaining budget (dx5msg findings; §17.165; round 14).** Decision: (1) **`SKILL.md` is at most 7,500 bytes of ASCII, pinned by a test** in `sakura_cli` (the crate that embeds it); the budget policy (measured on the filled text, a 7,000-byte source target) is §17.188. The harness's 8,000-character tool-output cut is one of many such cuts; the bar is the file's, with headroom for the metadata fill (a 40-character commit replaces a 20-character placeholder). Cuts, in order, until under the bar: the provenance paragraph of the opening section becomes one sentence ("The CLI fills the two `sakura-compiler-*` fields; if they do not match `sakura --version`, trust the installed compiler's `prompt`, `doc` and `explain`") with the rest moved to the head of `references/repairs.md`; the `audit`/`verify` paragraph under "Completion and deeper lookup" becomes one sentence pointing at `references/recipes.md`; the "Load references selectively" paragraph loses its bundle history. The four examples, the edit loop and the syntax list stay: the skill condition's +3/12 came from them. (2) **Prefix rule for `V1002`'s `Type.member` form**: when neither the proportional did-you-mean nor `only_within(…, 2)` finds a candidate and exactly one exported function of at least three characters is a prefix of the typed member or has it as a prefix (`List.lenght` → `len`, `List.fil` → `filter`), suggest it with a `suggested` fix, never `machine_applicable` (a prefix is weaker evidence than two edits); the folding of extensions and the fallback's withdrawal are §17.187. Nothing for `List.size` or `List.count`; `explain V1002` gains one sentence naming the std convention the pilot tripped on (`len`, not `length` or `size`). (3) **`V2002` names the callee as written**: a `Type.member` call reads `` `List.map` (std/list) takes 2 arguments, this call passes 1 `` rather than the bare def name; `expected`/`actual` unchanged; message text only, fixture updated. (4) **Next eval run.** `runner.py` replaces the all-runs `MAX_LIVE_EPISODES` constant with `--max-episodes` (default 48, per approved run; the committed dollar ledger stays the only hard rail across runs); `agent-eval.yml` gains `input_caps` (default `40000,60000`), `seed` and `max_episodes` inputs; the harness changes of §17.165 (3) land before any dispatch. Design: tasks 03, 04 and 10 (the pilot's cap-censored failures) × 2 conditions × 2 models × 2 caps = 24 episodes per seed; worst case per seed $5.04 (per episode at the 4,000-token output cap: Opus $0.24 at 40k and $0.32 at 60k, Sonnet $0.12 and $0.16), inside the $5.80 remaining, so seed 1 needs only a fresh key and the owner's written approval; seed 2 (§17.165 (3)'s second order) needs the owner to raise the eval total, which this entry does not do. Why: the paired arms answer whether the cap censors the failures, and those three tasks are where it did. Rejected: raising the dollar cap (the owner's); the full 96-episode design ($20.16 worst case per seed); the six-task subset ($10.08 worst case against $5.80: the ledger would stop mid-design and break the pairing). Changed: plan `agent-dx.md` §4 DX5. Impact: Opus (1–4), Astra (the `SKILL.md` and `explain` wording review, docs), the owner (key, approval, any raise).

183. **Performance gaps after the noisy run of 2026-10-18 (`bench/target/noisy-1-quiet.json`, load 4.06, indicative only): ranked, with the work item that closes each (round 14).** Numbers are the geometric mean over the four sizes of the Sakura/hand-JS median ratio, Node / Bun, at `397f4cc6`; none is a ceiling verdict (§17.153). Suite median of the fifteen family ratios: 1.81 Node, 2.38 Bun (plan §5 item 1 asks ≤ 1.5). Ranked by distance over the §17.109 ceiling and by generality: **1. `deltablue` 72× / 72× (ceiling 2.0)**, the one outlier, in no gap list until now. A Node `--cpu-prof` at n=1000 this round: `hget` 15.6%, generic `eq` 12.7%, `setAt` 8.3%, GC 6.4%, `hashOf` 3.9%, `mapFind` 3.2%. The `eq` time is seven `$rt.eq(dir, v)` sites comparing the nullary enumeration `Dir`: **WS-P11a** (Opus, small, after WS-P9 lands and before M4-OPT): equality whose static type is an enumeration of nullary constructors, or where either side is a nullary-constructor literal, lowers to a tag compare in both emitters, shown in `--dump core`, fixtures under both ABIs and on WASM, semantics unchanged. The `Map<Int, _>` path (`hashOf` dispatch, `hget`, `popcount` per probe) is **WS-P11b** (Opus, after WS-P11a): a key kind recorded per map at construction so `Int` keys skip the type dispatch and the generic leaf compare, measured on `map_set:int` and `deltablue`, every `map_set` fixture and the WASM runtime's `map.rs` mirror unchanged in semantics. The persistent record threading (`Planner { ..p, vars: … }` on a parameter) is M5's N3 and N4, not a JS item (§17.149's facts stop at `origin`). Precondition (Sol, now): the Sakura port's projection checks 3 and 4 (`compiler/REQUESTS.md`, WS-S 2026-10-18), so the lanes do equal work before any ratio is read; a benchmark-definition boundary row in `bench/deltas.md` (§17.150). **2. `handled_fwd` 9.0× / 7.4× (band 2.0)**: WS-P10 (§17.181 (2)). **3. `nbody` 12.2× / 10.4× (1.3, carried)**: representation, §17.177 (1)–(2); no JS item; the first native window reads it. **4. `map_set:int` 3.7× / 13.0× (3.0 / 5.0 provisional)**: WS-P11b; `:string` 1.4× / 2.0× and `:tuple` 2.0× / 4.5× are read at the window. **5. `countdown` 1.8× / 1.9× (1.3)**: WS-P9 (decided, §17.160 (5)). **6. `handled_reentry` 0.9× / 6.0×**: Bun only; the callback re-entry is CPS by design (§17.113); Astra profiles it on Bun before any item is named. **7. `spectral_norm` 4.6× / 2.1× (1.3, carried)**: perf-gaps-r2 rank 5, representation round. **8. A Bun tax on direct code that Node does not pay**: `json_validate` 1.4× / 3.4× (2.0), `rbtree` 2.2× / 3.3× (1.5), `binary_trees` 1.8× / 2.8× (1.5), `fib` 1.5× / 2.4× (1.5): **WS-P12** (Astra, beside the next quiet window): attribute it on Bun with gauge-free and check-free diagnostic builds of `fib` and `binary_trees` (diagnostic only, never a baseline, never in the harness), then a decision; no code before the attribution. **9. `contracts_plain` 2.6× / 1.9× (1.3)** and `contracts` 2.2× / 1.7× (2.0): Node over its ceiling on the contract-free companion, whose emitted module has no site or bracket code (plan §8 WS-P5); joins WS-P12's attribution on Node. **10. `sieve` 2.8× / 3.7× (1.5, carried)**: down from the carry's 5.2× / 6.65× after WS-P7; representation round. **11. `pipeline` 0.24× / 2.4× (2.0)**: Bun 18% over; read at the window. Within ceiling on both engines: `handled` d1/d4/d16 at about 1.0×, `handled_suspend` 1.9× / 0.3×, `shapes` 1.4× / 1.4×, `tokenizer` 0.3× / 1.1×. Why: the suite's median is set by a handful of programs with named causes, and two of them (`deltablue`'s enumeration compare, `handled_fwd`'s forwarder) are small lowering changes rather than representation questions. Rejected: ranking by the noisy run's absolute ratios without the profile (load moves ratios by tens of percent, not by 70×); any representation change before the §17.177 windows. Performance: measured advisory per row, judged at the quiet window. Changed: plan `performance-r1.md` §9 (rows WS-P11a, WS-P11b, WS-P12). Impact: Opus, Sol, Astra as named.

184. **Order after round 14.** Now, in parallel with §17.166's queue and touching none of its files: the WS-P2b step bound and the WS-P2 review packet (Opus, §17.181 (1), (3)); the platform code items of §17.180 (4a)–(4c) except the solver lookup (Opus); the `deltablue` projection checks (Sol, §17.183); `SKILL.md`'s bar and cuts, the `V1002` prefix rule, the `V2002` callee name, and the eval runner and workflow inputs (Opus, §17.182). After WS-P9 lands: WS-P11a, then WS-P11b (Opus). After V4b lands: the solver lookup item (Opus). After M4-OPT lands: WS-P10 (Opus). When the owner has accepted `v0.1.0-alpha.1` on a fresh host with npm published: platform B's packaging and CI (Sol), the install page per OS (Astra). After B is accepted: platform C's packaging (Sol). Beside the next quiet window: WS-P12 and the `handled_reentry` Bun profile (Astra). Owner-only: the fresh key and written approval for eval seed 1 (§17.182 (4)); acceptance of B and C on fresh hosts; the WS-P2 review sign-off. Nothing here waits on the M4 exit gate, and nothing here edits `opt.rs`, `emit.rs` beyond the one-function bound, or the ABI text M4-OPT owns.

185. **V4b readings ratified: manifest schema `1.1` with `target`, `1.1` on every `sakura verify` record, and Z3's `canceled` classified by elapsed time (plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §7 V4b items 2, 4 and 5; commits `61dd8a6`, `092ca9f`; round 15).** Decision: (1) **Manifest `1.1` and `target`.** Adding `target` (`js` | `wasm`) to `target/sakura/manifest.json` is a MINOR change under §12.2's rule, so both targets write `schema_version: "1.1"`; a consumer that read `1.0` reads `1.1` unchanged and learns which profile the statuses hold for. §17.76's shape is amended in place; the website's manifest page already says so. (2) **`1.1` on every verify record.** "Records written by `sakura verify`" (§17.164 (2)) means every line of one invocation, the `W7002` `diagnostic` records included: a stream whose records disagree on their version would force a consumer to check each line. §12.2 now states the invariant, and the test helper's rule (major `1`, one version per output) is its pin; `sakura check`'s diagnostics stay `1.0`. (3) **`canceled`.** Z3 reports `canceled` for both the resource limit and its own timeout. The worker's elapsed time is measured around the whole process, so it is never less than Z3's own clock: a `canceled` that arrives before 90 % of `timeout_ms` cannot be Z3's timeout and is `resource_limit` (cached, §17.164 (1)); one at or after that mark is `timeout` and is not cached. The 10 % margin covers clock granularity, and misreads fall only on the uncached side: a resource limit reached late on a loaded machine is solved again next run, which costs time and never trust. If a misclassification is ever observed, the precise rule is Z3's `(get-info :rlimit)` compared with the limit sent, not a wider margin. Why: each reading follows a rule already in the text (§12.2's MINOR rule, §17.164's "records written by `sakura verify`", §17.106's determinism of `rlimit`); none changes an interface beyond what §17.164 announced. Rejected: keeping the manifest at `1.0` with a new field (a consumer could not tell the two shapes apart by version); `1.0` on `W7002` lines inside a `1.1` stream; caching every `canceled` (a cached timeout would be a clock in the key). Performance: none beyond §17.164's. Changed: §17.76 (shape, amendment note), §12.2 (one version per invocation). Impact: none; plan §7 V4b items 2, 4 and 5 point here.

186. **Node permission grants name the real path on Unix and the absolute path on Windows; §17.180 (4c)'s "never `canonicalize`" is withdrawn (plan `road-to-1.0.md` §6 P2 deviation; commit `2224295`; round 15).** Facts (Opus, Node 26.11): the loader resolves the entry script's real path before the permission check, so a grant of `/tmp/x` is refused while `/private/tmp/x` is accepted; macOS's `/tmp` and `$TMPDIR` are symlinks, and the MCP `test` tool builds into `$TMPDIR`. Node's own fs checks compare resolved strings, not real paths, so the snapshot and fixture grants, which are passed to the driver as the same strings, need no canonicalisation. On Windows `canonicalize` yields a `\\?\`-prefixed path that Node's realpath never produces. Decision: `grant_root` is the real path where `canonicalize` succeeds on Unix and `std::path::absolute` on Windows and on any Unix failure, as landed; the grant and the entry argument derive from that one value. If C's acceptance (§17.180 (6)) shows a junction-backed project directory refused on Windows, the fix is to strip the verbatim prefix from `canonicalize`'s output in the same function, not a dependency and not a flag. Rejected: `--preserve-symlinks-main` so that `absolute` suffices everywhere (a resolution-semantics flag to carry for one grant, when the real path is the plainer invariant); `std::path::absolute` on Unix as §17.180 (4c) first said (refused by Node whenever the output directory sits behind a symlink). Follow-up (Opus, small): one `#[cfg(unix)]` test in `sakura_cli/tests/cli.rs` that runs `sakura test` with a snapshot write from a project whose path crosses a symlinked directory, pinning the loader rule and the snapshot grant together; nothing else changes. Why: Node's loader, not the design, decides what a grant must name. Performance: one `realpath` per test run. Changed: §17.180 (4c) (pointer), plan `road-to-1.0.md` P2 row (pointer). Impact: Opus (the one test).

187. **`V1002`'s `Type.member` suggestions: the prefix rule as built is ratified and §17.165 (2)'s flat two-edit fallback is withdrawn (commit `cad94591`; `List.size` → `List.slice`; round 16).** Facts: the proportional did-you-mean allows ⌊n/3⌋ edits and at least one, so a name of six or more characters already admits two; the flat `only_within(…, 2)` fallback therefore adds candidates only for names of five characters or fewer at distance exactly two, where two edits are nearly half the name, and it adds them as `machine_applicable`: `List.size` → `List.slice`, which an agent applies unread. Decision: (1) **Prefix rule, as built.** Among exported functions of at least three characters that are a prefix of the typed member or extend it: when every match extends the typed member, the shortest is suggested and the others count as extending it (`fil` → `filter` although `filter_map` matches too; `so` → `sort`); when matches lie on both sides of the typed member (`map_e`: `map` is a prefix of it, `map_err` extends it), nothing is suggested, since the user may have been typing past the shorter one; `suggested`, never `machine_applicable`. §17.182 (2)'s "exactly one" reads "exactly one after that folding". (2) **The two-edit fallback goes.** The `Type.member` form uses the proportional did-you-mean (`machine_applicable`, as the effect form does) and then the prefix rule (`suggested`); `only_within` is deleted with its one call. `lenth` → `len` moves from `machine_applicable` to `suggested` (a two-edit deletion is a prefix match); `List.size`, `List.count` and `Result.map_e` get nothing; `lenght`, `length` and `fil` are unchanged. Opus, small: `sakura_hir/src/{body,diag}.rs`, the `V1002_module_function_did_you_mean` fixture (applicability), `List.size` added to `V1002_module_function_no_near_candidate`, the CHANGELOG's Unreleased `V1002` line and the "within two edits" wording in `skills/sakura/references/diagnostics.md` and the `V1002` page (regenerated). Why: a wrong `machine_applicable` fix costs more than a missing suggestion, and the prefix rule now covers the case the fallback was written for (`lenth`). Rejected: keeping the fallback behind a minimum name length (a second threshold for one example); scaling the fallback (that is the proportional rule); making it `suggested` (two edits in a four-letter name is not evidence at all). Performance: none. Changed: §17.165 (2) (pointer), §17.182 (2) (pointer), plan `agent-dx.md` §4 DX5 round 14 item 2 (pointer). Impact: Opus (small).

188. **`SKILL.md` budget: the 7,500-byte bar is measured on the text an agent receives, the source keeps a 7,000-byte working target, and whoever crosses the target moves text in the same commit (commit `f93e8adb`: 7,494 bytes, six of headroom; round 16).** Facts: the `sakura_cli` test measures the embedded source; the CLI's fill replaces `@SAKURA_COMPILER_VERSION@` with the version and `@SAKURA_COMPILER_GIT@` with a 40-character commit, so a release binary hands out about seven bytes more than the source, and today's 7,494 is 7,501 there. Decision: (1) **Units.** Bytes, because the file and the test count bytes; the ASCII assertion makes bytes and characters equal, so the harness's 8,000-character cut is honoured by construction, and a non-ASCII character fails the test rather than the budget. (2) **What is measured.** The test applies `fill_skill_metadata` with the package version and a 40-character commit to the source and asserts `<= 7500`; the raw source is not the artefact. (3) **Target and ownership.** The source stays at or under 7,000 bytes. A change that would cross 7,000 moves text to `references/` in the same commit; the author of that change owns the move, never the next author. The test enforces the bar only; the target is this rule, and the test's failure message names both numbers. (4) **What lives in `SKILL.md`**: the frontmatter, the opening paragraph (run, retrieve, trust the installed compiler), the syntax list (six bullets), the edit loop (six steps with their commands), the four examples with their code unchanged, and one pointer paragraph each for repairs, completion and the references. Everything else, history, rationale, a caveat longer than one sentence, MCP detail beyond what the loop needs, lives in `references/`. (5) **Trim now** (Sol; Astra reviews the wording, as in §17.182 (1)): at least 500 bytes, prose only, in this order until the source is under 7,000: the examples' introduction to one sentence ("Each block is a separate file; check it with `sakura check --json <file>`, then run `sakura test --json <file>`; no test uses a live service."); the first paragraph of "Completion and deeper lookup" to its first sentence plus "Report missing prerequisites and failing checks; never claim a skipped test passed."; step 4's MCP sentences to one ("With MCP, check `interface_delta` and `frozen_spec_violations`, pass `frozen_specs: "all"` to both `check_patch` and `apply_patch`, and apply against the original base snapshot; results are objects, not JSON Lines."); the opening paragraph's tool-result sentence to "Every tool result stays in the conversation; do not reload guides." The sentences that leave go to the head of `references/repairs.md` (the MCP detail) and `references/recipes.md` (the completion detail); the generator's mirror, ZIP, checksum and manifest follow in the same commit. Why: six bytes of headroom is a trap for the next author, and the fill is what the agent reads, so the fill must be inside the bar. Rejected: raising the bar (the harness cut is fixed); counting characters (identical under ASCII, and bytes are what the file has); moving an example out (§17.182 (1): the examples carried the skill condition's gain); a second test for the target (a target is advice, a bar is a contract). Performance: none. Changed: §17.182 (1) (pointer), plan `agent-dx.md` §4 DX5 round 14 item 1 (the target, the measured text, the trim). Impact: Sol (trim, test), Astra (wording review).

189. **L5 readings ratified: ABI 1.1.3 is a PATCH, site groups are the site word, defect messages carry program data in a task-owned string, and function and block sites stay deferred (REQUESTS 2026-10-18 L5; plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.8 row 5; round 16).** Decision: (1) **1.1.3 is a PATCH**: §12's layout, the outcome's `site` word and `rt_raise`'s `site` argument existed before L5, so nothing shipped changes shape and `sakura_abi_version` stays `0x010100`; the erratum states role `u8` 0/1, site groups, zero hashes and the host's rendering, and the kind list of §17.161 still goes into 1.2.0. (2) **Site groups**: one `u32` site per contract the callee blames with, carried as a trailing `i64` parameter of a top-level def whose `requires` or `where` blames its caller, none on entry `main` and lambdas, adapters passing 0 and `SITE_MARK` rebinding it; endorsed, with the LIR validator's rule and `a_wrong_defect_site_or_message_is_rejected` as the pins. (3) **Program data in messages** lives in a `String` the task owns (`Option.expect`'s text, `D004`'s value, `D031`'s offset, `D011`'s inner code and message); endorsed, so `defect_*` fixtures have no backend variants. (4) **`D09x`** is covered by the shared status-2 path on both backends; `D090` texts of runtime invariants (a compiler or runtime bug) stay out of scope, as L5 said. (5) **Deferred, named**: function and block sites in `sakura.debug` (logical stack traces) and the module and content hashes (zero today) wait for a consumer; the first candidates are M5 N2's debug work (the `gimli` line table, where a function site is needed anyway) and the playground; until then the erratum's "zero, stated" is the contract. Why: each reading follows a rule already in the text (§17.129's PATCH rule, §17.162's three fields), and M4 exit needs the three fields, not traces. Rejected: a MINOR bump for a filled section (nothing a 1.1.2 consumer reads changed); a second site word per call (a group is one word); hashes before a consumer. Performance: none. Changed: plan §3.8 row 5 (pointer). Impact: none now; N2 inherits item (5).

190. **Corrections (round 16).** (1) `loop_resume_twice`'s `spin` has **six** CPS steps, not five: two `Counter.next`, two `Clock.sleep`, `Clock.now` and `Console.print`; it emits six `$sN` functions and stays `resumable` under the bound of 8. §17.181 (1), plan `performance-r1.md` §8 WS-P2b deviation 2 and the §9 bound row are corrected; the review packet already counted six. The corpus loop with the most steps is therefore six, and the bound's headroom is two steps, not three. (2) Plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.8's state line still read "after gate 10" and named WS12 items as in flight; it now states the state after round 15, and row 4's "`List.lenght` still gets no suggestion" note is superseded by §17.182 (2) and §17.187. Changed: §17.181 (1), plan `performance-r1.md` §8 item 2 and §9, plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.8. Impact: none.

191. **Order after round 16: what is unblocked now, with files, so parallel agents do not collide (plans `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.8, `performance-r1.md` §9, `m5-native.md` §2, `agent-dx.md`, `road-to-1.0.md` §6; `git log origin/main` at `665fa987`; round 16).** State: on `main` are WS-P2b and its bound, the WS-P2 review packet, L5, V4b, WS12 items 1–8, N0a, P2, the DX5 message and harness items, and the `deltablue` projection checks; `v0.1.0-alpha.1` is a GitHub prerelease with the Homebrew formula published, and its npm job failed (a token without the 2FA bypass, and a path bug that `f5df9861` fixed after the tag), so npm is the owner's to redo. In flight, with their files: WS-P9 (Opus; `sakura_core/src/opt.rs`, `sakura_lir/src/lower.rs`; landed as `ad6e2e19` while this round was written, readings in §17.192), platform P0 and P1 (Opus; `disk.rs` and the URI helpers in `sakura_cli`, `sakura_lsp`, `sakura_mcp`; the lexer, `fmt`, `new.rs`, CRLF fixtures), CI nextest (Sol; `ci.yml`), the slow-test diagnosis (Opus; test files only). Decisions: (1) **M4-OPT starts now, not after WS-P9.** Its files (`sakura_lir/src/layout.rs`, `sakura_core/src/opt/proved.rs`, `sakura_codegen_wasm`, `runtime/rt` `stats()`, `ABI.md`, the nested-option corpus) are disjoint from WS-P9's two; only the §17.118 bar on `countdown` is read after WS-P9 lands, and `fib` and `nbody` need not wait. **Its first commit is ABI 1.2.0's text with the `restarts` counter**: kinds 5–7 in `retire_handle`, the W1/W2/L4 imports listed, §5's fallback wording, `sakura run --stats` deleted and `SAKURA_WASM_STATS` named the interface (§17.160 (2), §17.161); that commit alone meets N1a's "1.2.0 text on `main`" condition. (2) **The WASM bench lane is built now**; its numbers wait. Opus (WS-B): `bench/harness` gains a `wasm` suite over the Node host and, for the pure corpus, Wasmtime through the WASI command embedding (plan §7 deviation 8), copying the last `SAKURA_WASM_STATS` line's counters into each record (§17.118); Sol: the Wasmtime pin in `bench/toolchains.toml` and the `bench.yml` nightly. No WASM number enters `bench/deltas.md` or a baseline before M4-OPT has landed and the owner has announced a quiet window (§17.153); the lane's acceptance is fixtures (a record carries the counters, a loaded host is `quiet: false`, the Wasmtime cell is absent for an impure program) and one advisory run posted as such. (3) **N1a** (Opus; `runtime/rt/src/native.rs` new, `mem.rs`, `sakura_host_native`) starts when (1)'s first commit is on `main`; N0b and N5 wait for M4-OPT whole, as planned, because they recompute its `owned` facts and read its counters. (4) **WS-P11a** after WS-P9 as decided, in parallel with M4-OPT: a Core peephole in a new `sakura_core/src/opt/` file plus one builtin in both emitters (`emit.rs`, the WASM builtin table and `builtins.sigs`), none of them an M4-OPT file; WS-P11b after it; WS-P10 after M4-OPT. (5) **Small Opus items now**, each one commit, none touching the files above: §17.187 (`sakura_hir`, two fixtures, CHANGELOG line, generated diagnostics text); §17.186's symlinked-project test (`sakura_cli/tests/cli.rs`, `#[cfg(unix)]`); the tree-sitter grammar of §17.148 (`editors/tree-sitter-sakura/`, new; a corpus generated from `compiler/tests/` fixtures; acceptance: every fixture parses with no `ERROR` node and the corpus's node spans agree with the CST's token spans on the same fixtures; no publication); `std/http.sak` `read_body`'s `List.concat` under `--record` (a growing buffer; the std owner's note of gate 10; measured linear, not quadratic, and replaced by a 16 MiB limit in §17.205). (6) **Sol now**: §17.188's trim and test (`skills/sakura/SKILL.md`, two references, the generated mirror, `sakura_cli/src/main.rs` test); the lane's pin and nightly of (2); after nextest lands, §17.180 (5)'s `agent_docs` separator normalisation (a Windows test item, `compiler/tools/agent_docs`, its test). (7) **Astra**: beside the next quiet window, WS-P12 and the `handled_reentry` Bun profile (§17.183); the install page per OS waits for P3. (8) **Waiting, each with its event**: P3 and DX8's plugin on the owner's npm publish of alpha.1 (a token with the 2FA bypass, then a re-run of the tag's npm job or a `workflow_dispatch` from the verified tarballs; §17.166 (9)'s "stable for one release" is read from that publish); the playground, N0b, N5 and WS-P10 on M4-OPT; WS-P11b on WS-P11a; N2 and N1b on N1a; P5–P8 on B's acceptance; the JS quiet window on an idle machine; eval seed 1 on a fresh key and written approval (§17.182 (4)). **Ranked hand-out**: 1 M4-OPT (Opus), 1a the deep-IR guard and `V4004` of §17.193 (Opus, one commit, before WS-P11a), 2 the WASM lane (Opus WS-B, Sol), 3 the §17.188 trim (Sol), 4 §17.187 (Opus, small), 5 the tree-sitter grammar (Opus), 6 the symlink test (Opus, small), 7 `read_body` (Opus, small), 8 WS-P11a now, WS-P9 having landed (Opus), 9 N1a the moment the 1.2.0 text lands (Opus), 10 the `agent_docs` normalisation after nextest (Sol), 11 WS-P9b after WS-P11a and the WS-P9 packet now (Opus, §17.192). Why: M4-OPT is the long pole of M4 exit and of M5's N0b, N1a and N5, and nothing in it waits on WS-P9's two files; the lane's code and its numbers were one row and are two activities with different preconditions. Rejected: starting N0b against ABI 1.1.3 (it keys records by `abi_version` and recomputes facts M4-OPT changes); DX8's plugin before the npm publish (the plugin's condition is a release agents can install); any representation work (§17.177). Performance: none directly; M4-OPT and WS-P11a are the next measured items. Changed: plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.8 (state line, rows 6, 7 and 9), plan `performance-r1.md` §9 (order after round 16), plan `m5-native.md` §2 N1a and its start conditions, plan `agent-dx.md` §4. Impact: Opus, Sol, Astra as named; the owner (npm publish, quiet window, key).

192. **WS-P9 readings: one loop per member with nested copies is endorsed in place of §17.160 (5)'s merged def; cycles with no nesting order get a dispatch join as WS-P9b; the review packet is owed (commit `ad6e2e19`; plan `performance-r1.md` §8 WS-P9 items 1–8; round 16).** Facts: each member keeps its def and signature and hosts a self-tail loop in which the other members' bodies are join points, so no `DefId`, export or ABI entry is added, `--dump direct`, the snapshot and both backends see ordinary Core, and the LIR mirrors it with no change (§17.118 parity by construction); the cost is k² bodies for a cycle of k, bounded by `MERGE_MEMBERS` = 4 and `MERGE_NODES` = 1,024 copied expressions per def; eligibility is syntactic in Core (monomorphic top-level defs, one return type, tail calls outside `Handle` and `Try`), effectful members included; a def with a contract check is never copied but may host, and the copied call binds `$site` at its position so blame is the call's; a body sees only the labels of the members enclosing it and of the next one, so an order is searched and a three-def clique (each calling both others) stays unmerged and runs through frames on WASM. Decision: (1) **Endorsed as built.** The merged def with a dispatch parameter that §17.160 (5) named was one way to the constant-stack property, and this way reaches it with the stronger invariants: no new symbol, no ABI text, blame and traces unchanged by construction, the two limits readable in the source. The syntactic eligibility is also endorsed: the direct facts live in the JS emitter and Core cannot see them, and an effectful member's loop performs from the loop body as a self tail loop already did. (2) **WS-P9b, the dispatch join** (Opus, after WS-P11a; `sakura_core/src/opt.rs` only, no emitter or LIR change): a cycle within both limits for which `nest` finds no order is merged with one join per host whose body is a `Switch` on an `Int` state local, one case per member body, (as built, an `if` chain on the state, since Core's `Switch` has no literal form; §17.208 (1)) parameters passed as the join's arguments in a fixed order (the union of the members' parameter lists, one slot per member parameter); a self tail jump stays a direct jump, and only a cross-member jump pays the switch. The same two limits apply, counting the switch once. Acceptance: a three-def clique at 10^6 (each member calling both others in tail position) runs in constant stack under both ABIs and on WASM with `frames` small on the benchmark counters, traces equal with `--trace`, a contract-checking member hosting with the violating iteration blamed at the member call (the `requires_in_a_mutual_tail_loop_blames_the_member_call` pattern), the five-def cycle still unmerged, `--dump direct` parity, `mutual_tail_calls_share_one_loop` and `mutual_tail_loop` unchanged. Why: three-state machines whose states each reach the others (a tokenizer, a protocol) are the shape agents write, and the nesting form cannot take them. (3) **The review packet** (Opus, now, no code): `docs/reviews/ws-p9-tail-loops.md` as `ws-p2-handlers.md` is: what moved (blame sites set inside copied bodies, copied code under a different interval context), the invariants (blame names the call as written, `--trace` records equal, a contract-checking def is never copied, the limits), and the fixture that pins each; the owner's sign-off there closes plan §5 item 6 for WS-P9, with the review line added to that item. Nothing waits on the sign-off; the work is merged and pinned. (4) The §5 item 4 code-size rule (`micro.sak` at most 2×, `bench compile` within budget) is the gate on the k² copies; the two limits are not re-tuned without a program that trips them. Why: the invariants are stronger than the plan's mechanism; the gap has a bounded fix in one file. Rejected: the merged def with a dispatch parameter now (a new symbol and ABI entry for a gap WS-P9b closes in Core); raising `MERGE_MEMBERS` for the clique (the clique fails on order, not size); a review by the implementer's own packet alone (the sign-off is the owner's, plan §5 item 6). Performance: `countdown` 1.91× → 1.36× Node and 1.34× → 0.91× Bun advisory at load 16; read at the quiet window with §17.118's bar. Changed: plan `performance-r1.md` §9 (WS-P9 row pointer; WS-P9b row), §5 item 6 (the WS-P9 packet). Impact: Opus (WS-P9b, the packet), the owner (sign-off).

193. **Deep intermediate representations: every recursive walk runs under `sakura_hir::ensure_stack`, a sequence in the source never bounds a pass's recursion, and a `match`'s decision tree has an explicit node budget (`V4004`); a debug build overflowed on a 3,000-arm `match` in `lower/matchc.rs` (WS-P9's finding; round 16).** Facts: the parser bounds nesting at 128 (`V0008`, `MAX_DEPTH`), so user-written depth is bounded; `sakura_hir` grows the stack on the heap through `ensure_stack` (`stacker::maybe_grow`, 100 KiB red zone, 1 MiB segments) and states that every recursive walk goes through it; `sakura_core_snapshot` deserialises through `serde_stacker`. The match compiler's `split_lit` recurses on the rows that do not match the literal, so a flat `match` of n literal arms, which no nesting limit touches, recurses n deep and emits an `If` chain n deep; every later pass (`opt`, `owned`, `ranges`, `proved`, the JS emitter and `direct`, the LIR lowering and validator, the evaluator, the WASM emitter) walks that chain recursively and would overflow on the same program in a debug build or on a smaller thread. No source-size limit exists. Decision: (1) **Rule.** A pass's recursion depth is bounded by source nesting, never by the length of a sequence (arms, `let`s, list elements, operands, fields); where a representation puts a sequence on a spine (`Let` and `If` chains, `LetJoin`), the walk goes through `sakura_hir::ensure_stack` at its recursive entry, as `sakura_hir` already does; a crate adds `sakura_hir` as a dependency for it rather than `stacker` of its own. No arm, `let` or element count is limited: a generated lookup table is a legitimate program. (2) **Audit and guard** (Opus, one mechanical commit, now, before WS-P11a and ahead of M4-OPT's emission changes so that both rebase over it): every recursive function over `hir::Expr`, Core `Expr` or LIR blocks in `sakura_core` (`lower/`, `opt.rs`, `opt/*.rs`), `sakura_codegen_js` (`emit.rs`, `direct.rs`), `sakura_lir` (`lower.rs`, the validator), `sakura_codegen_wasm`, `sakura_eval` and `tools/sakura_eval` wrapped at its entry; a one-line comment at each naming this entry. (3) **Regression test** (same commit, debug profile as `cargo test` runs it): a generated program with a 3,000-arm `Int` literal `match`, a 3,000-arm `String` literal `match`, a 3,000-constructor enumeration matched exhaustively, and a 3,000-`let` body, taken through `check --json`, `build` for both targets, `--dump core`, a snapshot round trip and `run` with the expected output; the fixture is generated by the test, not checked in at that size; `tools/sakura_eval`'s generator gains a long-chain case. (4) **Explicit limit: `V4004 match_too_large`** (PATTERN phase, an additive registry entry with its explain text and generated page): the decision tree of one `match` may have at most 1,048,576 nodes (tests and leaves); `matchc` counts as it builds and stops at the limit, so a pathological tree never runs away in memory or time; the message names the count reached and the limit and suggests splitting the `match` or moving wildcard rows first; `severity` error, the primary span the `match` keyword. Fixture: a tuple of five `Int` columns each matched by or-patterns of twenty literals with wildcard rows between them (row duplication across splits exceeds the budget); negative: the 3,000-arm matches of (3) are far under it. Deterministic: the count is a function of the source. (5) **Shape** (not now): `split_lit` emitting one `Switch` over the column's distinct literals, and the backends a `switch`/`br_table`, is a performance item for the representation round, not part of this fix; the guard makes the chain correct, and the chain is what the JS engine optimises today. Why: the one robustness rule the compiler already had was written in one crate and followed in one; the finding shows the rest did not follow it, and a flat `match` is the shape where flat source becomes deep IR. Rejected: a limit on arms (a limit on a shape agents generate, for a bug in the compiler); a larger main-thread stack in the CLI (the LSP, MCP and test threads would still overflow, and the limit would be invisible); `serde_stacker`'s approach for the walks (it covers deserialisation only). Performance: `maybe_grow` costs a stack check per guarded call, as in `sakura_hir` today; measured on `bench compile` with the audit commit. Changed: plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.8 (row 11). Impact: Opus (2–4), the owner none. §17.200 bounds the emitted output's nesting as well, exempts the verifier's and the evaluators' value walks, and moves `V4004` into `check`.

194. **Core is a function of the snapshot: no `sakura_types` interner index crosses into Core, type and row parameters are numbered per def, and a reused analysis emits the same Core as a fresh one (slow-tests finding, commits `b900d577`..`f87e7280`; round 17).** Question: `direct.rs` and the WASM corpus now lower programs on one thread per core, and `fresh.rs` was kept sequential because the corpus it compares "records Core whose row-variable numbering depends on which earlier analysis is reused" (`diff_0029.json`: `row_vars: [56]` against `[54]`). Facts: `Analysis::analyze` hands the latest cached analysis to `analyze` as `prev` (§17.74); `check` starts from `prev.interner.clone()`, so the `ParamId`s and `RowParamId`s a program's defs receive come after whatever the reused interner already held; `sakura_core::lower` writes those global indices into Core as `Ty::Var(p.0)` (`lower/mod.rs` `ty`), `Row.tail = Some(p.0)` (`row`) and `FnDef.row_vars` (`lower/expr.rs`); the JS emitter writes `["V", i]` into type descriptors from the same number (`emit.rs`) and the LIR schema and the validator read it; `pretty.rs` already renumbers row variables by position for display with a comment admitting that "global ids shift whenever another module gains a row variable". So two processes, or one process with a different cache history (parallel test threads, an MCP server serving two projects, an LSP after an unrelated edit), produce different Core JSON and can produce different emitted JS for the same snapshot, against the deterministic-output rule (AGENTS.md; §13.2). Decision: (1) **Rule** (§13.2): a Core module is a function of the snapshot alone; every number in it is assigned by the lowering in a canonical order; no index of `sakura_types`' interner appears in Core; two analyses of one snapshot, whatever any cache held, emit byte-identical Core, JS and WASM. (2) **Fix** (Opus; `sakura_core/src/lower/{mod,expr}.rs`, `validate.rs`, `pretty.rs`): the lowering keeps, per top-level def together with the lambdas and clauses lifted from it, one table from `ParamId` to a def-local index and one from `RowParamId` to a def-local index: the def's scheme parameters first, in scheme order (so `params` positions and the emitters' `vars` tables, which look a parameter up by the number in the def's signature, keep agreeing), then any further parameter met in the body (a let-generalised local's) in first-occurrence order of the lowering's deterministic walk; `Ty::Var(k)`, `Row.tail = Some(k)` and `row_vars = [0, 1, …]` carry the local index; `u32::MAX` stays the "no parameter" sentinel. `validate` checks every `Ty::Var(k)` with `k != u32::MAX` and every tail against the def's parameter counts (the unbound-tail error exists; it gains the bound). `pretty.rs` drops its positional renumbering, since the id is positional; `r?{v}` stays as the validator's complaint. (3) **Test** (same commit; `sakura_lir/tests`): one test analyses the corpus twice in one process, in path order and in an order that first analyses a program with extra type and row parameters, and compares every `to_json()` byte for byte; the same test takes the semantics corpus through the JS emitter in both orders and compares the text; `fresh.rs` loses its sequential note and runs on the `par_map` helper of `f87e7280`. Regenerate the corpus (`regen_core`) and review the diff: only `row_vars`, tails and `Ty::Var` numbers change. (4) **Variable aliasing check** (same owner, same or next commit; `sakura_types/src/check/mod.rs`): `check` starts from `prev.interner.clone()` while `Infer::new()` restarts `ty_vars` and `row_vars` at zero, so a fresh `TyVar(0)` interns to the `TypeId` the previous analysis gave its own `TyVar(0)`; this is harmless only if no `Type::Var` or `Tail::Var` survives in a reused `TypedBody`, scheme or hole. Add a debug assertion at reuse that the types a reused body references contain no inference variable, and a test in which a previous analysis with an error-typed (unsolved) body is followed by an edit to a dependent module; if a variable can survive, the counters start from the interner's highest variable instead of zero. (5) Side effects: `sakura verify`'s proof cache keys hash a def's Core (`cache.rs` `key`, `body_digest`), so records of defs with type or row parameters are recomputed once; `sakura-core-snapshot-v1` keeps its shape and name (§17.107); the emitted-JS and Core print snapshots that change are reviewed as numbering only. Why: a deterministic-output rule is testable only when the IR is canonical; every cache this round adds (§17.195) would otherwise be a new source of drift; and the display code already knew the numbers were unstable. Rejected: renumbering only in `to_json` (the emitted JS carries the same numbers); keeping `fresh.rs` sequential (it pins one history, it does not make the output a function of the source); clearing the cache per analysis (it throws away §17.74's reuse and would not fix two processes). Performance: one small map per def at lowering; nothing measurable. Changed: §13.2. Impact: Opus (`sakura_core`, `sakura_types` (4), `sakura_lir/tests`); the corpus diff reviewed; proof records recomputed once. Readings ratified, (4)'s WASM argument corrected, in §17.202.

195. **Compile latency: std is analysed once per process and once per compiler build, not once per program; `sakura check` targets for a hello-world and 10k lines, measured by `bench compile` (slow-tests profile; the owner's standing goal; round 17).** Facts, measured 2026-10-19 with the `v0.1.0-alpha.1` release binary on the loaded reference machine (12 cores; indicative, §17.153): `sakura --version` 3.5 ms; `sakura fmt --check` 4 ms; `sakura check` on the `sakura new cli` hello-world 26.5 ms (seven runs, 25.7–28.8); `sakura check` on the `bench compile` 10k-line corpus 64 ms (five runs); `sakura test` on the hello-world 138 ms (one Node spawn). std is 45 files and 3,967 lines; every `sakura` process re-parses and re-checks it (`Snapshot::with_std`), so about 22 ms of the hello-world's 26.5 ms is std, the 10k-line user package costs about 38 ms on top, and the slow-test profile (98% of `direct.rs` inside `analyze`, 215 programs each paying std) is the same cost multiplied. §17.74's in-process reuse fires only when the previous analysis had the same files with the same interfaces, which a different program never has; std's `FileId`s come after the user's (`Snapshot::new` sorts by URI, `file:` before `sakura:`), so `lower_reusing`'s `p.file == now.file` could not match std across programs even if the predicate allowed it. Decision: (1) **Targets** (reference machine, quiet window, p95 over the 15 fresh processes `bench compile` already runs): `check_hello` ≤ 10 ms and `check_cold_10k` ≤ 45 ms. The research/10 §6 budgets (1 s / 3 s / 500 ms / 100 ms) stay the hard ceilings that exit 2; the two targets are printed beside them as a `target_ms` column and a miss is reported, not an exit, until the owner promotes a target to a budget after one quiet-window run meets it. `check_hello` is the `sakura new cli` project; `test_hello` is recorded without a budget so the Node spawn's share stays visible. (2) **Std first, prefix reuse in process** (Opus; `sakura_analysis/src/lib.rs`; the `lower_reusing` doc comment in `sakura_hir/src/lower.rs`): `Snapshot::new` orders std files before user files, so std's `FileId`s are a fixed prefix as its `ModuleId`s already are (`packages[0]` is std); `analyze`'s reuse predicate becomes "prev's files are a prefix of this snapshot's files, equal by URI, content hash and interface hash"; (as built and ratified in §17.204 (1): the same files or std alone, interface hashes computed and compared only for files whose text changed, std at the first analysis once WS-C1b lands) a std-only analysis, computed once per process on first use (`OnceLock`), is the fallback `prev` whenever the latest cached analysis does not match; the user modules are lowered and checked fresh as today. Acceptance: `Stats.checked_modules` and `lowers` for a second, unrelated program in one process count user modules only (an instrumentation test beside the §17.74 ones); `direct.rs` and the WASM corpus wall time recorded in the commit message; snapshots that pin the snapshot hash or a `FileId` order are reviewed as ordering only (`--json` output is by URI). The LSP and MCP servers thereby keep one std analysis for every project they serve. (3) **On-disk std cache for fresh processes** (Opus; after (2) and §17.194; `sakura_analysis` with serde derives on `sakura_hir::Program`, `sakura_types::{TypedProgram, Interner, Scheme, TypedBody, …}` and `Symbol`, one binary serde format from crates.io at its latest stable version added under `[workspace.dependencies]`; `sakura_cli` for the environment variables' documentation): the std-only `Analyzed` of (2) is written once per compiler build to `<cache>/sakuralang/std/<version>-<build-id>-<std-hash>.bin`, `<cache>` being `$SAKURA_CACHE_DIR`, else `$XDG_CACHE_HOME` or `~/.cache` on Unix and `%LOCALAPPDATA%` on Windows; the directory is created `0700` as the proof key store's is; the file is written to a temporary name and renamed; it is read under a 16 MiB size limit with a content hash in its header; any failure to open, verify or deserialise is ignored and the analysis recomputed and rewritten, never a diagnostic (one line under the existing verbose logging if any); `SAKURA_NO_CACHE=1` disables reading and writing and is what the determinism test uses for the fresh side. The key names the exact build, so the format needs no compatibility story and no version field beyond the key; the file holds only what this binary computed from its embedded std, so it is not an input to trust (whoever can write the cache can replace the binary). Acceptance: the two targets of (1) at a quiet window; a test that `check --json`, `--dump core` and the emitted JS of the semantics corpus are byte-identical with and without the cache (§17.194 makes this a byte comparison); a corrupt, truncated or oversized cache file is ignored and replaced; an unwritable cache directory costs one failed write per process and changes no output; STABILITY.md names the cache directory and the two variables. (4) **Profile first** (Opus, in (2)'s commit message and `research/10-evidence/compile-latency-r1.md`): the per-phase split of the std cost in a release build (parse, `interface_hash`, `evaluated_functions`, lower, check, `symbols::index`), so a hot spot inside the analysis is fixed where it is rather than hidden by the cache; any phase over a quarter of the total gets its own follow-up row in `performance-r1.md` §9. Why: the owner's standing goal is a language that is fast by default and "keep optimising what feels slow"; the cost every user pays on every `check`, `run` and `test` is the same cost that made the test suite slow, so the measured fix serves both; and the std prefix is the one reuse that §17.74's mechanism can express with a predicate change rather than a query graph. Rejected: embedding the pre-analysed std in the binary (a build script cannot run the analysis crate it belongs to, and artifact dependencies are unstable); skipping std bodies in `check` (sound for `check` alone, but `build`, `run`, `test`, `--dump` and the evaluated functions need them, and two modes of std are a second thing to keep deterministic); Salsa (§17.74 stands; one prefix is not a query graph); a cache of user programs (per-project incremental state under `target/sakura` is a later item, once the std cache has proved the plumbing); JSON for the cache (a 2 MB `serde_json` parse would spend half the budget it saves). Performance: this item is the performance item; its numbers enter `bench/deltas.md` only from a quiet window (§17.153). Changed: plan `performance-r1.md` §9 (rows WS-C1 and WS-C2, the targets); `bench/README.md`'s compile table and `bench.py` (Opus, with the row). Impact: Opus; Sol none; the owner promotes targets to budgets.

196. **Line endings and paths after P0 and P1: `*.snap -text`, block comments fold their line terminators when read, and `/C:/…` in human output is a one-line P0 follow-up, not P5's (P0 `2329019`, P1 `f0fdaf7`; plan `road-to-1.0.md` §6; round 17).** (1) **`*.snap -text`.** §17.180 (4b)'s `*.snap text eol=lf` is withdrawn. A snapshot file is the rendered value plus `\n`, written by the runner and compared byte for byte, and the rendered value may legitimately contain `\r\n` (an HTTP response, a string a Windows API produced); under `text eol=lf` git normalises the committed bytes to LF, so a fresh clone fails the comparison and the runner's hint blames line endings in the wrong direction. `sakura new` and `sakura init` write `*.sak text eol=lf` and `*.snap -text`; the runner's line-ending-only message (`test_runner.js`, `sakura_wasm_host.js`) names `--update-snapshots` and `*.snap -text` and no longer states that snapshots are LF; the repository's own `.gitattributes` gains `*.snap -text` (no insta snapshot holds a `\r` today; the rule keeps the comparison byte-exact if one ever does); a CHANGELOG line beside `f0fdaf7`'s. Owner: Opus (`new.rs`, the two runners, their tests), one commit; Sol the repository attribute line. (2) **Block comments.** §2 said "a comment never includes the `\r`"; `f0fdaf7` makes a line comment's token end before the `\r` of `\r\n`, but a block comment is one token spanning its lines and keeps `\r\n` inside, so the sentence held for one of two comment forms. The rule is restated (§2): the lexer's tokens keep the file's bytes (spans are byte offsets), a line comment ends before the `\r`, and every reader of comment text reads `\r\n` inside a comment as `\n`; `sakura fmt` already does (it splits comment text on `\n` and trims each line's `\r`), and documentation extraction reads `///` line comments only. Test: the CRLF `fmt` case gains a two-line block comment and `fmt` is idempotent on its output. Owner: Opus, small; the CODEOWNERS review P1 owes covers the restated rule. (3) **`/C:/dir/src/main.sak`.** `render_human` strips `file://` from a URI (`sakura_diagnostics/src/human.rs`), which leaves `/C:/…` for a Windows file outside the working directory (files below it are already made relative by `display_files` through `disk::uri_below`). This is a P0 follow-up, not P5's: P5 is packaging and CI, and the fix is testable on every host. `display_files` (`sakura_cli/src/main.rs`) falls back to the OS path of `disk::uri_path` for a `file:` URI outside the cwd and keeps the URI for `sakura:///std/…`; a unit test with the Windows flag (`uri_file_path(.., true)`) as `disk.rs`' Windows tests already run; `sakura_diagnostics` gains no path logic (dependency direction, §14.1). Owner: Opus, one line and a test. Why: snapshots are data the runner writes, not text a person edits; a rule that holds for one of two comment forms is not a rule; a rendering bug is fixed where its test runs on every host rather than deferred to Windows CI. Changed: §2 (comments), §17.180 (4b) (`*.snap`), plan `road-to-1.0.md` §6 P1 row (question closed, items owed). Impact: Opus; Sol (one attribute line).

197. **The agent-DX offline harness runs its tasks in parallel (Sol); the per-process cost it multiplies is §17.195's (slow-tests finding; round 17).** Facts: `agent_dx::offline_task_oracles` runs `evals/agent-dx/harness/offline.py`, which for each of the 12 tasks spawns `sakura check` and `sakura test` for the starter, the gold and hollow candidates and the frozen-spec controls, one task after another (93 plus 160 spawns per run with `runner_offline`'s, as reported), 22 s under `cargo test`; each spawn pays the std analysis of §17.195 and each `test` a Node process. Decision: approved. `offline.py` runs `exercise` over the tasks on a `concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor` (`--jobs`, default `os.cpu_count()`, `1` restoring today's serial run), each task in its own scratch directory as today, the per-task lines printed in task order after all results are collected so the report text is unchanged, and a `require` failure propagating with its task name. `test_runner.py` stays serial (unit tests of the runner). Not approved: parallelism inside one task (the gold and hollow variants reuse the task's directory in sequence). Acceptance: the printed report is byte-identical to the serial run's; `cargo test -p sakura_cli agent_dx` wall time recorded before and after in the commit message (expected under 8 s on the 12-core machine); `--jobs 1` passes; `harness/README.md` names the flag. Why: the tasks are independent by construction (own directory, own git repository), the cost is process spawns not Python, and the harness's own numbers (episodes, dollars) are untouched. Changed: plan `agent-dx.md` §4. Impact: Sol; no task, schema or report change.

198. **Order after round 17: what is unblocked now, with files, so parallel agents do not collide (`git log origin/main` at `f87e7280`; round 17).** State: since §17.191, landed on `main`: nextest in CI (`47b48160`, `6ceac6a2`, `87f82696`), platform P0 and P1 (`23290197`, `f0fdaf71`, plan `1b50aba9`), the symlinked-project test of §17.186 (`c25e3a43`), the dev profile at `opt-level 1` (`0071dfc1`), and the slow-test diagnosis (`b900d577` analysis outside the cache lock, `a73ed006` and `f87e7280` concurrent harnesses). Still in flight or waiting as §17.191 lists them: M4-OPT, the WASM bench lane, the deep-IR guard and `V4004`, WS-P11a, the tree-sitter grammar, `read_body`, N1a, the npm publish, the quiet window. New this round, ranked, each one commit unless stated: **1** §17.194 determinism (Opus; `sakura_core/src/lower/{mod,expr}.rs`, `validate.rs`, `pretty.rs`, `sakura_types/src/check/mod.rs` for (4), `sakura_lir/tests/{fresh,direct}.rs` and a new order test, `regen_core` output; none of M4-OPT's files, though a corpus regeneration rebases over M4-OPT's nested-option corpus additions in the ordinary way) — first, because §17.195's caches are verified against it; **2** §17.195 (2) and (4), std-first prefix reuse with the phase profile (Opus; `sakura_analysis/src/lib.rs`, `sakura_hir/src/lower.rs` doc comment, `research/10-evidence/compile-latency-r1.md`; may start beside 1, different crates); **3** §17.196 (3) the `/C:/` path (Opus; `sakura_cli/src/main.rs`, `sakura_analysis/src/disk.rs` test); **4** §17.196 (1) and (2) `*.snap -text` and the block-comment fmt test (Opus; `sakura_cli/src/new.rs`, `test_runner.js`, `sakura_wasm_host.js`, `sakura_fmt/tests/fmt.rs`, CHANGELOG; Sol: the repository `.gitattributes` line); **5** §17.197 the parallel harness (Sol; `evals/agent-dx/harness/offline.py`, `README.md`); **6** §17.195 (3) the on-disk std cache (Opus; after 1 and 2 are on `main`; `sakura_analysis`, serde derives in `sakura_hir` and `sakura_types`, the workspace `Cargo.toml` and lockfile under the shared-file rule, `bench/harness/bench.py` and `bench/README.md` for `check_hello`, `test_hello` and `target_ms`, STABILITY.md's cache sentence); **7** §17.195 (1)'s quiet-window run of `bench compile` (Astra measures, the owner promotes targets to budgets). Collisions: 1 and 2 touch different crates; 6 touches `sakura_analysis` after 2 has landed; 3 and 4 touch `sakura_cli` files that 2 and 6 do not; nothing here touches M4-OPT's, WS-P11a's or the deep-IR guard's files. Why: the determinism fix is small, blocks the verification of everything else this round and removes the last reason for a sequential test; the latency items are the owner's standing goal; the rest are one-line closures of questions P0 and P1 left. Rejected: folding 6 into 2 (the on-disk format is a dependency and a persisted file; it lands on its own with its own tests); a user-program cache in this round (§17.195). Performance: §17.195 is the measured item. Changed: plans `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.8 (state line, rows 12–15), `performance-r1.md` §9 (rows WS-C1, WS-C2, order after round 17), `road-to-1.0.md` §6 P1 row, `agent-dx.md` §4. Impact: Opus, Sol, Astra as named; the owner (quiet window, promotion of targets).

199. **M4-OPT readings: the `Option` niche leaves M4 for the §17.177 windows, a proved check also drops the callees its proof inlined, `countdown`'s bar is met by its frames counter, and the Node host reads the ABI version from the `sakura.debug` header before instantiating (REQUESTS 2026-10-19 "M4-OPT"; plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §7 M4-OPT items 1, 2, 7, 8; commits `f93e7ab7`..`08030b43`; round 18).** Decision: (1) **`--layout=niche` is dropped from M4-OPT's acceptance and from the M4 exit gate.** The measured upper bound of its benefit is 11.8% of `shapes`' requested bytes, 3.6% of `map_set`'s and nothing elsewhere, with no timing; research/08 §6.2 retains the optimization "only with semantic parity and a demonstrated benefit", and the second condition is not met on the suite. It becomes pending representation question (5) in §17.177 and plan `m5-native.md` §3: decided at the first quiet window after N3 on the native lane by a prototype's `requested` bytes and time on `shapes` and `map_set` (where `Some` traffic is measurable) against the tagged layout; default: tagged stays. If it is built it is the universal ABI §4.3 form with the per-field schema table, an ABI MINOR allocated when it lands (after N1a's 1.4.0); the known-type form with tagged adapters is rejected now (it keeps nested `Option` tagged, amends §17.99, and saves nothing on the benchmarks that motivated it). §17.83 and §17.99's parity gate stays its acceptance whenever it is built; `--layout niche` stays parsed and refused by `from_core` (the LIR seam), and the CLI help names this entry. §14.3's M4 line "Option null-unboxing after nested-option tests" is read as the nested-option corpus on the tagged layout (`nested_options.sak`, `nested_options_json.sak`; JS, WASM and the LIR interpreter printing the same bytes with dups and drops balanced), which is landed. (2) **A proved check's predicate goes with it when the predicate is total by construction, and a call the proof inlined counts as such.** `opt::proved` keeps its class (atoms, primitives whose only defect paths are definedness obligations the fragment emits, projections, allocations) and gains one member: a call bind whose callee symbol is in the proof record's `inlined` set for that site (`sakura_verify` records every inlined callee and refinement, `vc.rs` `inlined`, `cache.rs` hashes their bodies into the key, so a body edit already invalidates the record) and whose body is itself in the class, recursively, calls to builtins allowed only from a `total` table. The CLI passes the record's `inlined` set per site instead of the bare site; the call bind is deleted, the callee def stays for its other callers. The `total` table is the one list `emit.rs`'s bracket elision already keeps (`TOTAL`, 18 builtins that return for every argument), moved to `sakura_core::builtins` as a column so both readers share it; `list_get_expect`, `map_get_expect` and every `D03x` sink are not in it and keep the predicate. Soundness: the verifier inlines only pure, non-recursive, loop-free, summary-less callees to depth 8 (`MAX_INLINE_DEPTH`), so an inlined body's modelled operations are in the obligation's definedness `D` (§17.84) and the class test guards the unmodelled ones. Acceptance (Opus, one commit; `opt/proved.rs`, `sakura_cli/src/verify.rs`, the builtins table, `emit.rs` reading it): `--dump core` and the emitted JS and WASM show a predicate calling a total helper gone with its check; kept: a helper calling `List.get_expect`, a recursive helper, a helper with its own contract (summarised, not inlined), a helper past depth 8; `cli.rs::build_deletes_a_proved_checks_predicate_unless_it_calls_a_function` is renamed to what it now pins. §14.3's "a `proved` check disappears together with its predicate's computation" reads with "when the predicate is total by construction, callees the proof inlined included". (3) **`countdown`'s §17.118 bar is met, witnessed by the counter.** Since WS-P9 both variants run the same loop and seven frames; the times (103 against 103 ms at n = 300,000, 116 against 113 at 3,000,000) sit at the Node startup floor (about 101 ms). Rule: when both sides of a §17.118 ratio lie within 1.1× of the host's startup floor, the ratio is "at floor" and the structural counter (`frames` here) decides; `bench/deltas.md` records `countdown` as met by counter, not as a carry, and M5 inherits nothing from it. `fib` (3.9×) and `nbody` (2.8×) meet the bar on advisory timings, so §17.160 (1)'s conditional carry of `nbody`'s *bar* is not needed; `nbody`'s §17.109 *ceiling* stays carried under §17.177 (2) and (4). All three are read once more at the quiet window (§17.153). (4) **The host checks the ABI version before it instantiates.** ABI §12's `sakura.debug` header already carries `abi_version`, and `readDebug` runs before `fresh()`; it returns the version, `instantiate` applies §0's MAJOR/MINOR rule to it before `WebAssembly.instantiate`, and still reads the exported global after, which stays the authoritative value (a forged section cannot pass a mismatched module). `sakura build --strip` keeps the 44-byte header (magic, version, module hash) with every table count zero, so a stripped module gets the same pre-check; a module with no section or a hash mismatch is instantiated and checked by the global as today. This is ABI **1.2.1** (PATCH: wording and the strip header; `sakura_abi_version` unchanged). Test: a module stamped 1.3.0 that imports a function the host lacks reports `V8003` before any `LinkError`, stripped and unstripped; `sakura_codegen_wasm/tests/abi.rs` keeps the host's constants to `abi.rs`. Rejected: a separate `sakura.abi` custom section (a second place for one number); diffing `WebAssembly.Module.imports` against the host table (V8's `LinkError` already names the import, and a module that passes the MINOR rule yet lacks an import is a toolchain bug, not a version case). (5) **WS12 item 8's three readings (REQUESTS 2026-10-18) are ratified:** 1.1.2 was a PATCH with kinds 4 and 7, which the 1.2.0 text lists among kinds 0–7; one full-table policy (`Err("too many pending operations")` where the result is a `Result<_, String>`, `D091` elsewhere); the W2 check is the top only and `cli_parse`'s `T` is unchecked. Why: an optimisation with a measured 11.8% ceiling on one program does not earn an ABI MINOR in the milestone that is M5's long pole; the record already knows what the proof inlined, so reading it is cheaper than a second analysis; a ratio at the measurement floor measures the host, not the program; the version is already in a section the host reads first. Changed: §14.3 (M4 line and acceptance), §17.177 (pointer), plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.8 (row 6 done, the exit gate), §7 M4-OPT (readings line), plan `m5-native.md` §2 (N0b and N5 start now) and §3 (two rows). Impact: Opus (2), (4), each one small commit; Astra (`bench/deltas.md` rows for (3)); the owner none.

200. **Deep output: emitted nesting is bounded by source nesting, never by a sequence; a literal `match` is one `Switch`; the WASM dispatch nests at most 256 blocks per level; the snapshot's depth bound stands; `V4004` is reported by `check`; the pattern checker's quadratic usefulness is a perf item (deep-IR guard `5e1bcafb`..`e6b26437`; §17.193; round 18).** Facts, measured this round with the debug CLI at `e6b26437` on Node 26.11: the 3,000-arm `Int` literal `match` emits 24,033 lines of JS with 3,001 labeled join blocks nested one inside the next (every arm's join is bound in a chain whose body is the next) and 3,000 `else` levels (`split_lit`'s `If` chain), and V8's parser overflows at module compile; the WASM module traps at compile (`D090 trap: Maximum call stack size exceeded`) on the function-level `br_table` dispatch, which needs one nested `block` per LIR block (3,000 here). Node compiles a flat 3,000-case `switch` and 3,000 flat `if`s; nested labeled blocks pass at 1,000 and fail at 1,500. So §17.193 (5)'s assumption that the JS engine optimises the chain was wrong: it does not load it, and `deep_ir.rs` could prove `run` only on the evaluators. Decision: (1) **Rule** (§13.2): the nesting of emitted output (JS blocks, WASM control structures) is bounded by the source's nesting plus a constant; a sequence in the source (arms, `let`s, joins, blocks) emits as a sequence. The emitters' constants: at most 64 nested inline join blocks in JS, at most 256 `block`s per dispatch level in WASM. (2) **A literal `match` is one `Switch`** (`sakura_core`, `matchc::split_lit`; `Expr::Switch` gains `SwitchOn::Lit(ty)` with arms keyed by the literal, §13.2; the snapshot `Terminator::Switch` the same): when the first row's column holds an `Int` or `String` literal, the node switches on every distinct literal of that column, each arm the rows holding that literal or a wildcard in their order, the default the wildcard rows (first-match semantics unchanged; the duplicated wildcard rows are what `V4004` counts); `Bool` keeps the `If`. JS emits `switch (x) { case 1: … }` (flat; strings compare by `===`). The LIR lowers `SwitchOn::Lit` to a terminator the WASM emitter turns into **index arithmetic, not nested blocks**: the arm blocks are allocated contiguously, a dense key range (span at most twice the arm count) sets the dispatch local to `base + (x − min)` after a bounds check that falls to the default, and a sparse one is a binary search of `if`s over the sorted literals (depth log₂ k) whose leaves are jumps. (3) **The WASM dispatch is two-level past 256 blocks** (`func.rs`): an outer `br_table` selects a chunk of 256 cases and an inner one the block, so nesting is at most 256 + ⌈n/256⌉; a function with 256 blocks or fewer keeps the one-level form byte for byte (reworded in §17.219 (8): and no literal switch past eight arms). (4) **JS joins and `else`** (`emit.rs`): the inline labeled-block form is used up to 64 nested levels, and a deeper join takes the arrow-function form the emitter already has (`const jN = (…) => {…}`, jumps as `return jN(…)`); an `If` whose `then` ends in a terminator (every ANF branch does: `Ret`, `Jump`, `Fail` emit `return`, `break`, `continue`, `throw`) emits its `else` at the same depth as the `if`, so no `else` chain nests. (5) **The snapshot bound `MAX_BLOCK_DEPTH = 1,024` stands** (§17.129): it bounds hostile input, not arms; after (2) a literal match is one node deep and no sequence the parser admits (depth 128) reaches it. The deep-IR test adds a 3,000-row guarded `match` (`n if n > k => …`) to show guard failure continues in a join, flat in the snapshot, and matchc binds the fall-through as a join if it does not. `to_json` runs the block serialiser under `sakura_hir::ensure_stack` (a manual `Serialize` for `Block` around the derived one), since serde's derives recurse and `serde_stacker` guards only reading. If a legitimate program ever trips the bound, the bound is raised with that program as the fixture. (6) **`V4004` as built is ratified with one correction.** Counting the rows or-pattern expansion produces is right (the budget is the tree's size, and 20^5 rows are the tree); the explain text says "decision-tree nodes and expanded rows". The correction: **`sakura check` reports every diagnostic `build` reports** (an agent's loop runs `check` first, and the LSP and MCP servers are `check`), so `check` lowers Core for the user modules and takes the pattern phase's `V4004` from it; the cost is measured on `bench compile` (`check_hello`, `check_cold_10k`, §17.195), and if lowering adds more than 10% to `check_cold_10k`, `check` lowers only the defs whose match matrices exceed a pre-filter (rows times or-alternatives over 4,096), which reports the same set. This lands after WS-C1 (same `sakura_analysis` file). (7) **Unguarded walks, ratified:** the verifier's `vc.rs` and `smt.rs` (input depth bounded by source nesting after (2) and by `MAX_INLINE_DEPTH`), and the evaluators' value walks and derived `Drop`/`Clone` (bounded by data, in test tools); §17.193 (2)'s list reads with these exemptions.
