(2) **WS-P13c** (Astra, now, no compiler code; `research/10-evidence/ws-p13c/`): port the Planner to `List<Var>` and `List<Con>` (`new_var` pushes, `put_var` sets, `var_of` indexes; AWFY checks 3 and 4 kept, every oracle input passing on both engines) and measure three arms with WS-P13a's protocol (eight fresh processes per cell, Node and Bun, n = 1000 and 5000, rotated order): (i) the `List` port as emitted, with its `--dump owned` recorded so the note says where ownership stops; (ii) (i) with `list_set_owned`/`list_push_owned` forced at `put_var` and `put_con`, which is what WS-P13b buys on the `List` form; (iii) (ii) with the two lists as flat JS arrays, which is what a flat representation buys on top. One table beside WS-P13a's; the hand-JS control unchanged. (3) **Rules read from the number, every bar as `1 − new/old`.** Astra's reading of §17.201 is ratified: "25%" is a 25% time reduction (`new/old ≤ 0.75`, 1.333× throughput), as WS-P11a's deltas were read; a bar in this log is a throughput factor only where it says "×" (§17.118's "2× faster"). (a) If (i) is at most 0.75 of the Map baseline at Node n = 5000, WS-B rewrites `bench/programs/deltablue/sakura/main.sak` to the `List` port (the `sieve` precedent; `meta.toml` and the oracle unchanged; the Map port is not kept, WS-P13a's evidence retains it) and `deltablue` keeps its carry with the new ratio in `bench/deltas.md`; otherwise the port stays and the ablation is the record. (b) WS-P13b's bar is re-read on (ii) against (i): at least 25% builds it as §17.201 (3) specifies, on the `List` port, `list_set` and `list_push` being consuming builtins already; under 25% WS-P13b stays closed and the Map reading (22.7%) is not re-read. (c) (iii) against (ii) is the number §17.177 (1)'s second window reads for `deltablue`, beside `sieve`, `nbody` and `spectral_norm` and against `rbtree`'s retained versions, `pipeline` and the builders; the window's flat candidate is one shape, a flat vector with copy on a shared write and `W8001` on the forced copy (Lean's `Array`, Koka's vector; research/12 §5), compared with the trie as is. A hybrid whose representation switches on an ownership fact is not a candidate: a value's representation cannot depend on a fact about one of its uses, and the facts are per use (§17.149). (4) **Backends:** nothing changes on WASM or native now; the `List` port runs on all three; its WASM cell follows §17.211 (5) (N0b's owned facts on the LIR, then N3/N4) and the same window reads both lanes. (5) **The sentence of (1)** lands with (3a)'s rewrite (Opus, small; `compiler/std/INDEX.md` under `List`, `skills/sakura/references/stdlib.md`, the mirror regenerated), so the benchmark port is its example; if (3a) does not fire, it lands alone. Milestones: WS-P13c now; the rewrite and the sentence now on its number; WS-P13b conditional, in the performance round, not an M4 exit item; the representation M5 at the window. Why: a dense id space is a sequence and the language has one; the ablation located the time (the hash walk and the log), it did not find a missing type; a number before a mechanism (§17.201). Rejected: an `Array` type; a dense `Map`; building WS-P13b on the Map ablation (below the bar, on the wrong form); reading the bar as 1.25× (1.29× would pass, and the precedent reads time). Performance: this entry is the measured item. Changed: §17.201 (pointer), plan `performance-r1.md` §9 (WS-P13a decided, WS-P13c row, WS-P13b's condition), plan `m5-native.md` §3 (the `deltablue` row). Impact: Astra (WS-P13c), WS-B and Opus (the rewrite and the sentence, conditional), the owner (the window).

218. **WS-W3 as measured: the frame protocol stays M5 work, the 2× acceptance stands, and no fix is named until the `wasm::rt` and `arena_obj` buckets are attributed (`research/10-evidence/ws-w3/README.md`; §17.211 (4); round 22).** Facts (advisory): all-frame `fib(30)` 351.6 ms against 13.1 ms with twins on the reference host; 2,692,542 frames, about 131 ns per frame as a whole-workload figure; the Linux `perf` profile puts 28.2% of samples in `wasm::rt` (symbol attribution only; the mechanism unresolved), 15.6% in `arena_obj` (generational id resolution shared by the driver, `call` and `pop_frame`), 11.6% in the compiled `fib`, `frame_alloc` 9.0% inclusive, `pop_frame` 15.8% inclusive, `Rt::drop` nil; `after` and `next` are inlined into the driver and not separately measurable; the function-name section confirms the inlining. Decision: (1) **M5, gating nothing**, as §17.211 (4) said; the WASM envelope is read on twins (WS-W1, WS-W2) and a frame is the slow path by design. (2) **The acceptance stands** (all-frame `fib` 2× faster, twins byte-identical and unchanged in time), but §17.211 (4)'s expected shapes are withdrawn as the plan: a perfect `frame_alloc` saves 9%, so a free list or a cached slot cannot carry 2×. **Attribution first** (Astra, when the machine is idle, no deadline; a follow-up section of the same note): a count of `rt()` acquisitions per frame on the all-frame path (a scratch build with a counter, not a shipped one) and a disassembly of the sampled offsets of `wasm::rt`, so the bucket is named; `after`'s share from a scratch build with the inlining off, if the count does not explain it. (3) **The order of fixes, once attributed** (Opus, M5; `runtime/rt/src/{wasm,exec,arena,heap}.rs`; each with its before and after on the WS-W3 protocol): one `rt()` acquisition per `run` step, the `&mut Rt` threaded through `next`, `after` and `pop_frame` (soundness: the one-live-borrow rule; host imports reenter only through `call_host`, which ends the step); the current frame resolved once per step and passed by reference where `call`, `pop_frame` and the driver resolve its generational id again; then the per-size free list. A fix that does not move its bucket is not landed (§17.201's rule). (4) **The protocol is adopted**: the twelve-call WASI wrapper with the first two intervals dropped, eight processes per variant, is the before/after for every M5 frame-path change, and the `frames` counter is its check that the path did not change. Why: the note's bound is honest and a 2× needs the two buckets it could not resolve; a fix named on a 9% bucket would be the profile-alone mistake WS-P11b made. Rejected: pulling the protocol into M4; caching `&mut Rt` in a static without the borrow review. Performance: none until M5. Changed: §17.211 (4) (pointer), plan `performance-r1.md` §9 (WS-W3 row), plan `m5-native.md` §3 (one line). Impact: Astra (the attribution), Opus (M5), the owner none.

219. **Row 16 as built: the nine readings (`88144a7e`, `3fefdf4c`, `16f73e5a`, `92219421`; §17.200; round 22).** Decision, one line each where the build is right: (1) `SwitchOn::Lit(lits)` with an arm's key indexing the literals, as `Errors` indexes its members: **ratified**; §13.2 reads so. (2) A dense table that jumps through one dispatch entry per key: **ratified**; the span rule (at most twice the arm count) bounds the entries, and each entry is a dispatch case running its arm's edge or the default's. (3) **Shared WASM locals are keyed on locals, not on blocks.** The cost is V8's optimising tier, quadratic in locals times back edges, and a single block of 3,000 `let`s shows it (572 MB reported) with no dispatch at all; the block count was a proxy. Rule (§17.200 (1) extended): the locals a function declares are bounded by its live variables, not by its `let` count. `func.rs` assigns slots by liveness, a slot free after its variable's last use and shared across blocks as today, whenever a function's locals exceed 256; under 256 the output is byte-identical to today. The two-level dispatch keeps its own key (256 blocks). Opus, small, after WS-W2 (same file); acceptance: 256- and 257-local functions pin the forms beside the block fixtures; the 3,000-`let` function's V8 memory before and after in the commit message; every function under 256 locals byte-identical across the corpus; `deep_ir.rs`'s shapes unchanged in output. (4) A table or a binary search for every LIR switch past eight arms: **ratified**; eight is a named constant in `func.rs` with an eight- and a nine-arm fixture, and it is not tuned until a WASM profile shows a switch. (5) An `Int` column holding a literal past ±(2⁵³ − 1) keeps its `If` chain in Core: **ratified**; `D003` is raised where the comparison is reached, in row order, on JS, and the chain is the form that keeps that order on both backends; the backend-specific stdout is pinned (`match_literal_beyond_js_range`) and the shape is rare. (6) `String` switches stay an equality chain in the LIR: **ratified**; a bytewise binary search is the next shape only if a WASM profile of `tokenizer` or `json_validate` at the window shows the chain; JS's `switch` on strings is the engine's. (7) The snapshot gained a variant without a bump: **ratified, with the rule** §17.107 left implicit: `-v2` when a committed v1 file reads differently, or not at all, under the new reader; a variant no committed file holds is not a shape change, the corpus is re-blessed in the same commit, and the readers are this repository's. `sakura_core_snapshot` depending on `sakura_hir` for `ensure_stack`: **ratified**; `ensure_stack` is the one stack guard every later phase uses (its doc says so), a crate for one function is the abstraction AGENTS.md refuses, and the critical path is unchanged because every consumer of the snapshot crate depends on `sakura_core`, which depends on `sakura_hir`. (8) §17.200 (3)'s "byte for byte" reads: *a function with at most 256 blocks and no literal switch past eight arms keeps its one-level form byte for byte*; the review line in `3fefdf4c` (every function without a literal match byte-identical) is the check. (9) The 3,000-`let` single-block function is (3)'s case and its measurement. Why: eight of nine are the shape the design asked for, read against the code; the ninth found the key named a proxy. Rejected: keeping the block key beside the local key (one cause, one key); a crate for `ensure_stack`. Performance: (3) is measured in its commit. Changed: §17.200 (3) (pointer), plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.8 (row 16's readings, row 29). Impact: Opus (row 29, small).

220. **A run of guarded rows is a sequence: Core inlines a join jumped to once at its jump site, outside any `Handle`, `Try` or clause boundary, so the JS and the WASM output stay flat; an M4 exit item (§17.200 (1); plan §3.8 row 16's open limit; round 22).** Facts: `16f73e5a` made an arm one leaf reaches that leaf's code and builds a guarded run in one pass, but each guard's fall-through is still a join; the JS emitter nests joins as labeled blocks to 64 levels and as arrow functions past them, and direct code in a loop keeps its joins inline (no bounce for a back edge), so about 1,500 guarded rows in a loop overflow Node's parser and about 10,000 outside one exhaust its stack (`D090`); the WASM dispatch is flat but carries one block per join. The proposal was that the JS emitter inline a once-jumped join at its jump site. Decision: (1) **The inlining is Core's, not the JS emitter's.** A join with exactly one jump and no other reference is replaced by its body at the jump, its parameters bound to the jump's arguments (Core locals are bound once, so no capture or shadowing question arises), when no `Handle`, `Try` or handler-clause boundary lies between the join's binding and the jump (across one the body would run under a handler it was bound outside of; `16f73e5a`'s exclusion, kept); an `If` whose `then` ends in a terminator already emits its `else` flat (§17.200 (4)), so a run of guards becomes a flat sequence on JS and one block fewer per guard on WASM. (2) Where: `sakura_core` (`lower/matchc.rs` where the run is built, or a general pass in `opt.rs` run before the snapshot; the implementer's choice, the snapshot holding the result either way); the LIR and both emitters unchanged. (3) Acceptance: `deep_ir.rs` runs 3,000 guarded rows in a direct-style loop and in a performing loop, and 20,000 outside one, on Node on both backends with the expected output; a guarded run inside a `try` and one inside a clause pin the boundary (the join stays, the output is the same); emitted JS of the semantics corpus changes only by inlined joins, reviewed as such; frames, dups, drops and restarts unchanged on every counter snapshot; `--no-direct`, trace and WASM parity; the snapshot prints reviewed as the inlining and nothing else. Opus, small; `matchc.rs`/`opt.rs`, `deep_ir.rs`, the Core corpus snapshots; after row 16 (landed), before WS-P9b-lit (which edits `opt.rs`); no file in common with WS-W1. **M4 exit item**: §17.200 (1) says nesting is never bounded by a sequence, and a guarded run is a sequence; the rule is the acceptance. Why: one fix in the shared form serves both backends and the snapshot, where an emitter-side fix serves one and leaves the WASM blocks. Rejected: the JS-only inlining (a second place that knows what a join is); raising the 64-level bound (the arrow form past it is the same chain, one call deep per guard). Performance: fewer blocks on WASM; nothing else. Changed: plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.8 (row 26, the exit gate). Impact: Opus.

221. **Suppression by place is ratified as built: any span of a diagnostic inside the broken body makes it a cascade (`380b4efd`; §17.207 (1); round 22).** Facts: §17.207 (1) said "primary span"; the build drops a diagnostic when any of its spans lies inside the body that holds the syntax error, because a `V3001` raised for a broken body has its primary span on the function's name and its causes on the body's calls, and the literal rule leaked it (`V0001_effect_violation_in_broken_body` pins the suppression); the two `V2002_signature_first_used_in_*` fixtures pin `[V0001, V2002, V2010]` fresh and reusing; no `sakura_types` diagnostic today has its primary span in one def and a secondary span at a use site in another def's body. Decision: ratified; the rule reads *a diagnostic is a cascade of a syntax error if and only if one of its spans lies inside the body that holds the error (same file, within the body's range)*, and the reason is the rule's: a span inside a broken body is evidence the parser could not vouch for, whichever span carries the message. The one edge, a diagnostic about another def that cites a use site inside the broken body, has no instance; when one is written, it is dropped by this rule and its author pins the fixture, which is the acceptable side (a lost report in a file that already reports its syntax error, never a wrong one). `check_program_with`'s doc reads as this entry. Why: the fresh output was wrong and the as-built rule is the one that names evidence. Rejected: "primary span, or the def's own header with a span in its body" (a second clause for a case the first already reads). Changed: §17.207 (1) (pointer). Impact: none.

222. **An export is feature-tested by presence; the ABI version states what a module requires of its host; `task_free` is an optional export and 1.2.2 stays a PATCH (`92f4158e`; §17.212; ABI §0; round 22).** Facts: §17.212 read `rt_task_free` as an existing export; `wasm.rs` defined it and the WASI command called it internally, but the linked module's export list did not carry it, so `92f4158e` added the export and the Node host calls it only when the module has it; `sakura_abi_version` stayed `0x010200`. ABI §0's MINOR rule names tags, imports, section fields and layouts; its refusal rule (a host refuses a module whose MINOR is greater) exists because such a module may need an import the host lacks. A reviewer argued MINOR (1.3.0) or a rule for optional exports. Decision: (1) **The rule, written into ABI §0**: the version a module records states what it *requires* of its host (imports, layouts, outcome codes, the counters it writes); an export is read by probing the instance, never by version. Adding an export that a host calls only when present, whose absence costs resources and never semantics, is a PATCH, listed in §9 as optional with the version that introduced it and what a host without it loses; making an export required (a host refusing a module without it) is MINOR, because a new host would then refuse an old module; changing or removing an export's signature is MAJOR. (2) **1.2.2 stays a PATCH** under (1): a 1.2.0 host runs a 1.2.2 module as before (it never calls `task_free`, and keeps the leak), a 1.2.2 host runs a 1.2.0 module by probing; a MINOR would have made every 1.2 host refuse a module that needs nothing from it, which is the refusal rule applied backwards. (3) §9's `task_free` line reads "optional since 1.2.2; a host without it keeps every finished root task" (Opus, one line, with row 27's commit in the same file). Why: the refusal rule protects a host from a module's needs, and an export is the other direction. Rejected: 1.3.0 (a refusal for nothing); a required `task_free` (a 1.2.0 module would then be refused by the host that fixed the leak). Changed: ABI §0 (this round, the designer), §17.212 (pointer). Impact: Opus (one line in §9).

223. **The Node WASM host's served requests: heap exhaustion is `503` with its `D096` record, a trap is `500`, its `D090` record and a stopped server, nothing reaches the guest after the server stops and every pending request is answered, and a request pipelined behind a `413` is bounded work and stays (`ea22b159`'s review; ABI §8.5, §8.6, §9; §17.159 (6), §17.216 (4); round 22).** Facts: `invoke` returns 0 when the listener frame is gone or the guest heap is exhausted, and the host answers both with the outcome `gone`, which `serveHttp` turns into a silent `500` with no record on stderr; a trap inside `invoke` throws inside Node's request callback, and Node ends the process, where ABI §8.6 says the host catches a trap, reports `D090` and never enters the instance again; after the host's `close()` (the ledger: an outcome, a trap or exit) the `served` map is cleared and its requests get no reply, and after the server stops (`s.close`, or the guest's `Serve.listen` returning) a request on a kept-alive connection still runs the callback and reaches `invoke` with a listener id the arena no longer holds (the generational id makes it `gone`, so the guest is safe, but the host relies on the guest to refuse what it should not send); a request pipelined behind a refused `413` is parsed and served before the connection closes, bounded by the same per-request caps, on both backends since `serveHttp` is shared. Decision: (1) **Heap exhaustion on a served request** (row 27, with (3), one commit): after (3) no stopped listener reaches `invoke`, so a 0 from `invoke` means the heap, and the host answers `{kind: "defect", code: "D096", message: "heap exhausted: request not started"}`; `serveHttp` reports it once on stderr as its other defects and answers `503` (`retry-after: 1`, the connection kept: the server is short of memory, the connection is sound); the server keeps serving, the guest having run nothing. The `gone` outcome is removed. ABI §9's `invoke` line reads: 0 is heap exhaustion; a stopped listener is the host's to prevent. (2) **A trap inside `invoke`** (row 28): the callback catches it, reports `D090` with the function name and PC as §8.6 says, answers that request `500`, and stops the server: no further request is accepted, every request still in `served` or arriving is answered `500` with the record already written, and the `serve_listen` request is never completed, because the instance is not entered again; `runTask` returns the trap's defect (exit 70) and `sakura test` continues on a fresh instance as today. The host's existing after-trap test (W9) gains the serve case with a module whose handler traps (a test-only `unreachable` through the existing trap fixture mechanism). (3) **After `close()` and after the server stops** (row 27): the ledger answers every request still in `served` with `500` and the record of what ended the instance (a trap's `D090`, a defect's record, or `D090 "server stopped"` on exit) before clearing the map; `serveHttp`'s `close` sets a stopped flag the callback checks first, answering `503` with `connection: close` and never calling `invoke`; the guest's own stop (`stopped`) runs the same path. The invariant for ABI §8.5: *`invoke` is called only for a listener the host knows to be live, while the guest is not running, and every served request is answered exactly once.* Owner Opus; `runtime/js/sakura_wasm_host.js` (after `ea22b159`, same file), `runtime/js/sakura_runtime.js` (`serveHttp`'s outcomes and stopped flag), `runtime/js/test/wasm_host.test.js`, `sakura_cli/tests/cli.rs` (the two-backend serve fixtures), `runtime/rt/ABI.md` §8.5 and §9 (1.2.3, an erratum: failure modes stated, no byte, import or export changes). Acceptance: with `SAKURA_MAX_HEAP` low, a request whose decoding exhausts the heap is `503`, one `D096` record is on stderr and the next request is served; a trapping handler gives `500`, one `D090` record, `503` for a request sent after it, and exit 70; a request on a kept-alive connection after the server stops is `503` and `invoke` is not called (a counter in the test); the JS backend's fixtures unchanged except where a status was `500` and is now `503` by the same rule (the JS host has no heap limit of its own: §12.1's `D096` is the WASM guest's). **Rows 27 and 28 are M4 exit items**: a server on the M4 backend that dies on a trap or answers a resource defect without its record breaks §8.6 and §12.2's contract that every defect reaches stderr once. (4) **Pipelined requests behind a `413` stay as built**: each is checked against the same caps, the work is bounded, and a `413` does not mark a client hostile; one sentence in ABI §8.5 and in `std/INDEX.md` says a request pipelined behind a refused one may be served before the connection closes (with row 27's commit). Why: §17.159 (6) and §17.216 (4) already sort served failures by whose fault they are (the client's: `413`; the handler's: `500`; the server's resources: `503` is the status for that third kind, which they had no case for); §8.6's trap rule exists and the callback was the one path around it. Rejected: `500` for heap exhaustion (tells the client its request broke the server); keeping the server up after a trap (§8.6); a second return value from `invoke` to tell `gone` from the heap (the host already knows whether it stopped the server); dropping pipelined requests after a `413` (a change to shared `serveHttp` for work the caps bound). Performance: one flag read per request. Changed: ABI §8.5 and §9 (by the implementer, 1.2.3), plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.8 (rows 27, 28, the exit gate), `std/INDEX.md`. Impact: Opus (two commits); the owner (the review AGENTS.md asks for a semantics change in the served statuses is requested in row 27's commit message).

224. **Order after round 22 (`git log origin/main` at `ea22b159`; round 22).** State: since §17.215, landed on `main`: row 16's two commits and the review fix (`88144a7e`, `3fefdf4c`, `16f73e5a`, `92219421`), the suppression rule (`380b4efd`), the nightly matrix (`9e906792`), the `tail_clique` ports (`36e3c184`), `bench envelope` (`37d7f9a7`), the task release (`92f4158e`), the serve copy-budget fix (`ea22b159`), WS-P13a and WS-W3's notes (`b5a35240`). `bench envelope` exits 0 on a miss: **confirmed as designed** (§17.213 (2); the gate is the owner's reading of the report, and a nonzero exit would make the window's run a CI failure on a reading only the owner makes). New this round, ranked, each one commit unless stated: **1** §17.220 the guarded-run joins (Opus, small; `sakura_core`; now; **M4 exit**); **2** §17.223 (1) and (3), row 27 (Opus; the two host files, ABI 1.2.3; now, after `ea22b159`; **M4 exit**); **3** §17.223 (2), row 28 (Opus; after 2, same files; **M4 exit**); **4** WS-W1 then WS-W2 (unchanged, Opus; row 16 has landed, so they start now); **5** §17.217 (2) WS-P13c (Astra; now; no code); **6** §17.217 (3a) and (5), the `deltablue` rewrite and the `List` rule's sentence (WS-B and Opus; on WS-P13c's number); **7** WS-P13b (Opus; only on WS-P13c's (ii)); **8** §17.219 (3), row 29 (Opus, small; `func.rs`; after WS-W2); **9** WS-P9b-lit (Opus, small; `opt.rs`; after 1); **10** §17.218 (2), the `rt()` attribution (Astra; when idle; no code); **11** WS-C2 and its sequence (Opus, the latency owner; unchanged, colliding with nothing above); **12** the WASM envelope window (the owner, after 4; Astra runs both suites and `bench envelope`). Collisions: 2 and 3 are one sequence in `sakura_wasm_host.js` and `sakura_runtime.js` after `ea22b159`; 1 and 9 both edit `sakura_core` (1 first; 9 is a different function if 1 lands in `matchc.rs`); 4 and 8 share `func.rs`, so 8 waits; 5, 6 and 7 are `bench/programs/deltablue` and `research/`, then `opt/owned.rs`. Why: the three exit items are small and name rules the output already breaks; the WASM rows are the exit path and were waiting only for row 16; the `deltablue` sequence is evidence first. Rejected: starting WS-P13b in parallel with WS-P13c (the condition is the number). Performance: WS-W1, WS-W2, WS-P13c are the measured items. Changed: plans `performance-r1.md` §9 (order after round 22), `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.8 (state line, rows 26–29, the exit gate), `m5-native.md` §3. Impact: Opus, Astra, WS-B, the owner as named; Sol none this round.
225. **1.0's evidence is produced in this repository on an accelerated path: the outside-programs gate is withdrawn by the owner, and replaced by a dogfood corpus from two model families upgraded across a minor, agent-DX evals at a stated bar on two families, a fuzzing and differential campaign of stated size, the envelope held at two back-to-back releases and the stability guarantees in STABILITY.md; packages are out of 1.0; native is experimental at 1.0 (owner, 2026-10-19: "no lets not wait for other people for a full cycle release"; §17.168 (7); plan `road-to-1.0.md`; round 23).** Facts: §17.168 (7) gated 1.0 on three programs outside this repository maintained for one release cycle by someone other than the owner and the agents, a bar nobody here can produce or schedule; the repository already has what the replacement needs: six libFuzzer targets with a nightly (`compiler/fuzz`, `fuzz.yml`, 600 s per target), a parity matrix comparing JS and WASM on stdout, traces, test records and defect code, message and site, the LIR evaluator and V4b's replay, the agent-DX harness with twelve tasks and offline oracles (`offline.py` in CI) whose paid pilot reached 11/12 final success in the skill condition on Opus with $5.80 of the $10 left, `bench envelope` over both suites, and two model families already writing this compiler (Claude and Codex). `sakura.toml` denies unknown fields (`manifest.rs`), and `sakura new` already writes a lock file. Decision: (1) **The outside-programs clause is withdrawn**; nothing else in the bar moves: every correctness, determinism and security gate stands, and what changes is who produces the usage evidence and how the soundness window is measured. (2) **Dogfood corpus** (`dogfood/<program>/`, outside `compiler/examples`): six programs, one each of a CLI over `Cli` and `Fs`, an HTTP service over `Serve` and `Sql.sqlite`, a data pipeline over `Http` with cassettes, an `Llm` agent loop with cassettes, a WASM library consumed from the Node host across the pull boundary, and a verified numeric kernel with `requires`/`ensures` under `sakura verify`; each at least 300 lines of `.sak` outside its tests, a `sakura test` suite of at least 20 tests with a cassette for every service, run in CI on `js` and `wasm` against the **installed `v0.1.0` binary**, never the source build; three written by Claude agents and three by Codex agents, from the installed binary, `sakura setup`'s skill and the website only, never from the compiler's source; a header records the author's family and the release built against; every friction is a dated `REQUESTS` or docs item. Upgrade: after `v0.2.0` tags, an agent of the other family upgrades each program following only the release notes' migration lines and records the diff; acceptance is all six passing on 0.2.0 with every migration explained by a CHANGELOG line; an unexplained break is a 0.2.x fix and a notes fix before the rc. (3) **Agent-DX bar**: in the skill condition, final success at least 10/12 and pass@1 at least 8/12 per family under the task budgets, censoring reported with the pilot's Wilson intervals, and no task failing on both families; a failing task's cause is a message or docs item fixed before the rc, or a task defect fixed in the task. Free and local: `offline.py`'s oracles in CI (already), and **subscription episodes**: the twelve tasks run through the owner's Claude Code and Codex seats as delegated agents under the harness's sandbox rules and tool allowlist, judged by a new `runner.py` `judge` mode that verdicts a submission directory produced outside the runner with `offline.py`'s rules and records family, condition and whether the skill was loaded from the client's transcript (Sol). Paid: the §17.182 (4) paired Anthropic run with the remaining $5.80 is the only paid run, on a fresh key with the owner's written approval; no OpenAI key is funded, so the second family's evidence is the subscription episodes, labelled as such. (4) **Soundness campaign of stated size** replaces "one release cycle": (a) the six targets at the nightly 600 s plus a `workflow_dispatch` campaign input on `fuzz.yml` (`max_total_time` as an input, corpora carried as artifacts) totalling at least 24 CPU-hours per target on GitHub-hosted runners, never on the reference machine, whose windows stay quiet; (b) one new differential target `exec`: well-typed programs generated from `tests/semantics` seeds by a typed mutation set (literals, operators, arm and handler order, `List` and `Map` operations), run through the JS backend, the WASM backend on the Node host and the LIR evaluator, comparing stdout, trace and defect code, message and site, with any divergence a soundness bug (Opus); (c) the same programs under `sakura verify`: an obligation the solver proves whose check the LIR evaluator's replay (V4b's machinery) reaches in failure is a verifier soundness bug. Exit: seven consecutive nightly runs with no new crash or divergence on every target, the campaign hours reached, every finding fixed with a `tests/ui` or `tests/semantics` regression, and zero open issues labelled `soundness` at the rc tag and again at the 1.0 tag; a finding during the rc soak restarts the seven nights. (5) **Envelope**: held at the two latest tagged releases before 1.0, `v0.2.0` and `v1.0.0-rc.1`, each at an owner quiet window with `bench envelope` over both suites (§17.213 (2)), a miss carried only under a named decision (§17.152 (2)); `v0.1.0`'s window, the M4 exit window, is the baseline; back-to-back releases count and no calendar wait exists. (6) **Stability guarantees** at `v0.2.0`, as RFC 0001 since STABILITY.md routes its promises through RFCs, each with the first 0.x that honours it: source compatibility within a major; diagnostic codes never reused, a withdrawn code staying listed as reserved in `codes.rs`; `--json` schemas versioned and additive within a major; the ABI MAJOR rule (ABI §0); the deprecation path STABILITY already states (two minors and six months); MSRV equal to `rust-version` in `compiler/Cargo.toml`, tested by a CI `cargo +<msrv> check --locked` job and raised only in a minor with a notes line. Fable drafts, Astra lands, the owner accepts at the tag. (7) **Packages are out of 1.0**, reversing §17.168's rejection: 1.0 programs are single packages over std; the `[dependencies]` table and the lock file's name are reserved, and a 1.0 compiler refuses a `[dependencies]` table with a named `V` diagnostic (allocated at 0.2.0) that says packages arrive in a later minor; packages then land in 1.x as an additive change, with their own research and plan as §17.168 (5) said. (8) **M5 for 1.0 is the native backend behind the experimental flag** (`m5-native.md` N0b, N1a, N1b, N2, N5 and the §17.173 lanes) with the parity matrix `{js, wasm, native} × {ubuntu, macos}` green; STABILITY labels `--target native` experimental at 1.0, outside the guarantees, as §17.175 (6)'s flag already planned; N3, N4, the §17.177 representation windows and `m5-native.md` §5's native rows for B and C are **M5b, after 1.0**: none changes source semantics or a stable contract, and the ABI versions they need are MINORs. §14.3's M5 line is split, not dropped; restoring N3 and N4 to 1.0 costs about three weeks and is the owner's call. (9) **The spec stays in 1.0, scoped**: `docs/spec/` with the grammar written from `sakura_parse` and pinned by the parse and `fmt` fixtures, the diagnostic contract generated from `codes.rs` as the website's code page already is, `runtime/rt/ABI.md` promoted by reference as the annex, and static and dynamic semantics as fixture-indexed prose where every normative sentence cites its `tests/semantics` fixture and a sentence without one is marked informative (Astra); complete at the rc. Why: the owner's decision; the agents are the language's first users and two model families are the independent population that exists; a measured campaign plus a consecutive-clean streak is the evidence a calendar cycle only proxies for; packages have no consumer and the additive path keeps 1.0's promise whole; native cannot be stable without the window numbers §17.177 still waits for. Rejected: dropping the spec from 1.0 (the guarantees would bind to nothing); counting `bench/programs` as the corpus (written with compiler access and for speed); fuzzing on the reference machine; a paid second-family run (no key, no budget); native stable at 1.0; Blacksmith for the campaign (no organisation). Performance: none. Changed: plan `road-to-1.0.md` (rewritten table; §6 kept under its number). Impact: the owner (the items §17.226 lists), Opus, Sol, Astra, Fable as named.
226. **Order from M4 exit to 1.0: four tags, the rows per step with agent-days, what runs in parallel, and the owner's items (round 23).** Estimates are agent-days of focused work; weeks are an estimate of wall time with the five agents the machine runs, not dates, and every boundary stays the owner's. **A. M4 exit** (now; about one week): rows 26, 27, 28 (Opus, 2 d), WS-W1 and WS-W2 (Opus, 4 d), row 29 (Opus, 0.5 d), WS-C2 (Opus, 2 d; latency, not a gate), M4-DOCS recheck (Astra, 1 d), the window run and `bench envelope` (Astra, 0.5 d); about 10 d. Owner: the quiet window and bless; the gate signature or a written delegation as for gate 10. **B. `v0.1.0`** (weeks 1–2): release notes naming what is and is not stable (Astra, 1 d), the dry run and the npm path (Sol, 1 d); about 2 d. Owner: an npm token that bypasses 2FA (alpha.1's npm publish is still owed and is P3's start condition), the tag and the three environment approvals. **C. Platforms B and C** (weeks 2–4, beside D and E; §6 rows): P3 (Sol, 2 d), P5 (Sol 3 d, Opus 1 d), P6 (Sol, 1.5 d), P7 (Astra 1 d, Sol 0.5 d); about 9 d. Owner: P4 on a Raspberry Pi, P8 on Windows 11 and an Intel Mac. **D. M5-core** (weeks 2–5): N0b 2 d, N1a 5 d, N1b 6 d, N2 10 d, N5 3 d (Opus), the §17.173 lanes 2 d (Sol); about 28 d on three Opus lines (N1a then N2; N1b beside; N0b then N5 beside); its gate is the native parity matrix. **E. Evidence** (weeks 2–6, beside C and D): six dogfood programs at 1.5 d each (three Claude, three Codex), the `judge` mode (Sol, 2 d) and the subscription episodes (2 d), the `exec` differential target (Opus, 5 d) with the campaign on CI, the spec (Astra, 12 d), RFC 0001 and STABILITY (Fable 1 d, Astra 1 d), the `[dependencies]` refusal (Opus, 1 d); about 33 d. **F. `v0.2.0`** (week 5): C shipped, D landed, RFC 0001 accepted, the spec's grammar and diagnostics published. Owner: window, bless, tag. **G. `v1.0.0-rc.1`** (week 6): the corpus upgraded (3 d), both families' eval numbers, the seven-night streak, the spec complete, the envelope held at 0.2.0. Owner: window, bless, tag; the fresh key and written approval if the paired run happens; the signing certificate and notarization credentials so the rc's macOS archives ship signed (an unsigned rc states it plainly; 1.0 ships signed). **H. `v1.0.0`** (week 7): a seven-day rc soak with no P1 defect, no `soundness` issue and no contract change; a contract change is rc.2 and a new soak. Owner: the tag. Totals: about 95 agent-days; about seven weeks if the owner's items land as they come due. Critical path: N1a → N2, then the three windows (A, F, G). Not on the path and not gates: DX8 (plugin after npm, playground, Linguist), N3, N4, the §17.177 windows, crates.io (§17.157 (3); revisited at the rc, default deferred). Why: everything in E starts the day `v0.1.0` tags and runs beside D, so the wall time is D's plus the soak; three windows is the minimum the envelope rule needs. Rejected: serialising E after D (doubles the wall time for nothing); an rc before the streak (the streak is the soundness evidence). Changed: plan `road-to-1.0.md` (the table and timeline). Impact: the owner (windows ×3, blesses ×3, gate signature, npm token, tags ×4, Pi and Windows and Intel Mac acceptance, signing credentials, eval key and approval), Opus, Sol, Astra, Fable as named.
227. **Platform acceptance without owner hardware: a fresh GitHub-hosted runner installing from the published channels is the "fresh host"; three claim levels for the docs; device runs are optional confirmation (owner facts of 2026-10-19; §17.180 (6); §17.226 C; ADR 0003; round 24).** Facts: the owner has no Raspberry Pi and no Intel Mac, has a Windows machine they would rather not depend on now, and asked for the acceptances to be done "some other way or later"; P3 landed (`b8e70b5c`: ARM64 archive and npm package built and dry-run-smoked on `ubuntu-22.04-arm`, CI suites and parity on `ubuntu-24.04-arm`); `release.yml`'s `smoke-install` job already installs from the dry run's `dist/` offline, and `release.py smoke` already runs the P4 list (`new cli`, `check`, `test`, `build`, `run`, `run --target wasm`, the MCP handshake, `setup --apply` twice, the no-Node refusal) against an extracted archive in a clean `HOME`. Decision: (1) **The fresh host is a fresh GitHub-hosted runner of the target's OS and CPU, installing from the real channels after publication**: a new `workflow_dispatch` workflow `.github/workflows/verify-published.yml` (Sol) with one input, `version`, checks out tag `v<version>` and runs, per matrix row, each channel's documented install command against the public endpoint (the release's `install.sh` by its download URL with the version pinned, `npm install --global --ignore-scripts sakuralang@<version>` from the registry, `brew tap icantcodefyi/sakuralang && brew install sakuralang` where the formula serves the host), then `release.py accept --sakura <installed path> --commit <tag commit>`, a new subcommand that is `smoke` without the archive-layout check and takes an installed binary; `--version` must print the tag's version and commit, which ties the public bytes to the tag. The matrix is hand-written in the workflow and its plan step fails if a `packaging/targets.json` target has no row: `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` on `ubuntu-22.04` (the glibc floor host; installer, npm, brew); `aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu` on `ubuntu-22.04-arm` (floor; installer with `gh` attestation, npm) and in a `debian:bookworm` arm64 container on `ubuntu-24.04-arm` with `--memory 1800m` (installer by its no-`gh` sha-only path, npm; Node 26 installed exactly as the install page tells a Pi user; this is the Raspberry Pi OS 64-bit userland, glibc 2.36, with a Pi 4's 2 GB in mind, not Pi hardware); `aarch64-apple-darwin` on `macos-15` (installer, npm, brew); at C, `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` on `windows-2025` (`install.ps1` by URL, npm; `fmt --check` clean on a fresh project committed and re-checked-out under `core.autocrlf=true`; an `initialize` round trip over stdio against `sakura lsp` in place of "the VS Code LSP", the extension's `sakura.exe` spawn rule staying a unit test; `setup --apply` is the Claude or Codex MCP configuration) and `x86_64-apple-darwin` on `macos-15-intel` (installer, npm, brew). `windows-11-arm` is not an inventory target and gets no row. (2) **Intel fallback**: when GitHub retires `macos-15-intel`, the row moves to `macos-15` under Rosetta (`softwareupdate --install-rosetta --agree-to-license` when `arch -x86_64 /usr/bin/true` fails) and smokes the archive only, with `install.sh` gaining `--target TRIPLE` so the Rosetta check of §17.180 (3) can be overridden on purpose; npm and brew on Intel then read "untested since <date of the last Intel-runner run>" in the docs, since Rosetta would need an x64 Node and an x64 Homebrew. (3) **Claim levels**, the only vocabulary the install page, STABILITY.md's target sentence and release notes use: **supported**: a `targets.json` entry whose CI lanes run the suites and parity and whose published-install smoke was green on a hosted runner of that OS and CPU for the release named (an install or run failure there is a P1 release defect, fixed forward in a new version per `docs/release.md`); **expected to work**: the same binary on an OS the project cannot run on a hosted runner, stated with what was tested, for example "Linux ARM64 is tested on GitHub's Ubuntu ARM64 runners and in a Debian bookworm arm64 container; Raspberry Pi 4 and 5 with 64-bit Raspberry Pi OS (bookworm, glibc 2.36) are expected to work and have not been run on Pi hardware by the project"; **community-confirmed**: appended to an expected-to-work line when a report through the new `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/platform_report.yml` (fields: `sakura --version`, `uname -a` or `winver`, `/etc/os-release`, `node --version`, the P4 list's outcome) shows the whole list passing, as "confirmed on <device>, <OS build>, <version> (#issue)"; never "verified on a Raspberry Pi" or "tested on Windows 11" unless a person did it. (4) **Owner hardware is optional confirmation, never a gate**: a device run the owner chooses to do is recorded in the release close-out as a confirmation under (3)'s wording; no stage, tag or row waits for it; the Windows machine is not required at C. (5) **Serialisation relaxed**: C's packaging (P5, P6) may start as soon as a stage's packaging has landed and its dry-run smoke is green on every row (true for B now), since acceptance no longer waits for a person; a stage's **exit** is the first published-install smoke green on every row of the first release that carries its archives (B exits at `v0.1.0`). The published smoke runs for every tag from `v0.1.0` on and is a close-out item in `docs/release.md` §6; the rc and `v1.0.0` both need it green on every row (stage 8). ADR 0003's consequence "accepted on a fresh host" reads as this entry; the ADR gains an amendment note. Why: the only acceptance evidence the project can produce itself is a fresh runner installing from the public channels, and that evidence is stronger than one owner's device because it is repeatable per tag; the Pi's specific risks (glibc floor, Debian userland without `gh`, little memory, Node not packaged) each have a hosted stand-in. Rejected: QEMU for Pi hardware (no new information over the ARM runner, and §17.175 (7) forbids its numbers); buying or borrowing devices as a gate (the owner's time); a Windows ARM64 row (not a target); deferring B's exit to a device run (indefinite); folding the published smoke into `release.yml` after the publish jobs (channels publish on separate approvals and may be deferred, so a dispatch after close-out is the honest moment). Performance: none; ARM and Intel macOS runners are free for public repositories. Changed: plan `road-to-1.0.md` (stages 3, 7, 8; §6 rows P3–P8 and the claims paragraph), ADR 0003 (amendment note), plan `m5-native.md` §5 (pointers), `docs/release.md` §6 (close-out line). Impact: Sol (`verify-published.yml`, `release.py accept`, `install.sh --target`, the issue template), Astra (install page per OS with (3)'s wording; STABILITY sentence with Sol), the owner (nothing required; optional confirmations). **Round 27:** the documented npm command is the plain `npm install --global sakuralang@<version>`; the packages ship no lifecycle scripts, `release.py` refuses to pack one that does, and only the release and verify workflows keep `--ignore-scripts` as a guard (`c391bce4`; §17.246).
228. **Round-24 owner confirmations folded into the plan: N3 and N4 stay out of 1.0, one quiet window after the last M4 commit, npm alpha.1 published, signing credentials stored (owner, 2026-10-19).** Decision: (1) The owner confirmed §17.225 (8)'s default: N3, N4 and the §17.177 windows are M5b, after 1.0; the "three weeks to restore" option is closed for 1.0. (2) **One window for M4**: the quiet-window read of §17.109's ceilings and `bench envelope` happens once, after the last M4 code commit (rows 26–29, WS-W1, WS-W2 and WS-C2 landed, M4-DOCS rechecked); no interim reads; the commit the window reads is the M4 exit commit and `v0.1.0`'s baseline; an M4 code change after the window that touches a benchmarked path reopens the question for the owner, anything else does not. (3) **npm `sakuralang@0.1.0-alpha.1` is published** with its two platform packages under the `latest` and `alpha` dist-tags; stage 1's "npm publish owed" and the "npm token" owner items are closed; `latest` moves to `v0.1.0` when it publishes, and P3's start condition is met (it landed). (4) **Signing and notarization credentials are stored in the `release-github` environment**; Sol wires the macOS signing and notarization steps into `release.yml` (certificate from the environment, `codesign` with hardened runtime, `notarytool submit --wait`, `stapler`, all under the existing `release-github` approval, a dry run skipping them and saying so); the owner item at stage 7 is closed and the agent item is a `release.yml` change before `v0.2.0` so the rc ships signed. Changed: plan `road-to-1.0.md` (stages 1, 2, 7, 8; timeline). Impact: Sol (signing steps), Astra (window run), the owner (none new).
229. **N1a's five readings: ABI 1.4.0's text with `0x010200` is confirmed and the constant moves straight to `0x010400` at N0b; the static-block image builder moves to `sakura_lir::image` as N2's first commit; a Rust-trait host with no C embedding API is the host through N2 and at 1.0; the blocking pool keeps its configuration until N1b, its first user, and goes if N1b has none; host `Err` text follows one errno table on both hosts and a sleep is honoured for any duration on both (plan `m5-native.md` N1a completion note; `compiler/REQUESTS.md` 2026-10-19 N1a; `cb3ed074`; round 25).** Facts: ABI §0's 1.4.0 bullet already says the constant stays `0x010200` until 1.3.0 is in and becomes `0x010400` then; `sakura_host_native/tests/eval/mod.rs` copies `sakura_codegen_wasm`'s `Statics`, `ops_table`, `native_slots`, `HOST_HANDLERS` and `NATIVE_CODES` (private), and `sakura_codegen_wasm` depends on `sakura_lir`, which the eval harness and `sakura_codegen_native` depend on too; the native host is a Rust trait with copy-in/copy-out and no import re-enters; `SAKURA_BLOCKING_THREADS` configures a pool nothing calls; `hostMessage` (JS) and `host_message` (Rust) map only `ENOENT`/`NotFound` to `not found` and echo their platform's text otherwise; Node's `setTimeout` past 2^31 − 1 ms fires after 1 ms with a warning, where Tokio sleeps the duration. Decision: (1) **Confirmed, no renumbering.** The constant never reads `0x010300`: N0b's commit sets `ABI_VERSION` to `0x010400` in one step and §0's 1.4.0 bullet says so (this round, the designer); a host compares MAJOR and MINOR, and no 1.3.0 host or module will ever exist to be confused. Renumbering would rewrite a landed §1b and three commits' text for a number nobody reads before N2 emits a module. (2) **The builder is shared through `sakura_lir`**: a new public module `sakura_lir/src/image.rs` holds `Statics`, `ops_table`, `native_slots`, `HOST_HANDLERS`, `NATIVE_CODES` and the code order the WASM linker and the native `CODES` dispatch table both read, so the two backends and the eval harness build one image from one definition; `sakura_codegen_wasm/src/data.rs` becomes a caller or goes; the eval harness deletes its copy. `sakura_lir::layout` is the layout table (slots and rows) and is not the place. Owner Opus, N2's first commit (small, mechanical); acceptance: every WASM fixture and parity run byte-identical, one definition of each table in the workspace, the eval harness green without its copy. (3) **Acceptable through N2 and at 1.0.** `--target native` is experimental at 1.0 (§17.225 (8)) and its only host is `sakura_host_native`, linked into the executable `sakura build --target native` produces; §1b's C prototypes are the runtime ↔ generated-code contract, not an embedding surface. A C embedding API (an import vtable, an instance constructor callable from C) is an M5b row with its own ABI MINOR and a §1c when a consumer exists; N1b, N2 and N5 assume none. (4) **The pool stays as configured** and `SAKURA_BLOCKING_THREADS` stays undocumented outside ABI.md until N1b, whose `Sql.sqlite` worker is the stated user; if N1b's services end on dedicated threads and nothing calls `spawn_blocking`, N1b's last commit deletes the pool and the variable (code with no user is not kept). No N1a change. (5) **Two rules on both hosts.** (a) *Host `Err` text follows one table* (ABI §8.2, beside the `fs_*` rows): the runtime's own messages (`not found`, `escapes the directory`, `not a relative path`, `no such directory`, `this directory has no host access`, the `not in the manifest's fs.<kind> list` message) are the contract as today; an OS error maps by class, never by its text: not found → `not found`, permission denied → `permission denied`, already exists → `already exists`, is a directory → `is a directory`, not a directory → `not a directory`; any other class answers the host's own text, which parity does not compare and no fixture prints (§17.216's rule for a host failure's text). Node maps `e.code` (`ENOENT`; `EACCES`, `EPERM`; `EEXIST`; `EISDIR`; `ENOTDIR`), Rust maps `e.kind()`; `hostMessage` and `host_message` are the two places. (b) *A sleep is honoured for any duration*: `clock_sleep_ms` on Node chains timers of at most 2^31 − 1 ms until the duration is spent (a cancel clears the current one), so a `Duration` used as "never" in a `timeout` waits as the program said, as native already does through Tokio; the virtual clock is unchanged. No clamp (a silent change of what the program asked) and no defect (a code for a case with a correct answer). Owner Opus, one commit with N1b: `runtime/js/sakura_runtime.js`, `sakura_host_native/src/fs.rs`, the `host_fs_env` fixture gains `is a directory` and, under `#[cfg(unix)]`, `permission denied`; a runtime test spies `setTimeout` for a 2^31 ms sleep and cancels it; `runtime/js/ABI.md` erratum; CHANGELOG under Changed (the JS `Err` for a permission error was Node's `EACCES: permission denied, open '…'`). Not an M4 exit item and not on a benchmarked path: it lands with N1b and does not reopen the window. Why: parity compares stdout and an `Err` a program prints is stdout; one table beats two hosts each echoing their platform. Rejected: Node's text as the contract (native cannot produce it without a table of Node's strings); a clamp on native; a `D` code for a long sleep; renumbering 1.3.0/1.4.0. Performance: none. Changed: ABI §0 (one sentence), plan `m5-native.md` (the readings answered, N2's first commit, N1b's acceptance), REQUESTS. Impact: Opus (N2, N1b).
230. **WS-W1 and WS-W2 as built are ratified; the three frame cliffs they leave (a clause that can abort, a lambda that performs, `deltablue`'s closure-calling entries) are M5 work as WS-W4, attributed before a fix is named, and the window reads them as they are (`881b7a69`, `6bf515d6`; ABI 1.2.4, 1.2.5; plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.8 rows 21–22; §17.211 (1), (2); round 25).** Facts (WS-W1): the `with` yields the activation id as a pseudo-local after `$site`; `Bound` is an instruction (the cancellation test and the trace record, yielding the clause's closure) before a direct `Call` of the clause; frame code does not enter a twin that can abort when it is a clause or reaches a bound perform, since its state write or trace record would repeat; a twin's state 3 is cancellation; `tests/parity.rs` holds every fixture's WASM run to its `--no-direct` run and caught the duplicated trace records of an aborted attempt; `handled_d1`/`_d4`/`_d16` 1,005 → 4 frames, `handled_suspend` 1,008 → 7, `handled_fwd` 2,005 unchanged. (WS-W2): the callback-taking functions are std Sakura code, so their twins are the native loop and `runtime/rt` is unchanged (no `dispatch_twin`); lambdas and adapters get closure-convention twins entered through `call_indirect` and a twin table in the static block; a closure without a twin (a lambda that performs) abandons the region as a gauge abort does, counted in `restarts`; a lambda reaching a bound perform is left out of the twin table so no trace record precedes an abandon; `contracts_plain` 1,121 → 4, `pipeline` 2,508 → 4, `shapes` 5,010 → 4, `map_set` 2,005 → 4, `nbody` 7,005 → 4, `deltablue` 7,921 → 1,871; dups and drops unchanged except `deltablue` (+1,144 each, 0.4%) and `handled_reentry` (+6, +5). Decision: (1) **Every deviation ratified as built**, one reading each: the activation id as a pseudo-local (the `with` owns the activation and the clause needs it); `Bound` before the `Call` (the cancellation point and the trace record stay one instruction that runs exactly once before the clause, the invariant the WS-P2 packet asks the owner to check); the entry rule (an aborted attempt would repeat a state write or a trace record, which the parity test showed); state 3 as cancellation (the fourth state ABI §5 now names); parity to `--no-direct` as the oracle (the frame path is the reference semantics); no runtime loop for callbacks (a twin of Sakura code is the loop); abandon-and-restart for a twinless closure (§17.211 (2)'s frame fallback, counted in `restarts`); `deltablue`'s +1,144 dups and drops (argument holds on entries that can abort, the price of the fallback, inside its WASM carry). `handled_fwd` at 2,005 is a forwarded perform, never WS-W1's. (2) **The three cliffs are M5, as WS-W4 in `performance-r1.md` §9**, gating nothing at M4: (a) a bound perform whose clause can abort runs on frames, and a twin for it needs the clause's state write to be deferred to the attempt's commit or checkpointed, a runtime rule rather than a codegen change; (b) a lambda that performs has no twin, so every entry into a region that calls it holds its arguments, abandons and restarts; the fix is a twin that can yield, the frame protocol inside a twin, which is WS-W3's territory and shares its attribution; (c) `deltablue`'s 1,871 frames at n = 100 are instances of (b) (the Planner's callbacks perform) and (a); at 131–200 ns a frame that is under 0.4 ms of a run, and `deltablue` is carried on WASM as allocation-bound already (§17.211 (5)), so the frames are inside the carry. Order: WS-W3's attribution first (§17.218 (2)); then (b) if the `wasm::rt` bucket is the frame's and a yielding twin removes it, else (a); each with the WS-W3 protocol before and after and `frames` as the check that the path changed; a fix that does not move its bucket is not landed (§17.201's rule). Ranked against M4: none of the three is an M4 item, because each needs a runtime rule the ABI does not have and the exit commit is row 29's. (3) **The window reads WS-W1 and WS-W2's state as is**: a miss on a program whose frames are (2)'s is carried under §17.152 (2) with WS-W4 named as its decision. Why: the design asked for twins on the common path and got them (seven programs at ≤ 7 frames); the residue is the frame protocol's cost, which §17.218 made M5's; pulling it into M4 reopens `func.rs` after row 29 and delays the one window for a number inside a carry. Rejected: a twin that enters an aborting clause anyway (repeats a state write); WS-W4 in M4; reading `deltablue`'s frames as a block. Performance: none now. Changed: plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.8 rows 21–22 (ratified), `performance-r1.md` §9 (WS-W4), `m5-native.md` §3 (one row). Impact: Astra (attribution), Opus (M5).
231. **Rows 26, 27 and 28 as built are ratified: a guarded run chunked at 256 nested blocks into a balanced tree of joins; `serveHttp`'s open flag on both backends, the `ended` outcome with one `D090 "server stopped"` record, `runMain`'s optional host; the trap's `D090` names the function and the PC (`fa8d954b`, `9ad401af`, `f02c8845`; §17.220, §17.223; ABI 1.2.3; round 25).** (1) **Row 26.** §17.220 (2) allowed `matchc.rs` or `opt.rs`; `matchc::leaf` puts the rows after a failed guard in the test's `else` when the join's one jump is outside any `Handle` or `Try` (the inlining at construction, where the join is made); past 256 nested blocks the rest of the run starts a chunk and the chunks are a balanced tree of joins, so Core stays inside the snapshot's 1,024-block bound (§17.200 (5)) and JS nests log₂ of the chunks; a sibling chain was tried and failed (past the emitter's 64 inline joins every `&&` join became an arrow per row); `deep_ir.rs` runs 3,000 rows in a direct loop, a performing loop and `&&`-guarded, and 20,000 outside one; `match_guard_run_regions` pins the `try`/`with` joins; only `match_literal_switch` changed (three trampolines gone, 144 → 141 blocks); counters unchanged. **Ratified**, with the reading that the chunk tree is the §17.200 (5) bound applied, not a new limit: a run of any length runs, and its nesting is log₂ of its length over 256. (2) **Rows 27 and 28.** The open flag lives in `serveHttp`, shared code, so on the JS backend too a request that finishes arriving after the stop is `503` and never reaches `run` (the "JS fixtures unchanged except `500` → `503` by the same rule" clause covers it); `close(o)` answers the waiting requests with the outcome `ended` (`500`) and writes one `D090 "server stopped"` when `o` is not a defect, a defect's record staying `runMain`'s single write; `runMain` takes an optional host as `runTests` does; the trap message carries the function (from the names section, else its index) and the PC (`trap: unreachable in boom at 0x1e`), which §8.6 required; `serve_listen` is never completed; `sakura test` continues on a fresh instance. **Ratified**; `ended` is the name for §17.223 (3)'s "the record of what ended the instance". (3) The owner's review the three commits request (heap exhaustion `500` → `503`; a request after the stop `503` instead of invoked; a request waiting at the end `500` instead of no answer; a trap `500`, then `503`, exit 70) is one item of the M4 exit checklist (§17.234 (3)). Why: each as-built choice is the design's rule in the nearest place and none widens a contract. Rejected: nothing. Changed: plan §3.8 rows 26–28 (done, readings). Impact: none.
232. **RFC 0001's open details, settled so code can start: `V8005 dependencies_not_supported`; `schema_version` per record kind is the JSON contract and a checked-in field baseline with an append-only history check is its gate; cross-version ABI fixtures from `v0.1.0`'s bytes; `deprecations.toml` with an empty table and a release check; the previous tag's fixtures as the compatibility corpus; the stable-interface inventory in STABILITY.md pinned by a CLI surface baseline (`c2400206`; `d7b63f0b`, `d1060573`; §17.225 (6), (7); plan `road-to-1.0.md` stage 5; round 25).** Facts: `V8001`–`V8004` and `V8020` are allocated, `V8005` is free; records already carry `schema_version` and `kind` (`sakura_diagnostics/src/json.rs` `SCHEMA_VERSION`; `audit` 1.0, `manifest` 1.1, `verify`'s summary, the `test` summary 1.0); `compiler/diagnostics/codes.txt` with `check_history.py` is the append-only shape the `diagnostic-codes` job runs; the `msrv` job is required; `tools/release/release.py` has `smoke` and `accept`; `codes.rs` has no deprecation; no stable inventory exists. Decision: (a) **`V8005 dependencies_not_supported`, class PACKAGE, confirmed.** Title "packages are not supported yet"; message `this project declares [dependencies]; Sakura <version> compiles one package over std, and packages arrive in a later minor release`; explain text: remove the table, do not vendor. Raised by `Manifest::parse` when the `dependencies` key is present (empty, inline `dependencies = {}` or populated), before anything else runs; primary span the key; `--json` as any `V` code; no fetch and the lock file untouched. The UI harness gains a project-shaped fixture directory (`tests/ui/V8005_dependencies_table/` with its `sakura.toml`, as `V1030_import_cycle/` is a directory); cases: empty table, populated, inline, with another unknown key (both reported), and malformed TOML (its parse diagnostic only). Owner Opus, 1 d, before `v0.2.0`; `codes.rs`, `codes.txt`, §12.3's row, `manifest.rs`, the website code page regenerated. (b) **JSON contracts.** The form is what exists: every record carries `kind` and `schema_version` `MAJOR.MINOR` per kind (the diagnostic record, `check`'s summary, `audit`, `manifest`, `verify`'s summary, the `test` summary and rows; Opus inventories every `--json` emitter and any record without the two fields gains them at its kind's 1.0); MINOR is additive, MAJOR is a new contract and is refused inside a compiler major (no exception path in the check; a breaking revision waits for a compiler major and its own RFC). The gate: `compiler/contracts/json.txt`, one line per `kind schema_version field type required|optional`, appended by hand; a Rust test `sakura_cli/tests/json_contracts.rs` runs every `--json` command over named fixtures (a success, a diagnostic and a failure per command), derives `kind → field → type` from the output and fails on a required field the output lacks, a field the baseline does not list (so an addition is a baseline line in the same commit, which review reads as additive) or a differing type; `compiler/contracts/check_json.py`, `check_history.py`'s shape in the same job (renamed `contracts`), compares the file with its version at the merge base: a removed line, a changed type, optional → required or a MAJOR bump fails, and an added line needs a MINOR bump of its kind in the same diff. Text fields (`message`, `title`, `explain`) are listed as `string` only. Owners: Opus (the inventory, the test, the baseline; 1.5 d), Sol (the script and the job; 0.5 d); before `v0.2.0`. (c) **Cross-version ABI fixtures.** Under `compiler/runtime/rt/tests/fixtures/abi/<abi-version>/`, one small module per ABI version a release shipped (`1.2.0` from `v0.1.0`, built by that release's `sakura build --target wasm` and committed with its `sakura --version` line in a README); `wasm_host.test.js` loads each with the current host: same MAJOR and a MINOR at most the host's runs and prints the expected output; a module stamped a greater MINOR (made by rewriting the global of a committed fixture, so no release is needed) is `V8003` before any `LinkError`; `task_free` absent and present on the same program leave `live_objects` equal after one run with only the retained count differing. A required-export refusal and a changed-signature MAJOR classification are tests over the ABI's own tables, not fixtures: `abi.rs`'s comparison of the host and the shared revision gains the export list. Owner Opus, 1 d, after `v0.1.0` tags (the first fixture needs its bytes), before `v0.2.0`. (d) **Deprecation metadata.** `compiler/deprecations.toml`, one `[[deprecation]]` per entry (`feature`, `code` (a `W` code), `since` version and date, `replacement` or `reason`, `earliest_removal` version and date written out as two minors and six months, `removed` absent or a version); empty at 0.2.0. Checks: a `codes.rs` test that every `W` code titled `deprecated_*` has an entry and every entry's code exists; `release.py prepare` (the candidate step, `docs/release.md` §2) refuses a tag that marks an entry `removed` before `earliest_removal` on either axis or outside a major bump, and refuses a patch version whose `rust-version` differs from the previous tag's, the one mechanical check RFC 0001's MSRV clause lacked. Owner Sol, 0.5 d, before `v0.2.0`. (e) **The corpus is the previous tag's fixtures; no new corpus.** A CI job `compat` (nightly and on tags) checks out the previous tag's `compiler/tests/semantics`, `compiler/tests/ui` and `compiler/examples` and runs them under the current compiler against the old expected outputs (stdout, codes, exit codes) on `js` and `wasm`; every difference is a line in `compiler/tests/compat/allow.txt` as `<fixture> <reason> <CHANGELOG anchor>`, and an unlisted difference fails. The dogfood corpus (§17.225 (2)) stays the separate installed-binary gate. The job exists before `v0.2.0` and compares `v0.1.0` with `main`, which is where the 0.1 → 0.2 migration lines get written. Owner Sol (the job, 1 d); Opus if the harness needs an expected-from-directory flag (0.5 d). (f) **Inventory and experimental labels.** STABILITY.md gains one table (surface, item, status `stable` | `experimental` | `internal`, first honouring version) over CLI subcommands and flags, exit codes, `sakura.toml` keys, the `SAKURA_*` environment variables the compiler and hosts read, `--json` kinds (from (b)), LSP extensions and MCP tools; it is pinned by `compiler/contracts/cli.txt`, generated from the clap definitions by a `sakura_cli` test (subcommands, flags, exit codes) and checked append-only by (b)'s script; an experimental item carries `experimental` in its `--help` line and in the table, and the test fails for a flag in neither state. `--target native` is `experimental`, with the `native` feature's refusal already pinned. Owners Astra (prose and the first table, 1 d), Sol (the baseline test and check, 0.5 d), Opus (`experimental` in `--help`, 0.25 d); the first table at `v0.2.0`, complete before `v1.0.0-rc.1`. **Ranked**: 1 (a) `V8005` (Opus; the only item that changes what a program sees); 2 (b) JSON (Opus then Sol; the promise is "from 0.2.0" and needs the baseline at the tag); 3 (d) deprecations and the MSRV patch check (Sol; small, before the tag); 4 (e) `compat` (Sol; runs against `v0.1.0` as soon as it tags); 5 (c) ABI fixtures (Opus; after `v0.1.0` tags); 6 (f) the inventory (Astra, Sol, Opus; first table at 0.2.0, complete at the rc). RFC 0001's unresolved questions: the boundary (0.2.x within a minor line, full within a major from 1.0) is confirmed as the designer's reading pending the owner's acceptance at the tag; `V8005` confirmed free today. Why: each gate reuses the one mechanism the repository already runs (an append-only text baseline with a git-history check), so six promises cost one script shape; what a program sees goes first. Rejected: JSON Schema files per command (a second language to keep in sync with the emitters; the field baseline is the contract and the test is the schema); a separate historical source corpus (the fixtures at each tag are one, already written and already expected); a deprecation check in `codes.rs` alone (a removal is a release event, so the release tool checks it). Performance: none. Changed: RFC 0001 (a round-25 pointer under its unresolved questions), plan `road-to-1.0.md` stage 5 (the six rows with owners), §12.3 (the `V8005` row lands with its code). Impact: Opus, Sol, Astra as named; the owner accepts at the tag.
233. **The JS host's write race: open the resolved path with `O_NOFOLLOW` and read or write through the descriptor; intermediate components, `Sql.sqlite` and Windows keep a documented limit; row 30, before `v0.1.0` tags, not an exit-gate item (`7c5a6dfd`; §17.159 (6); ABI §8.3; round 25).** Facts: `inside` resolves with `realpathSync`, refuses a dangling link by `lstat` and returns the real path; `fsResult` then calls `writeFileSync(path)` or `openSync(path, "rwa"[mode])`, the cassette append calls `openSync(path, "a")` and `Sql.sqlite` calls `DatabaseSync(path)`, each a separate call, so a link planted between the check and the open is followed. The racer is a concurrent process with write access inside the grant and less authority than the Sakura process; a Sakura program cannot race itself, since the check and the open run synchronously inside one host call. The native host opens through a directory handle (`cap-std`) and has no window; Node has no `openat`. Decision: (1) **Final-component atomicity.** Every open on the host passes numeric flags with `fs.constants.O_NOFOLLOW` (`O_WRONLY|O_CREAT|O_TRUNC` for `fs_write`, `|O_APPEND` for an append and the cassette, `O_RDONLY` for `fs_read` and an `r` open), on the resolved path `inside` returned, and reads or writes through the descriptor (`writeFileSync(fd, data)`, `readFileSync(fd)`); `ELOOP` answers `escapes the directory`. Because the path opened is the resolved one, a legitimate link inside the grant still works (it was resolved before the open) and only a link that appeared after the check is refused. (2) **Intermediate components**: a directory of the resolved path swapped for a link between the check and the open is not caught on the JS host; ABI §8.3 and `runtime/js/ABI.md` state it as the JS host's limit with the threat model above and the native host as the hardened one; the same sentence covers `Sql.sqlite` (`DatabaseSync` takes a path, so its final component keeps the window; a database inside a grant shared with a less-trusted concurrent writer is the stated caveat) and Windows (`O_NOFOLLOW` is undefined there, so `fs.constants.O_NOFOLLOW ?? 0`; creating a link needs a privilege). (3) **Row 30**, Opus, small: `sakura_runtime.js`'s open sites and `hostMessage`'s `ELOOP`; a runtime test that plants the link between the check and the open (a stubbed `realpathSync` that creates the link on its first return) and asserts the refusal and that the target is never created; CHANGELOG under Security; a dated note in `docs/reviews/m3-security-review.md`. It lands before `v0.1.0` tags, is not an M4 exit-gate item (the gate is parity and the envelope; this touches no benchmarked path and does not reopen the window, §17.228 (2)), and the owner's review of `7c5a6dfd` and row 30 is one checklist item (§17.234 (3)). Why: the final component is where the shipped hole was and `O_NOFOLLOW` closes it atomically in a few lines; a per-component walk without `openat` is not atomic either and would be twenty lines that still lose; a documented limit alone leaves a known escalation on the shipped backend. Rejected: `O_NOFOLLOW` on the unresolved path (breaks in-grant links); the walk; the limit alone; blocking the exit on it. Performance: none. Changed: plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.8 row 30; ABI §8.3 and `runtime/js/ABI.md` (with the row). Impact: Opus. **Round 26: `Fs.list`'s directory joins (2)'s limit, names and kinds only (§17.237).**
234. **M4 exit readiness: what remains is row 29, the M4-DOCS recheck and green CI on the exit commit, which is row 29's; WS-P13b moves past the window (WS-P9b-lit landed before row 29 and is inside it); the owner works through `docs/plans/m4-exit-checklist.md` (round 25).** State since §17.224, landed on `main`: rows 26, 27, 28 (`fa8d954b`, `9ad401af`, `f02c8845`), WS-W1 and WS-W2 (`881b7a69`, `6bf515d6`), WS-C2 (`e49aa5c7`) and WS-C1d/WS-C1e (`804379a9`, `d91a260f`), WS-P13c measured (`c2d35d32`) and the `deltablue` rewrite with the `List` sentence (`9565b992`), N1a (`f1680e5c`..`cb3ed074`), P4's workflow and `release.py accept` (`b8cd5f6b`..`cb82b2bd`), the signing steps (`499ab804`), RFC 0001's draft (`c2400206`), the MSRV job (`d7b63f0b`), the code baseline (`d1060573`), the symlink fix (`7c5a6dfd`). In flight: row 29 (Opus). WS-P13c's reading under §17.217 (3): (i) 0.325 of the Map baseline, the rewrite landed; (ii) 52.6% less time than (i), so **WS-P13b's condition fired** and it builds; (iii) 24.4% less than (ii), the window's number for `deltablue`. Decision: (1) **Before the window, in order**: row 29 (Opus; its commit is the M4 exit commit unless a defect fix follows it); the M4-DOCS recheck against that commit (Astra, 1 d: the backend pages for `--target wasm`, the served statuses and the stopped server, the `List` rule, the symlink note, `agents/verify.mdx`'s reuse wording, `roadmap.mdx` against `road-to-1.0.md` stage 2, and CHANGELOG's Unreleased drafted as the `0.1.0` section); `rust`, `parity`, `native`, `msrv`, `diagnostic-codes`, `website` and `ci-required` green on the exit commit, its run URL in the checklist as the gate report did; then the owner idles the machine and Astra runs both suites and `bench envelope` on that commit (§17.228 (2)). (2) **Not before the window**: WS-P13b (`opt/owned.rs` is on `deltablue`'s path, a benchmarked one, so it would reopen the window; it lands after the window as the first perf commit, with its CODEOWNERS review, and is read at the 0.2.0 window); WS-P9b-lit landed as `08f3720b` before row 29 while this round was being written, so it is inside the exit commit and the window reads it; row 30 (not benchmarked; before `v0.1.0` tags, §17.233); the host message table and the sleep chain (with N1b, §17.229 (5)); WS-W4 (M5, §17.230 (2)). (3) **The owner's checklist** is one file, `docs/plans/m4-exit-checklist.md`, one line per item with its link: the WS-P2/WS-P2b packet (awaiting; WS-P2's item 12 alone was approved 2026-10-18), the WS-P9/WS-P9b packet (awaiting), WS-P5's review (closed: approved 2026-10-18 in `6ccce80a`, `performance-r1.md` §8 WS-P5 item 9; no packet exists or is owed), the §2 line-ending rule (P1's owed CODEOWNERS review; §17.196), the served statuses (`9ad401af`, `f02c8845`), the cut-body `Err` (`187a8f80`), the symlink fixes (`7c5a6dfd`, row 30), the quiet window and bless, and the gate signature or its written delegation in §17.159 (1)'s form; each closes by the owner's name and date on its line or by a written delegation, as gate 10 did. (4) **Order after round 25**, ranked: **1** row 29 (Opus; in flight); **2** the M4-DOCS recheck (Astra; after 1); **3** the window (the owner, Astra; after 2); **4** row 30 (Opus; after 1, before `v0.1.0`); **5** N2's first commit, the shared image builder (Opus N2; now); **6** N1b with §17.229 (5) (Opus; now); **7** `V8005` (Opus; after 4); **8** the JSON baseline and check (Opus, then Sol; after 7); **9** deprecations and the MSRV patch check (Sol; now); **10** the `compat` job (Sol; after `v0.1.0` tags); **11** the ABI fixtures (Opus; after `v0.1.0` tags); **12** the inventory (Astra, Sol, Opus; first table at 0.2.0); **13** WS-P13b (Opus; after 3); **14** WS-W3's attribution, then WS-W4 (Astra, then Opus; M5). Collisions: 1 (`func.rs`) and 4 (`sakura_runtime.js`) none; 5 and 6 share `sakura_host_native` (`tests/eval` against `src/fs.rs`, different files); 7 and 8 both touch `sakura_cli` tests and `codes.rs`, 7 first; 13 waits for 3 by rule. Why: the exit gate is parity and the envelope on one commit, so everything that moves a benchmarked path waits behind the window and everything else runs beside it. Rejected: a second window; folding WS-P13b into the exit commit. Performance: WS-P13b is the measured item (after the window). Changed: plans `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.8 (state line, rows 21–22, 27–28, 30, the exit gate), `performance-r1.md` §9 (WS-P13c measured, WS-P9b-lit done, WS-P13b go, WS-W4, order after round 25), `road-to-1.0.md` stages 2 and 5, `m5-native.md`, the new checklist. Impact: everyone as named. **Round 26: the exit commit is `dee11301`; the record, the CI ruling and the window procedure are §17.238.**
235. **WS-P13b as built is ratified with its two deviations: a recursive call-graph component is iterated to a fixpoint instead of widened to shared, and a parameter is owned per call site through a consuming def's owned entry `f$o` rather than by the caller-driven module rule; four conditions before it pushes, after the window (`28e88c7d`..`83cb3e95`, unpushed in the `opus-wsp13b` worktree; §17.201 (3), §17.217 (3b), §17.234 (2); round 26).** Facts (`research/10-evidence/ws-p13b/README.md`, advisory, loaded host): `deltablue` on Node at n = 5000 took 86.86 ms before and 42.72 ms after (medians of 41 process medians): 51% less time; `after/owned` against WS-P13c's forced arm is 0.997 (quartiles 0.942–1.087), inside §17.201 (3)'s five points; the reference evaluator under `audit_owned` counts 918 → 37,877 in-place updates at n = 100 with the oracle clean, `nbody` 0 → 4 (`offset_momentum` consumes the bodies because its reads borrow them), every other program unchanged; WASM dups and drops do not move because the LIR reads no facts before N0b. *Recursion*: `summaries` runs Tarjan over the call graph; a component that calls itself starts every member at "consumes its first owned-type parameter", recomputes until a round changes nothing (at most 16 rounds) and otherwise drops every summary of the component; `deltablue`'s `satisfy` → `add_propagate` → `incremental_remove` → `incremental_add` cycle needs it; `owned_record_recursive` shows a linear and a mutual recursion in place and a non-linear one (`split`, two calls from one planner) shared, the retained input intact in each. *Parameters*: a top-level def whose parameter, owned at entry, stays owned to every return *consumes* it; a consuming def gets a CPS owned entry `f$o` beside its direct twin, a call whose argument is an owned last use calls that entry and every other call the plain body; no module-wide "every call site" fact exists. The review found and fixed two soundness bugs: a two-level projection (`o.inner.vars` read again after the projection's `vars` was consumed; `up()` now walks every projection link) and a local read only by a `try` handler (the handler's live-in set is live through the body); both are rows of `owned_record_retained`. Decision: (1) **The fixpoint is ratified.** The induction is sound: a member's summary is a promise about the calls that return, each a shallower call whose summary the same round established, and the base is a return path with no recursive call; a component that does not settle promises nothing, which is the widening §17.201 (3) asked for, applied only where it is needed. The non-linear case is shared by the facts themselves (the second use of `p` is not a last use), not by a rule about recursion. (2) **Per-call-site ownership through the owned entry is ratified and the caller-driven rule is withdrawn**: "every call site passes an owned last use" was a sufficient condition for one body; a twin per consuming def makes the fact per site, which is strictly more and needs no module-wide fixpoint; the cost is one more body per consuming def, inside plan `performance-r1.md` §5 item 4's size bar. The `--dump owned` reasons stay `param`, `field` and `tuple`. (3) **Conditions before the push** (Opus, in the same series): (a) a fixture or an `owned.rs` unit test with a component that does not settle in 16 rounds (two members whose consuming parameters alternate), every site `shared(param)`, output unchanged; (b) the five fixtures' `--dump owned` text pinned in `sakura_cli/tests/cli.rs` (the recursive one: `fill`, `even` and `odd` `owned entry owned`, `split`'s sites `shared`); (c) the emitted bytes of `micro.sak` and of the twenty-one bench programs before and after in the commit message, `micro.sak` inside plan §5 item 4's 2× bar; (d) an advisory `bench compile` pair on the loaded host, `check_cold_10k` and `build_10k` within noise, since the pass runs at every emission. (4) **Landing**: after the window, as §17.234 (2) says, the first commit on a benchmarked path; its CODEOWNERS review is the owner's (§17.149: semantics-adjacent) and is the checklist's item after the window; the 0.2.0 window reads `deltablue` with it.
