Why: the number says the inferred facts reach the forced arm; the induction is the standard one for consuming summaries; a twin per site is the mechanism the JS backend already has for direct twins. Rejected: widening recursion to shared (loses `deltablue`, the program the task exists for); the module-wide rule (all-or-nothing, and a module-wide fixpoint for a weaker fact); landing before the window (reopens it). Performance: this is the measured item; the 0.2.0 window reads it. Changed: plan `performance-r1.md` §9 (WS-P13b built and held, the conditions), plan `m5-native.md` §3 (pointer), the checklist. Impact: Opus (conditions, push after the window), the owner (review).

236. **Fan-in into the WASM dispatch loop is bounded per chunk: past about 16,000 `&&`-guarded rows in one function V8's optimising tier aborts on the loop header's input count after the program has printed the right answer; row 31, M5 (`v0.2.0`), not M4 and not `v0.1.0`; a stated limit in the 0.1.0 notes (§17.200 (1), (3); rows 26 and 29; round 26).** Facts: the dispatch (`func.rs`) is one `loop` whose header every block jump re-enters with the dispatch local set; row 26's chunking bounds nesting and row 29's liveness bounds locals, and neither bounds the number of back edges; at 16,000 guarded rows the module validates, the baseline tier runs it and the output is correct, then the optimising tier fails `inputs.size() <= max input_count` on the header's merge (one input per back edge) and the process aborts; 3,000 rows, rows 16 and 26's acceptance, pass; the JS backend has no dispatch loop and is not reported affected. Decision: (1) **The rule**: the number of jumps into one loop header is bounded by a constant, as the nesting is (§17.200 (1)); the emitter's constant is 256. (2) **The mechanism, proposed for the implementer to confirm**: jumps are grouped by the two-level dispatch's chunks (§17.200 (3)); a jump sets the dispatch local and branches to its chunk's stub, one `block` at the loop's top per chunk whose only exit is one `br` to the header, so the header has at most ⌈n/256⌉ + 1 inputs and a stub at most 256; nesting grows by one level; a function of at most 256 jumps is byte-identical. If the stub's extra `br` is measurable on a hot path, it is emitted only past 256 jumps. (3) **Ranking: M5, row 31 of plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.8, lands before `v0.2.0`.** Not M4: the exit commit is `dee11301` and a `func.rs` change reopens the window (§17.228 (2)). Not `v0.1.0`: the same reopening, for a limit no program near the acceptance size reaches and that the release notes can state. It goes beside N2 because `sakura_codegen_native` reads the same LIR and the implementer checks the native backend's block-predecessor behaviour on the same fixture. Acceptance: `deep_ir.rs` runs 16,384 guarded rows and a 16,384-arm sparse literal `match` on Node with the optimising tier forced (`--no-liftoff` or the flag that compiles the function before the output) and on Wasmtime, both backends, expected output and exit 0; 256- and 257-jump functions pin the forms; every function of at most 256 jumps byte-identical across the corpus; `bench_counters` and the WASM bench cells within noise. (4) **The 0.1.0 notes** say: on the WASM backend a single function with more than about 16,000 guarded `match` rows prints its output and then aborts Node's optimising compiler; split the match. Why: an output language's limit on fan-in is the same kind of limit as its limit on nesting and §17.200 (1)'s rule already covers it; the fix is local to the dispatch and a release away. Rejected: a limit on rows (§17.200 rejected it); a relooper now (§17.118's lattice); pulling it into M4 or `v0.1.0`. Performance: one extra `br` per jump, in functions past 256 jumps only. Changed: plan §3.8 row 31 (M5), `m5-native.md` §3 (one row), the 0.1.0 notes (Astra, stage B). Impact: Opus (M5), Astra (notes).

237. **§17.233 (2)'s limit is amended as landed: `Fs.list`'s directory joins the documented window, entry names and kinds only (`8458d102`, row 30; ABI §8.3; round 26).** Facts: `readdir` takes a path and no `O_NOFOLLOW`, so a listed directory swapped for a link between the check and the read lists the target's entry names and kinds, never a file's bytes; every open still refuses a planted link, so a name leaked this way cannot be read or written through the grant; Node has no `readdir` of a descriptor; the native host (`cap-std`) has no window. Decision: **confirmed**; the wording in ABI §8.3, `runtime/js/ABI.md`, the security review's dated note and CHANGELOG stands, and (2)'s limit reads: a directory of the resolved path swapped for a link, `Sql.sqlite`'s database file, `Fs.list`'s directory (entry names and kinds only) and Windows. The racer and the threat model are unchanged. Changed: §17.233 (trailer). Impact: none.

238. **M4's record: the exit commit is `dee11301` (row 29); the parity matrix was green on it on every cell, Windows included; the Windows `rust` failures are test-only and item 3 closes at the first fully green `main` run after it; the M4-DOCS recheck is closed with one item moved to stage B; the window benches `dee11301` in a clean worktree by `docs/release.md` §2; the commits after the exit commit reopen nothing (round 26).** Facts: `dee11301` (`perf(wasm): share locals by liveness past 256 variables, not 256 blocks`) is row 29: the 3,000-`let` function's V8 RSS 600 → 56 MB, 132 of 138 fixtures and 18 of 21 bench programs byte-identical, every changed function one that declared more than 256 locals. Its CI run (`github.com/icantcodefyi/sakuralang/actions/runs/39198384309`): `Parity (js, …)` and `Parity (wasm, …)` green on `ubuntu-latest`, `ubuntu-24.04-arm`, `macos-latest` and `windows-2025`; `Native`, `Rust MSRV`, `Diagnostic code history`, `Website`, `Tree-sitter`, `VS Code` and `Rust` on ubuntu, arm and macOS green; `Rust (windows-2025, 1/2)` and `(2/2)` red on `uris_round_trip` (`sakura_analysis/src/disk.rs`, a `mod tests` assertion that Windows maps `file://host/…` to `\\host\…`) and `agent_dx::runner_offline` (the harness's spend ledger uses `fcntl` locks), so `ci-required` red. The fixes are `7f377624` (Sol: the two test modules, `#[cfg(unix)]` on `runner_offline`, formatter fixture batches under Windows' command-line limit, `release.py`) and `5d2bae6c` (the owner: native Windows paths in the Node runner fixtures, `ci.yml`); at writing their runs were queued and `Rust (windows-2025)` was still red on `261513d6`. The M4-DOCS sweep landed as `98854ac0`, `a44d10cf` and `a35e1d7d` before row 29 (`/docs/changes`, the `List` rule, the served statuses, the symlink note, the roadmap, every runnable fence against `main`); CHANGELOG's Unreleased is not yet drafted as the `0.1.0` section. After the exit commit `main` gained row 30 (`8458d102`), the two Windows fixes, Intel macOS release wiring (`261513d6`, `e20d941c`), the interface inventory (`20984cf1`: STABILITY.md, `compiler/contracts/cli.txt`, a drift test), `V8005` (`61887ae8`, `3c9cc080`, `cd627ab8`: `manifest.rs` and one key lookup per analysis in `sakura_analysis/src/lib.rs`) and spec fixtures (`d2d1ad53`). Decision: (1) **The exit commit is `dee11301`.** The gate's parity requirement (§17.166, plan §3.8) is met on it: eight green cells on the commit itself. (2) **Item 3's `rust` and `ci-required` green is taken from the first `main` commit after `dee11301` whose CI run is green in every job**, its URL recorded beside the parity URL (Astra), on the ruling that a fix confined to test modules, test fixtures, the eval harness, workflows and release tooling changes no compiled product, so the product the gate signs is still `dee11301`'s; a Windows fix that has to touch non-test code is a new row and an owner question before the gate is signed. (3) **Item 2 is closed** on `98854ac0`..`a35e1d7d`: row 29 changed no documented behaviour, so a recheck against the exit commit is a recheck against those pages; drafting the `0.1.0` CHANGELOG section is release-notes work and moves to stage B (Astra, with the notes). (4) **The window benches `dee11301` exactly**, checked out detached in its own worktree, never `main`'s head: the blessed baseline's `meta.commit` is then the commit the gate signs and `v0.1.0`'s baseline (§17.228 (2)), `bless` sees a clean tree, and later commits do not move the target. None of the commits after it reopens the window: row 30 by §17.233; the Windows fixes and the release wiring compile no product; the inventory is prose and a test; `V8005` is one `BTreeMap` lookup on the manifest per analysis, on `bench compile`'s path in principle and below its resolution (budgets of 500–3,000 ms p95, targets of 10 and 45 ms); its number is read at the 0.2.0 window, and whoever wants one now runs an advisory `bench compile` pair on the loaded host, which is not a window. (5) **The procedure** for the window and the bless, for an owner who is not a compiler person, is `docs/release.md` §2 (new; the later sections renumber 3–7): the worktree, the toolchain check, the build, the four runs (`--suite js`, `--suite wasm`, `--suite native --lane matched`, `compile`) under `record_load.py`, `envelope`, the two `bless` commands and the files to commit, one command per line; checklist item 11 points to it. (6) **Order after round 26**, ranked: **1** Windows `rust` green and item 3's URLs (Sol, Astra; now); **2** the window (the owner; Astra reports after it); **3** WS-P13b's conditions, then its push (Opus; after 2); **4** `V8006` and the `[targets]` removal (Opus; §17.239; after 2, before `v0.2.0`); **5** the inventory rows per §17.239 (Astra; now); **6** N2 (Opus; in flight: `c12054ee`, N2b, N2c) and **7** N1b with §17.229 (5) (Opus); **8** the JSON baseline and check (Opus, then Sol); **9** deprecations and the MSRV patch check (Sol; now); **10** row 31 (Opus; M5, after 2, beside N2); **11** `compat` and the ABI fixtures (after `v0.1.0` tags); **12** WS-W3's attribution, then WS-W4. Why: the gate is parity on one commit and that commit has it; a test-only fix cannot change what parity measured; a baseline whose commit differs from the signed one is a record nobody can reproduce. Rejected: benching `main`'s head (moves with every docs commit and differs from the signed commit); re-running parity on the fix commit as the gate (the product did not change); a second window for `V8005`. Performance: none. Changed: plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.8 (rows 29 and 30 done, row 31, the state line, the gate's commit), `performance-r1.md` §9 (order after round 26), `road-to-1.0.md` stage 2, `docs/release.md` §2, the checklist, `m5-native.md` §3. Impact: the owner (the window), Astra, Sol, Opus.

239. **Inventory and `V8005` readings: `sakura/holeContext` is stable from 0.2.0 by reference to the `hole` record; a code action's `data` promises `impact` and `applicability` and nothing else; the `[targets]` key goes and is refused as unknown; a manifest that is not a valid manifest is `V8006 manifest_invalid` from `check`; `V8005`'s refusal is stable from 0.2.0; 0.2.0 is confirmed as the first honouring version for the CLI, configuration and MCP contracts (`20984cf1`, `61887ae8`..`cd627ab8`; §17.225 (6), (7); §17.232 (a), (f); round 26).** Facts: `sakura_lsp` answers `sakura/holeContext` with `TextDocumentPositionParams` and returns the `hole` record (`sakura_diagnostics/src/json.rs`, `kind: "hole"`, schema 1.0, what the MCP `hole_context` tool returns) of the hole at the position, or `null`; the VS Code extension's "Show Hole Context" calls it, and the extension reads a code action's `data` (`{impact, applicability}`, copied from the fix record, `lib.rs` `code_actions`) to title the action; `manifest.rs` declares `targets: BTreeMap<String, toml::Value>`, which nothing reads and no template, example, fixture or page writes; `Manifest::parse` is `toml::from_str` under `deny_unknown_fields` and its error reaches the user only from `build`, `run`, `test` and `verify` as a usage error (`sakura.toml: <serde text>`, exit 2, human stderr under `--json`), while `check` reports no manifest problem at all, so the `V8005_dependencies_table_unknown_key` fixture (`[bogus]` beside `[dependencies]`) shows one record where §17.232 (a) asked for two; STABILITY.md marks `V8005`'s refusal `stable` from 0.2.0. Decision: (a) **`sakura/holeContext` is `stable` from 0.2.0** as a method whose params are the LSP's `TextDocumentPositionParams` and whose result is the `hole` record of the JSON contract (versioned by its `kind` and `schema_version`, §17.232 (b)) or `null`; there is no separate schema to decide. **A code action's `data` is `stable` from 0.2.0 as exactly two fields**, `impact` and `applicability`, with the values of the JSON `diagnostic` contract's fix record; any other key is unpromised; the server's diagnostics carry no Sakura extension beyond the standard fields. (b) **`[targets]` goes**: Opus deletes the field, so the table is an unknown key under (c); §9 reads "reserved for a later minor"; the STABILITY row goes with it (a parsed, unread key is not a surface and "experimental" means something runs). (c) **`V8006 manifest_invalid`**, class PACKAGE, title "sakura.toml is not a valid manifest": `check`, `build`, `run`, `test`, `verify` and the LSP report every failure of `Manifest::parse` as this diagnostic, message the parser's text (an unknown key, a missing `[package] name`, a wrong type, malformed TOML), primary span the parser's span in `sakura.toml` (the key, the value or the syntax error), `--json` as any `V`, exit 1; the usage-error path for a manifest goes; `check` reports it from the place `V8005` is reported, after `V8005` when both apply, so the unknown-key fixture gains its second record and §17.232 (a) reads as written. Fixtures: an unknown table, an unknown key under `[package]`, a wrong type, malformed TOML, a missing `name`. One code, not one per failure kind: the identity is "the manifest is invalid"; the message carries the reason and the span the place. Owner Opus, small, one commit with (b), after the window (it edits `sakura_analysis`, which `bench compile` measures), before `v0.2.0`; `codes.rs`, `codes.txt`, §12.3's row, the website code page. (d) **`V8005`'s row is confirmed**: `stable` from 0.2.0; the refusal is the promise §17.225 (7) made for 1.0 and a 0.2.0 compiler already keeps it. (e) **0.2.0 is confirmed** as the first honouring version for the CLI, `sakura.toml`, the `--json` kinds and MCP, under RFC 0001's boundary (within a 0.x minor line until 1.0, within a major from 1.0; §17.232); the two "pending" rows read as (a) and (b) now; every other draft level stands until the owner accepts the RFC at the tag. Why: a method whose answer is a record a stable tool already returns needs no second schema; a key nothing reads is a promise about nothing; an agent's loop runs `check --json`, and a manifest error that only `build` reports, on stderr, with exit 2, is one the loop cannot see. Rejected: a code per manifest failure kind; keeping `[targets]` as experimental (unimplemented is not experimental); making `data` internal (the first-party extension reads it across versions, so it is a contract). Performance: none. Changed: STABILITY.md (three rows; Astra), §9 (one phrase), §12.3 (`V8006` lands with its code), plan `road-to-1.0.md` stage 5 (`V8006` and `[targets]` beside `V8005`). Impact: Opus, Astra.

240. **Row 31 as built is ratified (nesting grows by ⌈jumps/256⌉, not one level); the JS backend's join-chain stack limit at about 32,000 guarded rows is a stated limit, not a row before 1.0; the native backend's superlinear compile of one wide function is N2f's first measurement; WS-P13b, row 31 and `V8006` landing on `main` before the window is confirmed, since the window benches `dee11301` in its own worktree (`9a37edbe`, `2d808283`..`0530f328`; §17.236, §17.238 (4); round 27).** Facts: `9a37edbe` emits, past 256 jumps, one stub `block` per 256 jumps around the cases it serves, each stub's only exit one `br` to the loop, so the header has at most ⌈n/256⌉ + 1 predecessors; a stub must enclose its jumps, so nesting grows by ⌈n/256⌉ where §17.236 (2) guessed one level; functions of at most 256 jumps are byte-identical (semantics 288/288, ui 144/144, bench 44/44 modules identical) and engine compile time falls (8,192 rows on Node `--no-liftoff` 4.5 → 0.9 s; Wasmtime 32,768 rows 48 → 12 s). `deep_ir.rs` runs 16,384 rows on the JS backend and notes that at 32,768 the JS join chain exhausts the stack (`D090`); the JS backend has no dispatch loop (§17.236). The native backend compiles 16,384 rows in about 30 s and 32,768 in about 115 s, so the curve is superlinear in one function's size. §17.234 (2) and §17.235 (4) placed WS-P13b "after the window" because a perf commit on `main` would move the benched commit; §17.238 (4) then pinned the window to `dee11301` checked out detached in a clean worktree, so `main`'s head no longer moves the target; the owner's first window attempt failed for load (agents and daemons), not for commits. Decision: (1) **Ratified as built.** The nesting rule (§17.200 (1)) bounds nesting by a constant per level and a stub level per 256 jumps is a bounded, mechanical growth; the bound that matters is the header's fan-in, and the byte-identity of every function of at most 256 jumps is the acceptance §17.236 (3) asked for. §17.236 (2)'s "one level" was a proposal marked for the implementer to confirm, and the implementer corrected it. (2) **The JS limit is stated, not fixed before 1.0**: the 0.1.0 notes (Astra, beside §17.236 (4)'s WASM sentence) say that on the JS backend a single function of more than about 32,000 guarded `match` rows overflows the engine's stack at run time; split the match. Why not a row: it is twice the WASM cliff the row fixed, no corpus, bench or dogfood program is within a hundredfold of it, and a fix (a trampoline for the join chain past a count) edits `sakura_codegen_js` on every benchmarked path for a shape no agent writes; it becomes a row the day the `exec` differential target or a dogfood program reaches it, M5b otherwise. (3) **Native compile curve → N2f.** N2f's compile report (plan `m5-native.md`, left of N2) records lowering, Cranelift codegen, object and link time per phase at 4,096, 8,192, 16,384 and 32,768 rows beside the per-program p95; if the superlinear phase is ours (lowering or the block table) it is a small fix inside N2f; if it is Cranelift's register allocation over one function, the limit is stated in the native page and splitting one LIR function into several native functions is M5b. `--target native` is experimental, so no compile budget is claimed for it (§17.175 (4)). (4) **Landing before the window is confirmed**: "after the window" in §17.234 (2) and §17.235 (4) meant "not inside the commit the window benches", and §17.238 (4) satisfies that by construction; what a window needs from the repository is nothing, and what it needs from the machine is silence (`record_load.py`'s non-quiet abort). Rule from here: a perf commit lands when its conditions are met; the window benches the pinned commit; the next window reads the commit. WS-P13b, row 31 and `V8006` are read at the 0.2.0 window as §17.235 (4), §17.236 and §17.239 (c) already say. Why: the gate is parity and the envelope on one commit, and the commit is pinned. Rejected: holding perf commits in worktrees until a night the owner can run (idle code rots and the WS-P13b series already needed one rebase); a JS fan-in row before 1.0; a native compile budget. Performance: none at M4; the 0.2.0 window reads the three. Changed: plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.8 row 31 (done), `m5-native.md` (N2f's measurement, §3 row), `performance-r1.md` §9 (order after round 27), the 0.1.0 notes (Astra). Impact: Opus (N2f), Astra (notes).

241. **N1b's four readings: `begin`, `release` and `rollback` become worker requests so the runtime thread never blocks behind a statement; an SQLite `INTEGER` beyond ±(2^53 − 1) is a decode `Err` on the JS hosts and exact natively, a documented JS-only limit beside `D003`; one statement per query is the contract on every host, the decode message follows the JS text, and a `__proto__` key is kept; the decode depth counts value nodes, never refinement layers (`66599e50`, `fa88057a`; plan `m5-native.md` N1b; §17.171, §17.213 (1), §17.229 (5); round 27).** Facts: on the native host each database is a `rusqlite` connection on its own worker thread; `query` and `execute` are requests the frame resumes on (`sql.rs` `start`/`completion`), but `sql_begin` and `sql_end` call the worker and wait on `rx.recv()` on the runtime thread (`sakura_host_native/src/sql.rs`), so a `Sql.transaction` entered while another task's long statement runs on the same database stalls every timer and cancel until that statement ends; §17.171 (3) promises that a cancel interrupts a running statement. `node:sqlite` returns an `INTEGER` column as a JS number, so `fromSql` accepts only a safe integer (`Number.isSafeInteger`, `sakura_runtime.js`) and answers a decode `Err` otherwise, where `rusqlite` reads an `i64` exactly; §4.1 makes an `Int` beyond ±(2^53 − 1) `D003` on the JS backend. `rusqlite::prepare` refuses a text with a second statement (`select 1; select 2`) and `node:sqlite` compiles the first and ignores the rest without exposing the tail; the malformed-JSON-cell message differs between the hosts because the cell path is outside the shared codec; the JS `fromJs` builds a `Map` through a plain object, so a key `"__proto__"` sets the prototype and the entry is lost; the native codec counts a refinement wrapper (`kind_of` on a `w` descriptor) and `Option` as levels against `vt::MAX_DEPTH` 64, the runtime's value-tree bound (`vt::MAX_DEPTH`) that `D090` already enforces at the WASM boundary, and the JS runtime applies no depth bound to `Json.decode` at all. Decision: (1) **Requests.** `begin`, `release` and `rollback` are queued requests like `query` and `execute`: the frame parks at `ANSWERED` and resumes on the worker's completion; the worker is serial per database, so `SAVEPOINT`, the body's statements and `RELEASE` keep their order; the finalizer's rollback (`rt_code_sql_rollback`) is a request whose completion nobody waits for beyond `close`, which joins the worker (a `ROLLBACK TO` nobody observes may finish after the task). Acceptance: a host test with two tasks on one `Sql.memory()`, one running a slow statement (a recursive CTE) under `timeout`, the other entering `Sql.transaction`: the timeout fires on time and the statement is interrupted; `sql_cancel_committed` unchanged. Owner Opus, inside N1b's remaining work. (2) **Kept, and documented.** The JS hosts answer a decode `Err` (`column <name>: expected an integer`; data from outside is the `Err` channel, and a column value is data), native decodes exactly; the backend page's `D003` paragraph gains one sentence (an SQLite `INTEGER` or a JSON integer beyond ±(2^53 − 1) is a decode `Err` on `js` and `wasm` and exact on `native`), parity fixtures print no such value, and N2's exact-i64 lane is where native's exactness is tested. A silent rounding (what `node:sqlite` does without `readBigInts`) is the one thing refused, and `fromSql`'s check already refuses it. (3) **Align to the stricter or the correct host, per item.** (a) *One statement per query*: a query text with more than one statement is `Err("one statement per query")` on every host; the stricter host is the contract when the laxer one drops the program's input silently. On Node the host scans the text for a statement terminator outside string, quoted-identifier and comment lexemes (SQL's four lexeme kinds; forty lines), since `node:sqlite` exposes no tail; the text is the program's own `sql"…"` literal, so the scan is over trusted text. Acceptance: `sql_rows` gains the two-statement case, `Err` on all three backends. (b) *Malformed JSON cell*: the JS text is the contract (parity compares stdout, and the JS text is what every fixture already prints); native adopts it through `json::sql_rows`. (c) *`__proto__`*: a JS bug; the `Map` is built from a null-prototype object or directly into the runtime's map, the entry is kept, and `json_codec` gains the key. Owner Opus, N1b. (4) **Depth counts value nodes.** The decode limit is the value tree's: each record, variant (so `Option` too), list, map and tuple is one level, a refinement is a predicate on a node and adds none; `kind_of` stops counting `w` layers; 64 stays (it is the value tree's bound and raising it is an ABI MINOR for M5b if a program needs it). The JS runtime enforces the same bound in `Json.decode` as a decode `Err` (`nested deeper than 64 levels`), so the three backends agree and a deep document is an `Err` rather than a stack fault on `js`. Fixture: a 64-deep and a 65-deep list in `json_codec`. Why: a runtime that blocks its own scheduler cannot keep §17.171's cancel promise; parity compares stdout and an `Err` a program prints is stdout; a limit three backends share is a contract and one they do not is a parity bug. Rejected: a blocking `begin` as "simpler" (it is the only hosted call that blocks); `BigInt` rows on Node (`Int` is a JS number everywhere else); making native drop trailing statements like Node; counting refinements as depth (a `where` adds no nesting the program can see). Performance: none on JS's hot paths (`fromSql` already checks the integer; the SQL scan is per query text, which `sql"…"` interpolation builds per call: `sql_rows`-sized texts scan in microseconds, read at the 0.2.0 window's `--suite js` if any bench program queries); native: one more request per transaction. Changed: ABI §8.6 (Sql: one statement, the integer sentence, the depth sentence; Opus with the code), the backend page (Astra), plan `m5-native.md` N1b. Impact: Opus (N1b), Astra.

242. **The JSON contract after its first baseline: one `kind` per shape (`summary` for `check`/`fix`/`build`, `test_summary`, `audit_summary`, `verify_summary`), rewritten once before `v0.2.0` while the history is not yet enforced; `manifest` starts at 1.1; usage errors stay stderr text with exit 2; required → optional fails the history check; the `defect` record, the regression file and the MCP results join the contract; `check_history.py` reads the tree from the root (`bcfe2166`, `dd086062`, `313e8c47`; §17.232 (b), §17.239 (e); round 27).** Facts: `compiler/contracts/json.txt` lists `summary 1.0` with sixteen fields of which only `schema_version` and `kind` are required, because `check`, `fix` and `build` (snapshot, errors, warnings, notes, total, emitted, truncated, holes), `test` (passed, failed, skipped, total, seed) and `audit` (sites, by_site) emit records of one `kind` and different shapes; `verify`'s summary is `summary 1.1` with its own fourteen required fields, which `check_json.py` accepts only because 1.1 keeps every 1.0 field as optional, so "1.1" names a different record, not an additive step. `manifest.json` carried `schema_version` 1.1 before the baseline and gained `kind` with it. A CLI usage error under `--json` is clap's or ours on stderr, exit 2 (`EXIT_USAGE`), and §17.239 (c) already moved the one content-caused case (`sakura.toml`) to `V8006`. `check_json.py` fails removed lines, type changes and optional → required, and passes required → optional. The `defect` record on stderr (`schema_version` 1.0, `kind: "defect"`, code, message, spans) is §12.1's exit-70 contract and is not in the baseline; `tests/regressions/<fn>.json` is a file the test runner writes and replays whose records carry no `kind`; the MCP `symbol_query` result is built in `sakura_mcp/src/lib.rs` beside `sakura doc --json`'s record and lacks the `kind` `bcfe2166` gave the CLI's. The `contracts` CI job that runs `check_json.py` is Sol's owed row and is not wired. `compiler/diagnostics/check_history.py` runs `git ls-tree --name-only <base> -- <path>` without `--full-tree`, so from a subdirectory the path resolves relative to the cwd, nothing is found and the script prints that the history starts here and exits 0; `check_json.py` passes `--full-tree`. Decision: (1) **One `kind` per shape.** `summary` keeps `check`, `fix` and `build` (its eight fields required at 1.0); `test`'s closing record is `test_summary` 1.0 (passed, failed, skipped, total, seed, required); `audit`'s is `audit_summary` 1.0 (sites, by_site, required); `verify`'s is `verify_summary` 1.0 (today's fourteen fields, required; a new kind starts at 1.0 by `check_json.py`'s own rule, and §17.185's "every verify record is 1.1" was about the diagnostic record, whose 1.1 is additive over 1.0 and stays). The baseline is rewritten once in the same commit; `json_contracts.rs` pins each command's closing record to its kind; every in-repo consumer that matches `"kind":"summary"` (the `sakura test` path in `main.rs`, `offline.py`, the eval harness, the VS Code extension, `bench/harness`) is updated in that commit, found by grep. The rewrite is legal because §17.239 (e) makes 0.2.0 the first honouring version and the history check is not yet in CI: **the split lands before Sol wires the `contracts` job, and from that job's first run the baseline is append-only** (RFC 0001's gate). CHANGELOG under Changed names the three renamed kinds. (2) **`manifest` 1.1 is right.** A kind's history starts at the version its first baselined record carries; `schema_version` 1.1 was already on disk, and `kind` is the record's identity, not a field change. (3) **Usage errors stay text on stderr with exit 2.** A usage error is a wrong invocation, the convention every CLI and clap itself keep, and `cli.txt` lists exit 2 as the stable contract; anything caused by the program or the manifest is a diagnostic (`V8006`'s precedent), so the one remaining content-caused usage error, a source file that is not UTF-8, becomes a diagnostic in §17.243 (3). (4) **Required → optional fails.** Within a major a record's change is additive and nothing else: a consumer of 1.0 that reads a required field must find it in 1.1; `check_json.py` refuses required → optional at every step, as it refuses the reverse; `test_json.py` gains the case (Opus, with (1)). (5) **Scope.** (a) The `defect` record joins the baseline as `defect 1.0` (schema_version, kind, code, message required; spans optional; its `error` field is not emitted), checked by `json_contracts.rs` from a program that defects; (b) the regression file's records are the runner's `regression` records and gain `kind` and `schema_version` at the file level (`regression 1.0`), so a replayed file is checked by the same test; (c) the MCP tool results are the JSON contract's records by construction: `symbol_query`, `hole_context` and every tool that returns a CLI record returns the CLI's bytes, `kind` included, and `sakura_mcp` builds them through the same function as the CLI; STABILITY's MCP row reads "the tool results are the `--json` records of the same name". (6) **`check_history.py` gains `--full-tree`** and a test that runs it from `compiler/` (Sol, one line and one test, now). Why: a versioned schema is a promise about a shape, and one `kind` for three shapes promises nothing; additivity is one-directional; a record an agent parses at exit 70 is a contract whether it comes on stdout or stderr; a check that passes from the wrong directory is a check nobody can trust. Rejected: a `command` field under one `summary` kind (the baseline cannot say which fields each command requires); a JSON usage record (clap's errors cannot be made JSON, so the contract would be half-kept); allowing required → optional at a MINOR; leaving the regression file out (the runner reads it back, so its shape is a compatibility surface across versions). Performance: none. Changed: `compiler/contracts/json.txt` and `README.md`, `check_json.py`, `check_history.py`, STABILITY.md's MCP row and the `--json` kinds row (Astra), plan `road-to-1.0.md` stage 5. Impact: Opus (the split, the `defect` and `regression` kinds, `fromSql`'s consumers), Sol (`check_history.py`, then the `contracts` job after the split), Astra.

243. **Lexical and host decisions the spec fixtures raised: an `Int` literal token is in `[0, 2^63 − 1]` and §2.2 says so; a digit separator is one `_` between two digits of one digit run (`V0006` otherwise); a source file that is not UTF-8 is `V0014 source_not_utf8`, not a usage error; the Node WASM host reads the ABI version from the module's custom section before instantiation so `V8003` precedes any `LinkError`; `sakura test` relays the runner's contract exits unchanged (`docs/spec/gaps.md`; `8cf2a6ed`, `17d2bc09`..`d2d1ad53`; §17.199 (4), §17.203 item 6; round 27).** Facts: §2.2 says a literal outside `[−2^63, 2^63 − 1]` is `V0003` and that `−5` lexes as `−` then `5`, so `−9223372036854775808` is `V0003` on its magnitude and the sentence contradicts the lexer; the `numeric_boundaries` snapshot shows the lexer accepting `1_`, `1__000`, `0x_1`, `1_.5` and `1.5_e3` where §2.2 says "between digits"; a non-UTF-8 file is refused by the CLI with exit 2 before lexing (`invalid_utf8_source_is_a_usage_error`) and `sakura_syntax::lex` takes a `&str`; `V8003` is rendered by the Node host after instantiation, so a module of another ABI MAJOR whose imports differ fails with a `LinkError` first (M4-OPT question 4; §17.203 item 6 queued a pre-instantiation check that never landed; ABI 1.2.1 became §17.209's copy-budget erratum instead), while the native driver reads the `sakura.native` record and refuses with `V8003` before running (N2d); the `sakura test` CLI maps a runner status that is neither 0 nor 3 to exit 70 with "the test runner crashed", and the WASM host's `runTests` exits 2 for `V8003`, so a stale module under `sakura test --target wasm` is reported as a crash. Decision: (1) **The token's range is `[0, 2^63 − 1]`** and the value −2^63 is written `Int.min` (`std/int` gains `pub const min: Int` and `max`, `@total` not needed for constants) or `−9223372036854775807 − 1`; `V0003`'s message for exactly `9223372036854775808` suggests `Int.min`. §2.2 is reworded (this round). Why: negation is an operator in every position (there is no unary plus either), a lexer that folds `−` into the literal makes `− 5` and `−5` different tokens, and Rust, Gleam and Swift all take the same position. (2) **One `_` between two digits of one run**: not first, not last, not doubled, not beside `.`, `e`, a sign or a radix prefix; `V0006 malformed_number` with the message naming the place ("a digit separator goes between two digits"), `fmt` never sees one (the file does not parse). Python's rule; an agent reading `1_000.5` must not wonder whether `1_.5` is a number. The snapshot flips the five cases to errors (Opus, `sakura_syntax/src/lexer.rs`, small). (3) **`V0014 source_not_utf8`**, class LEX, "the source file is not UTF-8", primary span the first invalid byte's offset in a span of that file (byte offsets are what spans carry), message naming the offset and the byte, emitted by every command that loads sources and by the LSP for a document it reads from disk; the file's remaining diagnostics are suppressed as a broken module (§17.207's cascade rule by place), other files are checked; `check --json` carries it like any `V`, exit 1. `sakura_syntax::lex` keeps `&str`: the check is in `sakura_analysis`'s loader, where the bytes are. Fixture in `tests/ui` (a directory fixture with one Latin-1 byte). Why: an agent's loop runs `check --json` and a refusal it cannot see as a record is §17.239 (c)'s problem again. (4) **The Node host reads the version first**: the WASM module carries its ABI version in the `sakura.debug` custom section's header (§17.199 (4)); the host parses the custom section from the bytes before `WebAssembly.instantiate` (a forty-line scan of the section list, no compile), compares MAJOR and MINOR as §0 says and reports `V8003` with exit 2 before any import is resolved, so a `LinkError` can only mean a host bug and is reported as one (`D090`'s wording, "the host could not link the module"); the `wasm_host.test.js` case gains a module whose imports differ. ABI.md §0 gains the sentence as an erratum of the reading order, no version bump (a host that reads a byte earlier changes no contract). Owner Opus, small, before `v0.2.0`, with the native driver's check as the model. (5) **`sakura test` relays 0, 2, 3 and 70**: the runner's exit codes are §12.1's, the CLI passes them through unchanged (2 after the runner's own stderr, which already names the cause), and only a status outside that set, a signal or a timeout is "the test runner crashed" or the time-limit message with exit 70 and 3 as today. Why: a runner that says `V8003` and a CLI that says "crashed" are two reports of one fact; the first is the right one. Rejected: folding the sign into the literal (two tokens for one spelling); Rust's lax separators (they exist to let `1_` be typed mid-edit, which an agent never does); a usage error with a code but no span; reading the version after instantiation with a `LinkError` translation (a `LinkError` message is V8's text, not ours). Performance: (4) adds one scan of the custom-section list per module load, under 0.1 ms on `hello`'s module, read at the 0.2.0 window's `--suite wasm`; the rest none. Changed: §2.2 (this round), §12.3 (`V0014` lands with its code), ABI §0 (erratum), `docs/spec/gaps.md` (three decisions closed by reference), `codes.rs`, `codes.txt`. Impact: Opus (five small items, one commit each or batched), Astra (spec and code page).

244. **The Intel macOS lane: `parity` on `macos-15-intel` stays required with the time scale; the `rust` partitions on it leave `ci-required` for a nightly schedule; no further diagnosis is owed before `v0.2.0` (`6eeb9db4`, `9aadc756`, `da6e5413`; §17.227 (2); round 27).** Facts: `6eeb9db4` measured the `macos-15-intel` image building and running about 2.5× slower than `macos-latest` (test build 6 m 41 s against 2 m 41 s, both x86_64-native in the dev profile) and made the `sakura test` run limit `30 s × SAKURA_TEST_TIME_SCALE`, set to 3 on that lane in the `rust` and `parity` jobs, with `parity` single-threaded there; the 20× reading (`runner_offline` 135 s against 6 s) was the agent-DX harness test, which spawns dozens of processes and is since `#[cfg(unix)]` and bounded; `ci-required` needs `rust` on every matrix child; `macos-15-intel` is the C platform's acceptance cell for the Intel archive (`road-to-1.0.md` §6 P6, P8) and §17.227 (2) already states the fallback when GitHub retires the runner. Decision: (1) **`parity (macos-15-intel)` stays in `ci-required`**: it is the cell that says the Intel archive runs programs the same way, which P8's acceptance needs and nothing else provides. (2) **`rust (macos-15-intel, 1/2, 2/2)` leave `ci-required`** and run on the nightly schedule (and on `workflow_dispatch`), red mailing the owner as the fuzz nightly does: the compiler's unit and integration tests are covered on x86_64 by Ubuntu and on macOS by `macos-latest`, and the Intel-specific surface (the archive, the solver assets, the formula's `on_intel` block) is what `parity`, the release dry run and `verify-published.yml` test. Half an hour of a slow runner on every push, twice, for no new cell is what agents wait on. (3) **No diagnosis is owed.** A runner class that is 2.5× slower is a fact `docs/ci-timing` (`fa1f5b8c`) can record, not a defect; `TIME_SCALE` 3 is the right mechanism (a limit scaled per lane, never a global one) and stays. Owner Sol, `ci.yml`, one commit, now. Why: CI is on the agents' critical path every day and a required job must buy a cell no other job has. Rejected: dropping the lane (P8 needs the cell); a global `TIME_LIMIT` increase (hides slow tests on every lane); investigating the 20× (its test is gone from that lane). Performance: none on the product; CI wall time on a push falls by the Intel `rust` partitions' duration. Changed: `ci.yml` (Sol), plan `road-to-1.0.md` §6 P6 (acceptance wording: `parity` and the dry run). Impact: Sol.

245. **Shortening the path to 1.0: the calendar floors, not agent-days, set the date; ranked, with the native question answered (owner's question, 2026-10-19; §17.225, §17.226, §17.228; round 27).** Facts: `road-to-1.0.md` counts about 96 agent-days over about seven weeks with five agents, so agent-days are not the binding constraint; the binding items are calendar floors: three owner quiet windows (`v0.1.0`, `v0.2.0`, the rc; each one night of an idle machine, §17.228 (2), §17.238 (5)), seven consecutive clean nightly fuzz runs ending at the rc tag (§17.225 (4)), and a seven-day rc soak (§17.225 (4), §17.226 H), which alone is at least fourteen nights after the `exec` differential target exists. State at `313e8c47`: M4 exit commit pinned, the window pending the owner's night; P0–P6 landed, P4's workflow green, P7's install page in flight; RFC 0001 drafted with `V8005`, `V8006`, the MSRV job, the code and JSON baselines landed and the `contracts`, `compat`, deprecations and ABI-fixture rows open; the spec's grammar and diagnostics drafted, semantics prose not started; native: N1a, N1b's JSON, CLI and Sql, N2a–N2e landed (179 of 179 corpus programs, both twins and `--no-direct`, clean under `--native-checks`), left: N1b's Http, serve, Llm, secrets, log, test-runner codes and `V8004` (about 3 d), N2's three sanitizer controls with the ASan and Memcheck lanes and `crabgrind` (about 3 d), N2f (about 2 d), N5 (3 d), the §17.173 lanes (2 d): about 13 agent-days on Opus lines (N0b landed as `86262eff`..`4ee9e954` while this round was written, §17.247), and §17.226 made N1a → N2 the critical path; no `exec` fuzz target exists (`compiler/fuzz/fuzz_targets` has six), no `dogfood/` directory, no `judge` mode, and `fuzz.yml` has no campaign input. Decision, ranked by days saved on the critical path: **(1) Start the soundness clock now.** The `exec` differential target (Opus, 5 d) is the first Opus slot after the window's conditions are met, ahead of every native row; `fuzz.yml`'s `max_total_time` input and corpus artifacts (Sol, 0.5 d) land this week and the nightly runs every night from then, so the seven-night streak and the 24 CPU-hours per target accumulate while everything else lands; the streak that counts is the one ending at the rc tag, so starting early costs nothing and waiting costs a day per day. **(2) Native leaves the `v0.2.0` gate and the 1.0 gate, and stays in the tree behind the `native` feature.** Decision, under the owner's standing choice to confirm: `--target native` ships in release binaries as an experimental flag in the first minor whose native matrix `{js, wasm, native} × {ubuntu, macos}` and §17.173 lanes are green, whether that is 0.2.0, 0.3.0 or 1.1.0; no tag waits for it; one Opus line keeps landing N1b's remainder, N2's controls and N2f at the lowest priority of the queue, and N5 is M5b with N3 and N4 (N0b is landed, §17.247). Evidence for: 15 agent-days leave the 0.2.0 gate and three Opus lines become two for `exec`, the JSON split and the dogfood programs; §17.225 (8) and STABILITY already label native experimental and outside every guarantee, so no promise moves; native brings no performance claim at 1.0 because §17.177's numbers wait for windows after N3; the sanitizer lanes, which are what make an experimental backend safe to ship, are the part not yet built; `ABI` 1.3.0 and 1.4.0 are MINORs and additive whenever they land. Evidence against: N2 is five slices in and 179 of 179 programs agree, so the remaining work is small and the sunk cost argues for finishing; the landing page says native is experimental (true either way: experimental means "may be absent or change"); `research/12` and §17.173 set a bar that an unshipped backend never meets in public; a release binary without the flag means the dogfood corpus never runs natively before 1.0, which it would not have as evidence anyway (parity is the gate, dogfood runs `js` and `wasm`). Net: the gate is removed, the work is not; the critical path stops running through it. Saved: the `v0.2.0` date no longer depends on native, about one to two weeks on §17.226's D. **(3) Dogfood starts now against the published `v0.1.0-alpha.1`** (npm and brew; the header records the release built against), and acceptance is unchanged: all six pass against the installed `v0.1.0` binary when it exists and upgrade to 0.2.0 from the notes; the alpha.1 → 0.1.0 differences are in CHANGELOG already and are the first migration the corpus exercises. Saved: the days between now and the `v0.1.0` tag for six programs (nine agent-days that would otherwise wait). **(4) The windows run the night each release's last commit lands**, and nothing else is scheduled for those nights: `v0.1.0`'s now (pending), `v0.2.0`'s the night RFC 0001's rows and the spec's grammar land, the rc's the night the streak reaches seven; the owner's role per window is one command before sleep and one `bless` after (`docs/release.md` §2). **(5) Collapse the RFC rows into one Sol series this week**: `contracts` job (after §17.242 (1)), `check_history.py --full-tree`, `deprecations.toml` and `release.py prepare`'s checks, with `compat` and the ABI fixtures the day `v0.1.0` tags; none is big and all are on 0.2.0's list. **(6) Spec semantics prose is parallel, not a path item**: Astra's 12 d run beside everything until the rc; the one scope rule is §17.225 (9)'s (a sentence without a fixture is informative), so the rc ships whatever is cited by then and nothing waits. **Not cut**: the seven-night streak, the seven-day soak, the three windows, the envelope held at 0.2.0 and the rc, the agent-DX bar, the RFC at 0.2.0, the spec in 1.0: each is the evidence §17.225 substituted for outside users and cutting one weakens the promise. **The shortest honest schedule**: `v0.1.0` within days of the window; `exec` landed in week 1 and the nightly campaign running; `v0.2.0` in week 2 to 3 (RFC 0001, `V8006`, WS-P13b, row 31, the JSON split, C platforms, the spec's grammar and diagnostics; native if green, else not); rc at the first tag after the seventh clean night with the corpus upgraded and the eval numbers (week 3 to 4); `v1.0.0` seven days later (week 4 to 5). About two to three weeks less than §17.226, all of it from the calendar floors starting earlier and from no tag waiting on native. Why: a plan whose longest chain is agent work shortens with agents, and a plan whose longest chain is nights shortens only by starting the nights sooner. Rejected: cutting the soak or the streak (the evidence); a 1.0 that keeps native as a gate (the one item the owner can drop that moves a date); a second paid run (no budget); skipping the 0.2.0 window (three perf commits land in it). Performance: none. Changed: plan `road-to-1.0.md` (stage 4, stage 7 (a), the timeline, the critical-path paragraph), `m5-native.md` (the gate sentence, N0b and N5 to M5b, the priority), `performance-r1.md` §9. Impact: the owner (confirm (2); the window nights), Opus (`exec` first), Sol (`fuzz.yml` input, the RFC series), Astra (dogfood programs, notes), Codex agents (three dogfood programs).

246. **Order after round 27 (`git log origin/main` at `313e8c47`; round 27).** State since §17.238: landed on `main` row 31 (`9a37edbe`), WS-P13b (`2d808283`..`0530f328`), the exhaustiveness index and its tuple-column follow-up (`25a8430e`, `78d8456b`, `1401ba2a`), N1b's errno table and sleep chain (`bc61e0c5`), JSON codec and `Cli.parse` (`fa88057a`), Sql (`66599e50`), N2e (`f0f30ad9`), the JSON contract baseline and history check (`bcfe2166`, `dd086062`, `313e8c47`), the Intel lane scale (`6eeb9db4`), Windows `rust` green (`7f377624`, `5d2bae6c`), the README and headline (`c391bce4`, `926c1674`, `b8efd6ab`), CI scoping (`579f6e06`). Readings folded in: the tagline is "The programming language built for agents." (owner, 2026-10-19; §17.47 trailer); the documented npm command is the plain `npm install --global sakuralang` and the workflows keep `--ignore-scripts` as a guard (§17.227 trailer); STABILITY.md's native row still says `--target native` is refused by clap, which N2c made false (Astra, with the inventory). Queue items before this round's that were still listed: lines 2–37 of the designer's queue are answered in §17.180–§17.239 (dx5 in §17.182–§17.188, WS-P2b in §17.183, V4b in §17.185, P0/P1 in §17.196, WS-C1 in §17.205–§17.207, suppression in §17.221, row 16 in §17.219–§17.220, WS-P13a in §17.217, N1a in §17.229, RFC 0001 in §17.232, WS-P13b in §17.235, the exit record in §17.238); the exhaustiveness follow-up is landed (`1401ba2a`); the spec bugs are landed (`dc90f0c5`, `9dcddecf`). Ranked: **1** the window (the owner; one night; Astra reports); **2** `exec` differential target (Opus; now, beside 1; `compiler/fuzz`, CI only) and `fuzz.yml`'s campaign input (Sol; now); **3** the JSON kind split and `defect`/`regression` kinds (§17.242 (1), (4), (5); Opus; now; `sakura_cli`, `contracts/`, `sakura_mcp`, consumers) then `check_history.py` and the `contracts` job (Sol; after 3's split); **4** dogfood programs against alpha.1 (three Claude agents, three Codex agents; now; `dogfood/`); **5** the Intel `rust` schedule (§17.244; Sol; now); **6** §17.243's five small items (Opus; now; `sakura_syntax`, `sakura_analysis`, `sakura_wasm_host.js`, `main.rs`, `std/int.sak`); **7** `V8006` and `[targets]` (§17.239 (b), (c); Opus; after 1 by its own rule, before `v0.2.0`); **8** N1b's remainder with §17.241 (Opus; one line, lowest priority); **9** N2's controls and lanes, N2f with §17.240 (3) (Opus; same line, after 8); **10** `deprecations.toml` and `release.py prepare` checks (Sol); **11** `compat` and the ABI fixtures (Sol, Opus; after `v0.1.0` tags); **12** the 0.1.0 notes with §17.236 (4) and §17.240 (2)'s sentences, the `0.1.0` CHANGELOG section (Astra; stage B); **13** the spec's semantics prose (Astra; continuous); **14** the inventory rows and the STABILITY fixes above (Astra); **15** WS-W3's attribution then WS-W4 (M5b unless idle). Collisions: 3 and 6 both touch `sakura_cli/src/main.rs` (3 the `summary` match, 6 the exit relay), 3 first; 6's host change and 2 are disjoint; 8 and 9 are one line. Owner: the window and bless, the gate signature, confirm §17.245 (2), the `v0.1.0` tag and approvals. Why: the two items that move the 1.0 date (the nights and the native gate) lead; everything that is small and unblocks a stage follows; native keeps one line and blocks nothing. Rejected: launching N5 (M5b); a new window for `V8006`. Performance: WS-P13b, row 31 and `V8006` are read at the 0.2.0 window. Changed: plans as §17.240–§17.245 name. Impact: everyone as named.

247. **N0b's five readings: the candidate set `E` keeps §17.176 (2)'s facts until N4a, which widens it to every managed type and re-records the baseline; miss reasons wait for N3 and never live in `stats()`; recomputing `opt::owned` over the snapshot read back as Core meets §17.176 (2); a remove's untested path copies are neither attempts nor misses until N3 adds the test; the allocation-bound WASM cells must not move at the 0.2.0 window beyond noise (`86262eff`..`4ee9e954`, ABI 1.3.0, `sakura_abi_version` `0x010400`; §17.176, §17.229 (1); REQUESTS 2026-10-19 N0b; round 27).** Facts: `stats()` appends `reuse_attempts`, `reuses`, `borrow_candidates` and `borrows` after `restarts` (120 bytes; the eleven 1.2 counters keep their offsets); every uniqueness test of an owned `List`/`Map`/`Set` update goes through `Rt::reusable`, counted per wrapper and per node on the path; a `Dup` is marked `DupCandidate` when the reference stays live after a call to a read-only builtin (`opt::owned::BORROWS`) or a direct def whose summary borrows the parameter, and `rt_dup_candidate` counts it at run time; the summary tracks collections, carriers and functions, so `String.length(s)` with `s` live after is a `Dup`/`Drop` pair not counted; `sakura_core_snapshot::convert::back` reads a snapshot back as Core, tested as `convert`'s exact inverse on 243 programs, and `sakura_lir` runs `sakura_core::opt::owned` itself over it, the JS and LIR `--dump owned` agreeing on every corpus program; `Map.remove`/`Set.remove` copy their HAMT index path with no uniqueness test (ABI §10 says so); the LIR print snapshots change only `dup` → `dup.candidate`, hand-audited fixtures reconcile each counter, `bench_counters.txt` holds the baseline with the 1.2 counters unchanged, and no WASM code path changed beyond the counter increments; `4ee9e954` fixes `no_wasi`'s reading of a trapped import after N1a moved it. Decision: (1) **`E` stays as built at N0b**: the counter's definition is "executed `Dup`s the LIR marked" and the marking is what the facts the LIR has can justify; a LIR-level "never consumes" fact for every managed type is N4a's first commit (the builtin table already says which builtins only read; a direct def's "never consumes" for scalars-in-boxes, strings and records falls out of the same summary once it tracks them), landed as a marking change before the convention changes, with `bench_counters.txt` re-recorded in that commit as the baseline §17.176 (4)'s bar is read against; ABI §10's sentence gains "the set of marked sites may grow in a later version; a benchmark record's `abi_version` names it". M5b. (2) **Miss reasons are N3's** and, when built, are a separate debug-only export (`stats_reasons`, or `SAKURA_WASM_STATS`'s JSON line gaining a `reasons` object in a debug build) and never fields of `stats()`, whose layout is the same in every build; §17.176 (1)'s "miss reasons in a debug build only" is read that way. Two of the four reasons exist only with tokens, so a reason set now would be half a vocabulary. (3) **Confirmed, and preferred.** §17.176 (2) asked for recomputation from the committed snapshot with one implementation and a parity dump; reading the snapshot back as Core and running the pass is one implementation where `direct.rs` is two, the snapshot stays fact-free (§17.149 (2)), and `back`'s exact-inverse test over the corpus is the condition that keeps it honest and stays in CI. §13.2's "facts in the LIR" sentence is read as "recomputed by the Core pass over the snapshot read back". (4) **Not counted now.** An attempt is an executed uniqueness test (§17.176 (1)); a copy nobody tested is neither, and a miss invented from a path length would make `reuse_rate` depend on which operations happened to be instrumented. N3's in-place delete adds the test, and those copies become attempts and hits or misses there; `bench/deltas.md`'s row for `map_set` and any remove-heavy program names that as the reason attempts rise at N3's first window. ABI §10's sentence stands as written. (5) **Confirmed.** No emitted WASM instruction changed beyond the counter calls, so at the first window after N0b, which is the 0.2.0 window, the allocation-bound set (`nbody`, `sieve`, `spectral_norm`, `rbtree`, `binary_trees`, `deltablue`) reads within noise of the `v0.1.0` baseline on the WASM lane, and the counters' record is `bench_counters.txt`; a move beyond noise is reported with its cause named (the candidates are WS-P13b's facts reaching the LIR through (3), or row 31's stubs), never blessed as N0b's, and `deltablue`'s WASM cell is read under §17.235 (4) in any case. Why: a counter's value is only evidence if its definition does not move under it; one implementation of a fact is a parity test for free; a window reads what changed and names what moved. Rejected: a wider `E` now (a baseline the N4 bar would be read against would then be N0b's accident); reasons in `stats()` (layout per build); counting remove copies as misses (not tests). Performance: none beyond §17.176's four increments. Changed: ABI §10 (one sentence on the marked set; Opus with N4a), plan `m5-native.md` (N0b done, N4a's first commit, N3's remove test), §17.245 (N0b is landed, not M5b). Impact: Opus (N3, N4a, M5b), Astra (`bench/deltas.md` at the 0.2.0 window).

248. **Windows x64 and Intel macOS ship in `v0.1.0`: the release matrix is `packaging/targets.json`, one matrix per tag, no row withheld; every row's claim level for the version is read from `verify-published.yml`, so C's exit moves from `v0.2.0` to the first tag whose published smoke is green on the C rows (changelog agent's question 1; §17.227 (3), (5); §17.180; round 28).** Facts: `targets.json` has five rows and `release.yml`'s plan step emits the whole file as the build and smoke matrix; the last dry run on `main` (`39199739466`, at `e20d941c`) built and install-smoked all five rows green, Windows and Intel included; `verify-published.yml` already carries a `windows-2025` row (`install.ps1` by URL, npm) and a `macos-15-intel` row (installer, npm, brew); the Darwin signing job signs both inventory binaries; `release.py accept` runs the P4 list plus the MCP and LSP `initialize` round trips, and the `core.autocrlf=true` `fmt --check` case of §17.227 (1) is not in it; the install page says Windows and Intel Mac are "coming in v0.2.0", written before P5 and P6 landed; plan `road-to-1.0.md` P8 names `v0.2.0` as "the first release carrying the C archives", an estimate from round 24, while §17.227 (5) defines a stage's exit as the first published-install smoke green on every row of the first release that carries its archives, whichever tag that is. Decision: (1) **Ship them.** The matrix a tag builds is the inventory file at that commit; withholding rows would need a filter that does not exist, written on tag day, to keep back archives whose dry run is green, and would make the inventory and the release disagree. (2) **Claim level by the same rule as every other row**: at the tag no row is supported for 0.1.0; after `verify-published.yml` on `0.1.0` a green row is **supported** for 0.1.0 and a red row is not, stated per row on the install page with the run URL; the notes' sentence ("called supported for this version only after the published-install smoke passes") already says this and stays. A red C row on 0.1.0 is not a P1 release defect (it was never supported) and does not touch the published assets: the page says "published for 0.1.0; its published-install check failed on <row> (<run>); fixed forward", and C's exit waits for the next tag. A red A or B row stays a P1 as §17.227 (3) says. (3) **C's exit is the first tag with both C rows green**, `v0.1.0` if tomorrow's run is; P8 reads so. (4) **The missing `autocrlf` case** of §17.227 (1) is owed on the Windows row (Sol, after the tag, before `v0.2.0`): until it is in, the Windows "supported" line says what was run (installer, npm, the P4 list, MCP, LSP) and not more; the proof-cache limitation (P7) stays stated. (5) **The install page and the `0.1.0` CHANGELOG line** ("explicit Windows/Intel Mac v0.2.0 availability") are rewritten before the tag to "published for 0.1.0; supported once its published-install check is green" (Astra, docs only), and after the run to the result. Why: the evidence that makes a platform supported is the run on the public bytes, which exists only after a tag; delaying the archives to 0.2.0 buys no evidence and keeps two runners' worth of green work in a drawer. Rejected: a per-tag matrix override (code nobody needs); shipping the C archives unlisted (an asset nobody is told about is still an asset); calling C "expected to work" at the tag (that level is for OSes with no hosted runner, §17.227 (3)). Performance: none. Changed: plan `road-to-1.0.md` P8 and timeline row C (this round), install page (Astra), CHANGELOG line (Astra). Impact: Astra (page and changelog line before the tag; per-row result after the run), Sol (the `autocrlf` case; the run and its URL), the owner (nothing new: the same `verify-published.yml` dispatch at close-out).

249. **The 0.1.0 notes and flags: "a pre-1.0 preview" with the no-promise sentence pointing at RFC 0001 and STABILITY.md, never "pre-alpha"; the GitHub pre-release flag and the npm dist-tag follow the version string, so `v0.1.0` is a release marked latest and published to npm `latest`, and only a version with a pre-release identifier (`-alpha.N`, `-rc.N`) is a GitHub prerelease under that identifier's dist-tag (changelog agent's question 2; §17.228 (3); round 28).** Facts: `release.py release_notes` says "a pre-alpha release… Nothing beyond the versioned diagnostic schema and the fixture formats is stable"; `release.yml` runs `gh release create --prerelease --latest=false` unconditionally and `npm publish --tag alpha` with a comment "never the `latest` dist-tag"; §17.228 (3) decided `latest` moves to `v0.1.0` when it publishes; `install.sh` takes `--version` and never resolves `releases/latest`; the CHANGELOG's own header calls 0.1.0 "the first non-alpha preview" with "no stability promise yet… if the owner accepts [RFC 0001], its promises begin at `v0.2.0`"; SemVer defines a pre-release as a version with a hyphenated identifier, and `0.1.0` has none. Decision: (1) **Wording.** The template's first paragraph reads: "Sakura Lang {ver}, a pre-1.0 preview release from commit {commit}. There is no stability promise yet: RFC 0001 proposes the guarantees and, if the owner accepts it, they begin at v0.2.0; STABILITY.md lists what each contract covers today." The rest of the template stands (targets with the supported-only-after sentence, signing, Node 26, Z3, install, the CHANGELOG link). The same two sentences are the rule for every 0.x tag until the RFC is accepted, after which they say which contracts are stable from which version. (2) **Flags from the version string** (`release.py` already parses the pre-release identifier for its version checks): a version without an identifier is created with `--latest=true` and no `--prerelease`, and its npm packages publish under `latest`; a version with one is `--prerelease --latest=false` and publishes under the identifier's first word as the dist-tag (`alpha`, `rc`). `--verify-tag` and the environment approvals stay; the job name "Publish GitHub prerelease" becomes "Publish GitHub release". Owner Sol (`release.yml`, `release.py`, one commit, and `docs/release.md` §6's two sentences in the same commit), before the tag: it is release tooling, compiles no product, and the tag run is the first that exercises it, so the dry run on `main` after it must be green on every row first. Why: the pre-release flag and the dist-tag are statements about the version string, which SemVer already defines, and the readiness statement belongs in the notes and STABILITY.md where it can say exactly what is promised; a 0.x line whose every tag is flagged pre-release makes the flag say nothing, and a `/releases/latest` that resolves to nothing while 0.1.0 exists is a worse answer than 0.1.0. Rejected: flagging every 0.x tag pre-release until 1.0 (the flag stops meaning anything); keeping npm on `alpha` for 0.1.0 (contradicts §17.228 (3) and the install page's plain command); "beta" (the project has no beta stage, §17.225). Performance: none. Changed: `docs/release.md` §6 (Sol, with the code), the notes template. Impact: Sol.

250. **What `v0.1.0` points at: the candidate SHA on `main`, after the preparation commit, with one fully green `ci.yml` run on that exact head; never the exit commit. The window benches the milestone's exit commit and the tag ships the product at tag time, and the two are different commits by design. Between the preparation commit and the tag push nothing lands on `main`. `V8006`, the `[targets]` removal and every post-exit commit ship in 0.1.0 (changelog agent's questions 3 and 4; §17.228 (2), §17.238 (2), (4), §17.240 (4), `docs/release.md` §3 and §5; round 28).** Facts: `main` is 70 commits past `dee11301` at writing: row 30 (security), the Windows test fixes, Intel and Windows packaging, `V8005`, `V8006` with the `[targets]` removal, WS-P13b, row 31, the exhaustiveness index, N0b's counters, trace batching, N1b and N2 native work behind the feature, the JSON baseline and contracts job, the docs and the `0.1.0` CHANGELOG; §17.240 (4) already ruled that a perf commit lands when its conditions are met, that the window benches the pinned commit and that the next window reads the commit; `release.py prepare` writes the version into the workspace in a preparation commit and `release.yml` refuses a tag whose version differs from the workspace's, so a tag can only point at or after that commit; `docs/release.md` §5 says the tag must peel to the approved SHA; CI runs on `main` cancelled each other all afternoon, so no fully green run on the newest head has been read since the Windows fixes (handoff §8). Decision: (1) **The tag points at the candidate SHA**: `main`'s head after the preparation commit, the SHA `release.py prepare` recorded and the owner approves against the dry run's inventory. For every baseline release the rule is the same: the window benches the milestone's exit commit in its own worktree (§17.238 (4)), the blessed baseline carries that commit, and the tag points at the candidate SHA on `main`. (2) **The exit commit is not a tag candidate**: it lacks row 30, the Windows fixes, the C packaging, `V8005`, the release tooling fixes and the changelog, and its workspace version is the alpha's. (3) **Freeze from the preparation commit to the tag push**: nothing lands on `main` in between, so the green `ci.yml` run the tag needs (one run, every job, on that head; the nightly or a `gh workflow run ci.yml --ref main` counts) is on the candidate SHA and is not cancelled by a later push; the freeze is hours, and agents queue their pushes in their worktrees. (4) **`V8006` and `[targets]` ship in 0.1.0**, Breaking as the changelog says: §17.239's "before `v0.2.0`" was a deadline and §17.246's "after the window" was superseded by §17.240 (4); a manifest that is not valid is reported by `check` as a diagnostic from 0.1.0 on, which is what the alpha.1 → 0.1.0 migration section is for. Nothing on `main` is pulled back for the tag. (5) **What 0.1.0 claims about performance**: nothing numeric (the changelog says so); the landed-not-accepted commits (WS-P13b, row 31, `V8006`, the index) are read at the 0.2.0 window per §17.153 (4), and the baseline at `dee11301` is the base `bench compare` reads until then. Why: a release is the product at a point in time and a milestone gate is a verdict about one commit; the two coincide only by accident, and §17.238 (4) chose a pinned commit for the window precisely so that `main` could keep moving; a tag that waits for every push to settle never lands, and a freeze of hours is the cheapest way to get one green run on one SHA. Rejected: tagging `dee11301` (the alpha's version, no security row, no C archives); a release branch (one branch on the remote is the owner's standing rule); re-running the window on the candidate (the window is the gate's evidence, not the release's, and the owner has one night). Performance: none. Changed: `m4-exit-checklist.md`'s closing paragraph (this round). Impact: the owner (approve the candidate SHA; push the tag after one green run), every agent (hold pushes during the freeze), Sol (the preparation commit's checks).

251. **Deterministic counters are reportable anywhere: the WS-W1/W2 frame counts go back into the `0.1.0` changelog; the "no unblessed benchmark numbers" rule covers times, ratios, peak RSS and any other host-dependent measurement, not counts the emitted code fixes (changelog agent's question 5; §17.153, §17.191 (2), §17.199, §17.247; round 28).** Facts: the changelog agent dropped the `handled_*` (1,005 → 4 frames per `run(1000)`) and the callback-loop counts (1,121, 2,508, 5,010, 2,005 and 7,005 → 4) as a precaution; those numbers come from the module's `stats()` counters, recorded in `bench_counters.txt`, identical on every host and every run, and §17.199 (4) already lets a frames counter settle `countdown`'s bar; the same section keeps `restarts`, `reuse_attempts` and the "406 MB to 5.4 MB" file size, which are deterministic too; the rule's reason (§17.153) is that load on the shared machine moves timings, and a count of frames does not move under load. Decision: counts the compiler or the runtime fix deterministically (frames, restarts, reuse attempts and hits, candidates, module and file sizes, instruction and node counts, fixture tallies) may be quoted in the changelog, the notes, the landing page and the docs at any time, with the program, size and backend named; timings, Sakura/hand-JS ratios, peak RSS, compile latencies and anything else `bench run` samples stay out until a blessed window reads them, except in design entries and plans, which already mark advisory readings as advisory. The two WS-W1/W2 bullets return as written (Astra, with (248)'s changelog edit). Why: the rule protects claims from noise, and a counter has none. Rejected: keeping counts out to be safe (a true, repeatable fact withheld makes the changelog less useful to the agent reading it); a per-number approval (the rule is enough). Performance: none. Changed: nothing beyond the changelog. Impact: Astra.

252. **The reference machine stays the owner's M4 Pro for every window to 1.0; no cloud or VM window; nothing is cut from the window's protocol for `v0.2.0` or the rc without a noise study of the `--stable` configuration that does not exist; the one change made now is that a window resumes at the first suite that was not quiet instead of starting over, because each suite's file carries its own quiet verdict (owner's question through the changelog agent, question 6; §17.109, §17.110, §17.153, §17.175 (7), §17.238 (4); round 28).** Facts: the window is four runs, about 4–4.5 hours: `--suite js` and `--suite wasm` with `--stable` (2 s warm-up, a ≥ 30 ms batch, nine samples, five fresh processes per cell), `--suite native --lane matched` (the Rust, Go, OCaml and Koka controls under pinned toolchains) and `compile`; the warm-up is most of each cell's wall time; the baselines are keyed by host id (`bench/baselines/<host-id>.json`, `d88970189598`) and §17.109's ceilings were set on this host; the noise study in `bench/README.md` characterised the PR configuration (250 ms warm-up) and says it "does not establish the noise of the stable nightly configuration"; `record_load.py` reads the host's load average and its busiest executables, which on a shared-tenant VM cannot see steal time; §17.153 rejected a paid host and §17.175 (7) forbids emulated numbers; the owner's first window attempt failed for load, not for length; the procedure says "on `not quiet` keep the file, stop, and start the window again later", without saying whether a suite already completed quiet must be rerun. Decision: (1) **The reference machine stays** for `v0.1.0`, `v0.2.0` and the rc: a new host is a new host id with a baseline history of zero and ceilings that were never read on it, a shared VM's noise is invisible to the quiet verdict, and the window costs the owner a night the machine is idle, not hours of their day (§17.245 (4)). A faster window on another machine would be a different measurement, not the same one sooner. (2) **No cut without evidence.** `--processes` stays 5 (the RSS statistic and the confirmation groups are defined on five), the warm-up stays 2 s, every size runs, and no program is skipped as "unchanged": the window measures a commit's whole output against hand JS on one night, every cell shares that night's host state, and the result file records no per-program output hash from which "unchanged" could even be read. The evidence that would let a cut be decided is a noise study of the stable configuration on the reference host: two runs of one unchanged build in one quiet night at the current settings and one at the candidate settings (1 s warm-up, three processes), per-cell bands compared as the weekly `noise` job does; if the bands agree, §17.153's protocol can name the cheaper settings. That night is the owner's to offer, and it is not before the rc: a shorter window saves part of a night the owner sleeps through anyway, and a wrong number costs a release. (3) **Resumable window, now.** A suite's run is its own file with its own `meta.host.quiet`, blessed on its own; a window that aborts on a non-quiet run keeps every completed quiet run and resumes at the suite that was not quiet, the same night or the next, on the same commit in the same worktree; the pairing `envelope` reads (wasm file, native file) is already a pairing of two runs hours apart, so two nights are the same kind of pairing. `docs/release.md` §2's sentence reads "keep the file, stop, and resume at that run later; completed quiet runs stand" (Astra, one sentence; `run-window.sh` in the handoff brief gains a skip of a suite whose file is quiet, Sol, optional). Why: the quiet verdict is the thing that makes a run evidence, and it is per run; what the protocol forbids is rerunning a cell inside a run to get a better number, not keeping a run that was clean. Rejected: a cloud reference host (a different measurement, and a paid one); carrying the native controls forward from the previous window (their toolchains are pinned but the night is not); `--sizes quick` in a window (direction only). Performance: none; the window's length is unchanged until the study exists. Changed: `docs/release.md` §2 (one sentence, Astra). Impact: Astra, Sol (optional), the owner (a night for the study, if ever, after the rc).

253. **Three contract readings for the docs agent: `source_not_utf8` is `V0015` (`V0014` is `named_argument` and codes are never reused); `symbol_query 1.0` and `manifest 1.1` are stable from 0.2.0 as marked; `[permissions] process` is reserved: `process = []` is accepted and means what the absent key means, any other value is `V8006` until an RFC adds a `Process` effect (docs agent's questions 7–9; §17.232, §17.239 (c), §17.242 (2), (5c), §17.243 (3); round 28).** Facts: `codes.txt` has `V0014 active named_argument` and its header says every assignment is kept forever; §17.243 (3) assigned `V0014` to `source_not_utf8` without reading the file, and `docs/spec/diagnostic-codes.md`, `grammar.md`, `gaps.md` and the errors index copied the number; the next free `V00xx` is `V0015`; `symbol_query 1.0` and `manifest 1.1` are rows of `compiler/contracts/json.txt`, §17.242 (2) ruled `manifest` 1.1 right and (5c) made the MCP results the CLI's records by construction; `manifest.rs` declares `permissions.process: Vec<String>` ("Programs `Process` may start"), the startup denial maps an effect named `Process` to the `process` key, the design's §9 example writes `process = []  # no subprocesses`, and no `effect Process` exists in std or anywhere in the tree. Decision: (1) **`V0015 source_not_utf8`**; §17.243 (3) is read with `V0015`; Opus allocates it in `codes.rs` and `codes.txt` with the fixture, and Astra corrects the four documents in the same commit or the next. (2) **Confirmed**: `symbol_query 1.0` and `manifest 1.1` are stable from 0.2.0; the JSON split (§17.242 (1)) renames three summary kinds and touches neither. (3) **`process` is reserved**: the key stays in the manifest grammar, `process = []` parses and grants nothing (the same as leaving it out), and a non-empty list is `V8006` with the message "`process` grants nothing in this version: no std effect starts processes; the key is reserved"; STABILITY.md's `sakura.toml` rows gain the line (reserved; `[]` only; 0.2.0); a `Process` effect, when wanted, is an RFC that turns the list into a grant. Opus, with the `V8006` fixtures, before `v0.2.0`, not needed for 0.1.0. Why: a grant the runtime cannot honour is a lie an agent reading the manifest would believe; a key that nothing reads but whose empty form appears in the design's own example is cheaper to reserve than to refuse. Rejected: reusing `V0014` (the file's one rule); refusing the `process` key outright (invalidates the §9 example and the fixture for no gain); accepting a non-empty list silently (today's behaviour, and the lie). Performance: none. Changed: §17.243 (3) read with `V0015`; STABILITY.md (Astra); the four spec and docs files (Astra). Impact: Opus (`V0015`, `process` under `V8006`), Astra.

254. **Diagnostic and generated text is not a contract, so wording fixes land whenever they pass the gates; the four named ones land tonight in one Opus commit if CI is green on it before the preparation commit, else in the next version; user-facing text never names a milestone, a round, a plan or a version other than the compiler's own, and the `D096` text drops the environment name. Order after round 28 (docs agent's question 10; RFC 0001 "human wording… may improve", STABILITY.md's wording sentence; round 28).** Facts: `sakura_core/src/lower/mod.rs` says "`[permissions]` arrive in M3" in `V9002`'s text; `manifest.rs` builds `V8005`'s message from `CARGO_PKG_VERSION` as "Sakura {version}" (the brand rule is "Sakura Lang", never "Sakura" alone; the version is the compiler's own, not hard-coded); `D096`'s text in `codes.rs` names `SAKURA_MAX_HEAP`, which the JS and WASM hosts do not read (the module's memory maximum is fixed at build) and only the experimental native driver reads from the environment, so STABILITY.md's "this checkout does not read them" is right for the shipped backends and wrong for native; `compiler/examples/cookbook.md` says "pre-alpha APIs can change" and feeds the skill through `agent_docs`, whose mirror, ZIP and manifest must agree (`agent_docs --check` is a release gate, `docs/release.md` §3); RFC 0001 and STABILITY.md both say human wording and diagnostic prose are not contracts; the codes' identity and JSON fields are. Decision: (1) **Not a contract change.** A message's text may change in any version; what may not change is the code's condition, its `kind` and fields, and its class. (2) **Land tonight if green**: one Opus commit, `V9002` rewritten to what §6.2 and §9 say today (which effects the driver installs root handlers for and that `[permissions]` grants them), `V8005` as "Sakura Lang does not support packages yet: `[dependencies]` is reserved and refused; packages arrive in a later minor" (the RFC's MUST, no version), `D096` as "the WASM runtime's heap limit (ABI §13) was reached or memory could not grow", the cookbook's sentence as "pre-1.0: read STABILITY.md before depending on an API", the website error pages and the skill mirror regenerated, fixtures and snapshots updated and reviewed; the commit precedes the preparation commit only if its `ci.yml` run is green before the owner is ready to prepare; otherwise it waits for the freeze to lift and ships in 0.1.1 or 0.2.0. The tag never waits for a wording fix. (3) **The rule**: user-facing text (diagnostics, help, generated pages, the skill, templates) names no milestone, round, plan, agent or release other than the compiler's own version; a test asserting a message checks the sentence, so a stale sentence is a failing snapshot, not a contract event. (4) **STABILITY.md's runtime-limits row** reads "the JS and WASM hosts do not read these from the environment; the experimental native driver reads `SAKURA_MAX_HEAP`" (Astra). **Order after round 28**, ranked: **1** the window tonight on `dee11301` (the owner; `docs/release.md` §2; resumable per §17.252 (3)); **2** the release-flag change (Sol; §17.249 (2); `release.yml`, `release.py`, `docs/release.md` §6; dry run green on every row) and the docs edits before the tag (Astra; §17.248 (5), §17.251, §17.253 (1)'s documents, §17.254 (4)); **3** the wording commit (Opus; §17.254 (2)) if green in time; **4** bless, the gate signature, `release.py prepare`, the freeze, one green `ci.yml` run on the candidate, the tag, the three approvals, `verify-published.yml` on `0.1.0` (the owner, Sol runs the dispatch; §17.250); **5** after the tag: the per-row platform results on the install page (Astra; §17.248 (2)), `compat` and the ABI fixtures (Sol, Opus; §17.245 (5)), the Windows `autocrlf` case (Sol; §17.248 (4)), `V0015` and `process` under `V8006` (Opus; §17.253), then §17.246's list from its item 2 (`exec` first). Why: text that teaches an agent the wrong thing is a bug like any other and is fixed at the speed of the gates; a contract is what a program can parse, and no program parses a sentence. Rejected: holding the wording for 0.2.0 on principle (nothing protects a stale sentence); delaying the tag for it (the one thing that must not wait). Performance: none. Changed: STABILITY.md's limits row (Astra). Impact: Opus, Astra, Sol, the owner as ranked.
